Sakkapañhasuttaṃ

Dīgha Nikāya 8
← Previous: Mahāsamayasuttaṃ Home Next: Mahāsatipaṭṭhānasuttaṃ →
344
<p n="344" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">344</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Evaṃ <pb ed="T" n="2.0298"/><pb ed="M" n="2.0211"/><pb ed="V" n="2.0194"/><pb ed="P" n="2.0264"/> me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā magadhesu viharati, pācīnato rājagahassa ambasaṇḍā nāma brāhmaṇagāmo, tassuttarato vediyake pabbate indasālaguhāyaṃ. Tena kho pana samayena sakkassa devānamindassa ussukkaṃ udapādi bhagavantaṃ dassanāya. Atha kho sakkassa devānamindassa etadahosi – ‘‘kahaṃ nu kho bhagavā etarahi viharati arahaṃ sammāsambuddho’’ti? Addasā kho sakko devānamindo bhagavantaṃ magadhesu viharantaṃ pācīnato rājagahassa ambasaṇḍā nāma brāhmaṇagāmo, tassuttarato vediyake pabbate indasālaguhāyaṃ. Disvāna deve tāvatiṃse āmantesi – ‘‘ayaṃ, mārisā, bhagavā magadhesu viharati, pācīnato rājagahassa ambasaṇḍā nāma brāhmaṇagāmo, tassuttarato vediyake pabbate indasālaguhāyaṃ. Yadi pana, mārisā, mayaṃ taṃ bhagavantaṃ dassanāya upasaṅkameyyāma arahantaṃ sammāsambuddha’’nti? ‘‘Evaṃ bhaddantavā’’ti kho devā tāvatiṃsā sakkassa devānamindassa paccassosuṃ.</p>
Thus have I heard. Once the Blessed One was staying in Magadha, to the east of Rājagaha, near a Brahmin village called Ambasanda, to the north of that village, on Mount Vediya, in the Indasāla Cave.

At that time, Sakka, the lord of the gods, felt a strong desire to see the Blessed One.

Then Sakka, the lord of the gods, considered: "Where is the Blessed One, the worthy and perfectly enlightened one, staying now?" And Sakka, the lord of the gods, saw the Blessed One staying in Magadha, to the east of Rājagaha, near a Brahmin village called Ambasanda, to the north of that village, on Mount Vediya, in the Indasāla Cave.

Perceiving this, he addressed the gods of the Tāvatiṃsa heaven: "Gentlemen, the Blessed One is staying in Magadha, to the east of Rājagaha, near a Brahmin village called Ambasanda, to the north of that village, on Mount Vediya, in the Indasāla Cave.

Suppose, gentlemen, we were to visit that Blessed One, the worthy and perfectly enlightened one?" "So be it, good sir," the gods of the Tāvatiṃsa heaven replied to Sakka, the lord of the gods.
<p n="344" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">344</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Evaṃ</hi><pb ed="P" n="3.0697"/><pb ed="V" n="2.0260"/><pb ed="M" n="2.0290"/><hi rend="bold">me suta</hi>nti sakkapañhasuttaṃ. Tatrāyamanuttānapadavaṇṇanā – <hi rend="bold">ambasaṇḍā nāma brāhmaṇagāmo</hi>ti so kira gāmo ambasaṇḍānaṃ avidūre niviṭṭho, tasmā ‘‘ambasaṇḍā’’ tveva vuccati. <hi rend="bold">Vediyake pabbate</hi>ti so kira pabbato pabbatapāde jātena maṇivedikāsadisena nīlavanasaṇḍena samantā parikkhitto, tasmā ‘vediyakapabbato’ tveva saṅkhyaṃ gato. <hi rend="bold">Indasālaguhāya</hi>nti pubbepi sā dvinnaṃ pabbatānaṃ antare guhā, indasālarukkho cassā dvāre, tasmā ‘indasālaguhā’ti saṅkhyaṃ gatā. Atha naṃ kuṭṭehi parikkhipitvā dvāravātapānāni yojetvā supariniṭṭhitasudhākammamālākammalatākammavicittaṃ leṇaṃ katvā bhagavato adaṃsu. Purimavohāravasena pana ‘‘indasālaguhā’’ tveva naṃ sañjānanti. Taṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ ‘indasālaguhāya’nti.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Ussukkaṃ udapādī</hi>ti dhammiko ussāho uppajji. Nanu ca esa abhiṇhadassāvī bhagavato, na so devatāsannipāto nāma atthi, yatthāyaṃ na āgatapubbo, sakkena sadiso appamādavihārī devaputto nāma natthi. Atha kasmā buddhadassanaṃ anāgatapubbassa viya assa ussāho udapādīti? Maraṇabhayena santajjitattā. Tasmiṃ kirassa samaye āyu parikkhīṇo, so pañca pubbanimittāni disvā ‘‘parikkhīṇo <pb ed="P" n="3.0698"/> dāni me āyū’’ti aññāsi. Yesañca devaputtānaṃ maraṇanimittāni āvi bhavanti, tesu ye parittakena puññakammena devaloke nibbattā, te ‘‘kuhiṃ nu kho idāni nibbattissāmā’’ti bhayaṃ santāsaṃ āpajjanti. Ye katabhīruttānā bahuṃ puññaṃ katvā nibbattā, te attanā dinnadānaṃ rakkhitasīlaṃ bhāvitabhāvanañca āgamma ‘‘uparidevaloke sampattiṃ anubhavissāmā’’ti na bhāyanti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Ussukkaṃ udapādī</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0698"/> <p rend="bodytext">Sakko <pb ed="V" n="2.0261"/> pana devarājā pubbanimittāni disvā dasayojanasahassaṃ devanagaraṃ, yojanasahassubbedhaṃ vejayantaṃ, tiyojanasatikaṃ sudhammadevasabhaṃ, yojanasatubbedhaṃ pāricchattakaṃ, saṭṭhiyojanikaṃ paṇḍukambalasilaṃ, aḍḍhatiyā nāṭakakoṭiyo dvīsu devalokesu devaparisaṃ, nandanavanaṃ, cittalatāvanaṃ <pb ed="M" n="2.0291"/>, missakavanaṃ, phārusakavananti etaṃ sabbasampattiṃ oloketvā ‘‘nassati vata bho me ayaṃ sampattī’’ti bhayābhibhūto ahosi.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0261"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0291"/> <p rend="bodytext">Tato ‘‘atthi nu kho koci samaṇo vā brāhmaṇo vā lokapitāmaho mahābrahmā vā, yo me hadayanissitaṃ sokasallaṃ samuddharitvā imaṃ sampattiṃ thāvaraṃ kareyyā’’ti olokento kañci adisvā puna addasa ‘‘mādisānaṃ satasahassānampi uppannaṃ sokasallaṃ sammāsambuddho uddharituṃ paṭibalo’’ti. Athevaṃ parivitakkentassa tena kho pana samayena sakkassa devānamindassa ussukkaṃ udapādi bhagavantaṃ dassanāya.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Kahaṃ nu kho bhagavā etarahi viharatī</hi>ti katarasmiṃ janapade kataraṃ nagaraṃ upanissāya kassa paccaye paribhuñjanto kassa amataṃ dhammaṃ desayamāno viharatīti. <hi rend="bold">Addasā kho</hi>ti addakkhi paṭivijjhi. <hi rend="bold">Mārisā</hi>ti piyavacanametaṃ, devatānaṃ pāṭiyekko vohāro. Niddukkhātipi vuttaṃ hoti. Kasmā panesa deve āmantesi? Sahāyatthāya. Pubbe kiresa bhagavati saḷalaghare viharante ekakova dassanāya agamāsi. Satthā ‘‘aparipakkaṃ tāvassa ñāṇaṃ, katipāhaṃ pana atikkamitvā mayi indasālaguhāyaṃ viharante pañca pubbanimittāni disvā maraṇabhayabhīto dvīsu devalokesu devatāhi saddhiṃ upasaṅkamitvā cuddasa pañhe pucchitvā upekkhāpañhavissajjanāvasāne asītiyā devatāsahassehi saddhiṃ sotāpattiphale patiṭṭhahissatī’’ti cintetvā okāsaṃ nākāsi. So ‘‘mama pubbepi <pb ed="P" n="3.0699"/> ekakassa gatattā satthārā okāso na kato, addhā me natthi maggaphalassa upanissayo, ekassa pana upanissaye sati cakkavāḷapariyantāyapi parisāya bhagavā dhammaṃ desetiyeva. Avassaṃ kho pana dvīsu devalokesu kassaci devassa upanissayo bhavissati, taṃ sandhāya satthā dhammaṃ desessati. Taṃ sutvā ahampi attano domanassaṃ vūpasamessāmī’’ti cintetvā sahāyatthāya āmantesi.</p> <hi rend="bold">Kahaṃ nu kho bhagavā etarahi viharatī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Addasā kho</hi> <hi rend="bold">Mārisā</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0699"/> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Evaṃ bhaddaṃ tavāti kho devā tāvatiṃsā</hi>ti evaṃ hotu mahārāja, gacchāma bhagavantaṃ dassanāya, dullabho buddhuppādo, bhaddaṃ tava, yo tvaṃ ‘‘pabbatakīḷaṃ nadīkīḷaṃ gacchāmā’’ti avatvā <pb ed="V" n="2.0262"/> amhe evarūpesu ṭhānesu niyojesīti. <hi rend="bold">Paccassosu</hi>nti tassa vacanaṃ sirasā sampaṭicchiṃsu.</p> <hi rend="bold">Evaṃ bhaddaṃ tavāti kho devā tāvatiṃsā</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0262"/> <hi rend="bold">Paccassosu</hi>
344. Thus.

" *Me sutaṃ*": The Sakkapañha Sutta.

Here follows a word-by-word explanation of that which is not explicit: "*Ambasaṇḍā nāma brāhmaṇagāmo*": That village was situated not far from the mango trees; therefore, it is called "Ambasaṇḍā."

"*Vediyake pabbate*": That mountain was entirely surrounded by a blue forest grove, resembling a jeweled altar, that had grown at the foot of the mountain; therefore, it was named “Vediyaka Mountain.”

"*Indasālaguhāyaṃ*": Formerly, this cave was between two mountains, and an *indasāla* tree was at its entrance; therefore, it became known as “Indasāla Cave.”

Then, having enclosed it with walls, installed door-windows, and made the cave beautiful with well-finished plaster work, garland work, creeper work, and various decorations, they gave it to the Blessed One.

However, according to ancient usage, they recognize it as "Indasāla Cave."

With reference to that, it was said, 'in the Indasāla Cave'.

" *Ussukkaṃ udapādi* ": Religious enthusiasm arose.

Indeed, he was a frequent visitor of the Blessed One. There was no assembly of gods where he had not been before. There was no god-son like Śakka who was diligent in *vihāra*.

Then, why did enthusiasm arise in him, as if the sight of the Buddha was unprecedented for him? Because he was terrified by the fear of death.

At that time, his life was depleted. Having seen five omens, he realized, "Now my life is depleted."

And those godsons for whom signs of death become apparent—of those, those who were born in the world of gods as a result of minor meritorious actions experience fear and terror, [thinking,] "Where shall we be reborn now?"

Those who, having done much merit, were born possessed of the quality of fearlessness, having approached giving, moral discipline, and cultivation, do not fear, [thinking,] “We will experience prosperity in the upper world of the gods.”

Enthusiasm arose.

Then Śakka, the king of the gods, having seen the omens, surveyed the city of gods, ten thousand *yojanas* in extent; Vejayanta, one thousand *yojanas* in height; the Sudhamma assembly hall of the gods, three hundred *yojanas* [in extent]; the Pāricchattaka tree, one hundred *yojanas* in height; the *paṇḍukambala* stone, sixty *yojanas*; one and a half *koṭis* of actors; the company of gods in the two worlds of gods; Nandana Grove; Cittalatā Grove; Missaka Grove; and Phārusaka Grove, all this wealth, [and,] overcome with fear, he thought, "Indeed, this wealth of mine is perishing!"

Then, thinking, "Is there any *śramaṇa* or *brāhmaṇa*, world-father, or great Brahmā who, having extracted the dart of sorrow that is lodged in my heart, would make this wealth permanent?" and, not seeing anyone, he further saw, "The *sammāsambuddha* is capable of extracting the dart of sorrow that has arisen even in hundreds of thousands like me."

While he was thus contemplating, at that very time, enthusiasm arose in Śakka, the lord of the gods, for seeing the Blessed One.

"*Kahaṃ nu kho bhagavā etarahi viharati*": In which district, relying on which city, enjoying whose requisites, teaching whose immortal *dharma*, does the Blessed One dwell at this time?

"*Addasā kho*": He saw, he perceived.

"*Mārisā*": This is an endearing expression, a distinct mode of address among the gods.

It is also said, "free from suffering."

Why did he address the gods? For companionship.

Formerly, when the Blessed One was dwelling in the Salala house, he went alone to see him.

The Teacher thought, "His knowledge is not yet mature. However, after a few days have passed, while I am dwelling in the Indasāla Cave, having seen five omens and being terrified by the fear of death, having approached the gods in the two worlds of gods, having asked fourteen questions, at the end of the explanation of the questions of equanimity, he will be established in the fruit of stream-entry, together with eighty thousand gods," and so he did not give [him] an opportunity.

He [thought:] "Because formerly, when I went alone, the Teacher did not give an opportunity, certainly I do not have the support of the path and the fruit. However, when there is support in one person, the Blessed One teaches the *dharma* even to an assembly as far as the *cakkavāḷa*."

Certainly, however, there will be support from some god in the two worlds of gods. With reference to that, the Teacher will teach the *dharma*.

Having heard that, I too will calm my dejection," thinking, he addressed them for companionship.

"Where, now, is the Blessed One dwelling at this time?" "He saw." "Dear sirs!"

"Thus, good for you!" Thus the gods of the Trāyastriṃśa [said:] "May it be so, great king! Let us go to see the Blessed One! Rare is the arising of a Buddha! Good for you that, not saying, 'Let us go to a mountain sport, a river sport,' you are engaging us in such things!"
"They accepted that statement with a bow," and "Yes, may it be well with you," is what the gods of the Thirty-three said, "accept."
345
<p n="345" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">345</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Atha kho sakko devānamindo pañcasikhaṃ gandhabbadevaputtaṃ <note>gandhabbaputtaṃ (syā.)</note> āmantesi – ‘‘ayaṃ, tāta pañcasikha, bhagavā magadhesu viharati pācīnato rājagahassa ambasaṇḍā nāma brāhmaṇagāmo, tassuttarato vediyake pabbate indasālaguhāyaṃ. Yadi pana <pb ed="T" n="2.0299"/>, tāta pañcasikha, mayaṃ taṃ bhagavantaṃ dassanāya upasaṅkameyyāma arahantaṃ sammāsambuddha’’nti? ‘‘Evaṃ bhaddantavā’’ti kho pañcasikho gandhabbadevaputto sakkassa devānamindassa paṭissutvā beluvapaṇḍuvīṇaṃ ādāya sakkassa devānamindassa anucariyaṃ upāgami.</p>
Then Śakka, the lord of the gods, addressed Pañcasikha, son of a *gandharva* god, saying: “My dear Pañcasikha, the Blessed One is staying in Magadha, to the east of Rajagaha, in the Brahmin village called Ambasanda, to the north of which is Mount Vediya and the Indasala Cave. If we were to approach that Blessed One, the *arhat*, the perfectly enlightened one, to see him?” “Very good, Lord,” said Pañcasikha, son of a *gandharva* god, and agreeing with Śakka, the lord of the gods, taking his yellow beluva-wood lute, he followed in attendance on Śakka, the lord of the gods.
<p n="345" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">345</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Pañcasikhaṃ</hi><pb ed="M" n="2.0292"/><hi rend="bold">gandhabbadevaputtaṃ āmantesī</hi>ti deve tāva āmantetu, imaṃ kasmā visuṃ āmantesi? Okāsakaraṇatthaṃ. Evaṃ kirassa ahosi ‘‘dvīsu devalokesu devatā gahetvā dhurena paharantassa viya satthāraṃ upasaṅkamituṃ na yuttaṃ, ayaṃ pana pañcasikho dasabalassa upaṭṭhāko vallabho icchiticchitakkhaṇe gantvā pañhaṃ pucchitvā dhammaṃ suṇāti, imaṃ purato pesetvā okāsaṃ kāretvā iminā katokāse upasaṅkamitvā pañhaṃ pucchissāmī’’ti okāsakaraṇatthaṃ āmantesi.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Evaṃ bhaddaṃ tavā</hi>ti so ‘‘evaṃ mahārāja, hotu, bhaddaṃ tava, yo tvaṃ maṃ ‘ehi, mārisa, uyyānakīḷādīni vā naṭasamajjādīni vā dassanāya gacchāmā’ti avatvā ‘buddhaṃ passissāma, dhammaṃ sossāmā’ti vadasī’’ti daḷhataraṃ upatthambhento devānamindassa vacanaṃ paṭissutvā anucariyaṃ sahacaraṇaṃ ekato gamanaṃ upāgami.</p> <hi rend="bold">Evaṃ bhaddaṃ tavā</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Tattha <hi rend="bold">beluvapaṇḍu</hi>nti beluvapakkaṃ viya paṇḍuvaṇṇaṃ. Tassa kira sovaṇṇamayaṃ pokkharaṃ, indanīlamayo daṇḍo, rajatamayā tantiyo, pavāḷamayā veṭhakā, vīṇāpattakaṃ gāvutaṃ, tantibandhanaṭṭhānaṃ gāvutaṃ, upari daṇḍako gāvutanti tigāvutappamāṇā vīṇā. Iti so taṃ vīṇaṃ ādāya samapaññāsamucchanā mucchetvā agganakhehi paharitvā madhuraṃ gītassaraṃ nicchāretvā sesadeve sakkassa gamanakālaṃ <pb ed="P" n="3.0700"/> jānāpento ekamantaṃ aṭṭhāsi. Evaṃ tassa gītavāditasaññāya sannipatite devagaṇe <hi rend="bold">atha kho sakko devānamindo…pe… vediyake pabbate paccuṭṭhāsi. </hi></p> <hi rend="bold">beluvapaṇḍu</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0700"/> <hi rend="bold">atha kho sakko devānamindo…pe… vediyake pabbate paccuṭṭhāsi. </hi>
345. *pañcasikhaṃ*

The *gandhabbadevaputtaṃ āmantesī*: He addressed the Gandharva, the son of a god, saying: "He first addresses the gods, why does he address this one separately?" For the purpose of making an opportunity.

Thus it occurred to him: "It is not fitting to approach the Teacher (satthāraṃ) like someone taking deities in both hands and striking with the yoke. But this *pañcasikha*, the attendant of the Ten-Powered One (dasabalassa), is a favorite. Having gone at a moment whenever he wishes, he asks a question, and hears the Dharma. Having sent him ahead, having him make an opportunity, having approached after an opportunity has been made by him, I will ask a question." Thus, he addressed him for the purpose of making an opportunity.

*Evaṃ bhaddaṃ tavā*: Then he said: "Yes, great king, may it be well with you. Because you say, 'Let us go to see the Buddha, let us listen to the Dharma,' rather than saying, 'Come, friend, let us go to see pleasure gardens and festivals.'" Thus, strongly supporting him, having promised the word of Śakra, lord of the gods, he approached accompanying, going together, going in the same direction.

*Evaṃ bhaddaṃ tavā*: There, *beluvapaṇḍu* means yellowish like a ripe *beluva* fruit.

It is said that his lute had a golden sound box (sovaṇṇamayaṃ pokkharaṃ), an indigo handle (indanīlamayo daṇḍo), silver strings (rajatamayā tantiyo), coral frets (pavāḷamayā veṭhakā), a *vīṇāpattakaṃ* of one *gāvuta*, a string fastening place of one *gāvuta*, and an upper rod of one *gāvuta*, thus a lute measuring three *gāvutas*.

Thus, having taken that lute (vīṇaṃ ādāya), having loosened the strings which were skillfully tuned, having struck it with his fingernails, having produced a sweet song, making known to the remaining gods the time for Śakra to go, he stood to one side.

Thus, when the host of gods had assembled at the signal of his singing and playing, then Śakra, lord of the gods…pe… stood up on Mount Vediyaka.

*beluvapaṇḍu*

Then Śakra, lord of the gods…pe… stood up on Mount Vediyaka.
346
<p n="346" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">346</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Atha kho sakko devānamindo devehi tāvatiṃsehi parivuto pañcasikhena gandhabbadevaputtena purakkhato seyyathāpi nāma balavā puriso samiñjitaṃ vā bāhaṃ pasāreyya pasāritaṃ vā bāhaṃ samiñjeyya; evameva devesu tāvatiṃsesu antarahito magadhesu pācīnato rājagahassa ambasaṇḍā nāma brāhmaṇagāmo, tassuttarato vediyake pabbate paccuṭṭhāsi. Tena kho pana <pb ed="V" n="2.0195"/> samayena vediyako pabbato <pb ed="M" n="2.0212"/> atiriva obhāsajāto hoti ambasaṇḍā ca brāhmaṇagāmo yathā taṃ devānaṃ devānubhāvena. Apissudaṃ parito gāmesu manussā evamāhaṃsu – ‘‘ādittassu nāmajja vediyako pabbato jhāyatisu <note>jhāyatassu (syā.), pajjhāyitassu (sī. pī.)</note> nāmajja vediyako pabbato jalatisu <note>jalatassu (syā.), jalitassu (sī. pī.)</note> nāmajja vediyako pabbato kiṃsu nāmajja vediyako pabbato atiriva obhāsajāto ambasaṇḍā ca brāhmaṇagāmo’’ti saṃviggā lomahaṭṭhajātā ahesuṃ.</p>
Then, Śakra, the lord of the gods, surrounded by the Thirty-Three Gods and preceded by Pañcaśikha, the son of a gandharva god, just as quickly as a strong man might stretch out his contracted arm or contract his outstretched arm, vanished from the heaven of the Thirty-Three and appeared in Magadha, to the east of Rājagṛha, in the village of the brahmin Ambasanda, on Mount Vediya to the north of it.

And then, at that time, Mount Vediya was exceedingly radiant, as was the brahmin village of Ambasanda, by the power of the gods.

And indeed, people in the surrounding villages were saying, "Look, Mount Vediya is on fire today! It is blazing! It is burning! What is this? Mount Vediya and Ambasanda are exceedingly radiant!" And they were agitated and their hair stood on end.
<p n="346" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">346</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Atiriva obhāsajāto</hi>ti aññesu divasesu ekasseva devassa vā mārassa vā brahmuno vā obhāsena obhāsajāto hoti, taṃdivasaṃ pana dvīsu devalokesu devatānaṃ obhāsena atiriva obhāsajāto ekapajjoto sahassacandasūriyauggatakālasadiso ahosi. <hi rend="bold">Parito gāmesu manussā</hi>ti samantā gāmesu manussā. Pakatisāyamāsakāleyeva kira gāmamajjhe dārakesu kīḷantesu tattha sakko agamāsi, tasmā manussā passitvā evamāhaṃsu. Nanu ca majjhimayāme devatā bhagavantaṃ upasaṅkamanti, ayaṃ kasmā <pb ed="M" n="2.0293"/> paṭhamayāmassāpi purimabhāgeyeva āgatoti? Maraṇabhayeneva tajjitattā. <hi rend="bold">Kiṃsu nāmā</hi>ti kiṃsu nāma bho etaṃ, ko nu kho ajja mahesakkho devo vā brahmā vā bhagavantaṃ pañhaṃ pucchituṃ dhammaṃ sotuṃ upasaṅkamanto, kathaṃsu <pb ed="V" n="2.0263"/> nāma bho bhagavā pañhaṃ vissajjessati dhammaṃ desessati, lābhā amhākaṃ, yesaṃ no evaṃ devatānaṃ kaṅkhāvinodako satthā avidūre vihāre vasati, ye labhāma thālakabhikkhampi kaṭacchubhikkhampi dātunti saṃviggā lomahaṭṭhajātā uddhaggalomā hutvā dasanakhasamodhānasamujjalaṃ añjaliṃ sirasmiṃ patiṭṭhapetvā namassamānā aṭṭhaṃsu.</p>
346. *Atiriva obhāsajāto* means that on other days a radiant light would arise from the radiance of only one deva, or Māra, or Brahmā. But on that day, a surpassing radiant light arose from the radiance of devas in two heavens; a single flame, like the time when a thousand moons and suns have risen.

*Parito gāmesu manussā* means people around the villages, people all around the villages.

It seems that at the usual evening time, while children were playing in the middle of the village, Śakra approached. Therefore, seeing him, the people said thus:

"Indeed, deities approach the Blessed One in the middle watch of the night, why has this one come in the early part of the first watch? Because he is terrified by the fear of death."

*Kiṃsu nāmā* means "What is this?" Then, "Who now is this mighty deva, or Brahmā, who is approaching to ask the Blessed One a question, to hear the Dharma? How will the Blessed One answer the question, teach the Dharma?" It is a gain for us, for whom the teacher who dispels the doubt of the deities dwells not far from the monastery. We will obtain alms of food in a bowl, alms of food in a spoon to give." Thus, agitated, with hairs standing on end, with uplifted hair, placing their radiant joined hands on their heads, they stood paying homage.
347
<p n="347" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">347</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Atha kho sakko devānamindo pañcasikhaṃ gandhabbadevaputtaṃ āmantesi – ‘‘durupasaṅkamā <pb ed="P" n="2.0265"/> kho, tāta pañcasikha, tathāgatā mādisena, jhāyī jhānaratā, tadantaraṃ <note>tadanantaraṃ (sī. syā. pī. ka.)</note> paṭisallīnā. Yadi pana tvaṃ, tāta pañcasikha, bhagavantaṃ paṭhamaṃ pasādeyyāsi, tayā, tāta, paṭhamaṃ pasāditaṃ pacchā mayaṃ <pb ed="T" n="2.0300"/> taṃ bhagavantaṃ dassanāya upasaṅkameyyāma arahantaṃ sammāsambuddha’’nti. ‘‘Evaṃ bhaddantavā’’ti kho pañcasikho gandhabbadevaputto sakkassa devānamindassa paṭissutvā beluvapaṇḍuvīṇaṃ ādāya yena indasālaguhā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā ‘‘ettāvatā me bhagavā neva atidūre bhavissati nāccāsanne, saddañca me sossatī’’ti ekamantaṃ aṭṭhāsi.</p> <p rend="subhead">Pañcasikhagītagāthā</p>
Then Śakra, the lord of the gods, addressed Pañcaśikha (Pañcasikhaṃ), the Gandharva (gandhabbadevaputtaṃ) son of the gods: "Tathāgatas (tathāgatā) are difficult to approach (durupasaṅkamā) by one like me. They are meditative (jhāyī), delighting in meditation (jhānaratā), and are in retreat (paṭisallīnā) at such times. But if you, dear (tāta) Pañcaśikha, were first to please (pasādeyyāsi) the Blessed One (bhagavantaṃ), then, dear (tāta), after you have pleased him, we (mayaṃ) might afterwards approach (upasaṅkameyyāma) that Blessed One (taṃ bhagavantaṃ), the worthy one (arahantaṃ), the fully enlightened Buddha (sammāsambuddha)."

"Very good, Lord (evaṃ bhaddantavā)," said Pañcaśikha (pañcasikho), the Gandharva (gandhabbadevaputto) son of the gods, and, assenting to Śakra (sakkassa), the lord of the gods (devānamindassa), he took his yellow beluva-wood lute (beluvapaṇḍuvīṇaṃ ādāya) and approached (upasaṅkami) the Indasāla Cave (yena indasālaguhā). Having approached (upasaṅkamitvā), he thought: "From here (ettāvatā) the Blessed One (bhagavā) will be neither too far (neva atidūre bhavissati) nor too near (nāccāsanne), and he will hear (sossati) my voice (saddañca)," and he stood to one side (ekamantaṃ aṭṭhāsi).

The Song of Pañcaśikha (pañcasikhagītagāthā).
<p n="347" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">347</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Durupasaṅkamā</hi>ti dupayirupāsiyā. Ahaṃ sarāgo sadoso samoho, satthā vītarāgo vītadoso vītamoho, tasmā dupayirupāsiyā tathāgatā mādisena. <hi rend="bold">Jhāyī</hi>ti lakkhaṇūpanijjhānena ca ārammaṇūpanijjhānena ca jhāyī. Tasmiññeva jhāne ratāti <hi rend="bold">jhānaratā. Tadantaraṃ paṭisallīnā</hi>ti tadantaraṃ paṭisallīnā sampati paṭisallīnā vā. Tasmā na kevalaṃ jhāyī jhānaratāti durupasaṅkamā, idānimeva paṭisallīnātipi durupasaṅkamā. <hi rend="bold">Pasādeyyāsī</hi>ti ārādheyyāsi, okāsaṃ me kāretvā dadeyyāsīti vadati. <hi rend="bold">Beluvapaṇḍuvīṇaṃ ādāyā</hi>ti nanu pubbeva ādinnāti? Āma <pb ed="P" n="3.0701"/>, ādinnā. Maggagamanavasena pana aṃsakūṭe laggitā, idāni naṃ vāmahatthe ṭhapetvā vādanasajjaṃ katvā ādiyi. Tena vuttaṃ ‘‘ādāyā’’ti.</p> <p rend="subhead">Pañcasikhagītagāthāvaṇṇanā</p>
347.
*Durupasaṅkamā* means *dupayirupāsiyā*.

I am full of passion ( *sarāga*), full of hatred ( *sadosa*), full of delusion ( *samoha*). The teacher ( *satthā*) is without passion ( *vītarāga*), without hatred ( *vītadosa*), without delusion ( *vītamoha*). Therefore, the *tathāgatas* are *dupayirupāsiyā* through someone like me.

*Jhāyī* means *jhāyī* through contemplation of characteristics ( *lakkhaṇūpanijjhāna*) and through contemplation of objects ( *ārammaṇūpanijjhāna*).

*Jhānaratā* means delighting (*ratā*) in that very meditation ( *jhāna*).

*Tadantaraṃ paṭisallīnā* means secluded (*paṭisallīnā*) at that interval, or presently secluded.

Therefore, not only is a *jhāyī* who delights in meditation ( *jhānaratā*) *durupasaṅkamā*, but even one who is presently secluded is *durupasaṅkamā*.

*Pasādeyyāsī* means you should propitiate (*ārādheyyāsi*), saying, "Having caused an opportunity for me, may you give (*dadeyyāsīti*)."

*Beluvapaṇḍuvīṇaṃ ādāyā* means, "Surely it was taken before?" Yes, taken.

Having been attached to the shoulder ( *aṃsakūṭa*) by means of the way of going on the path ( *maggagamanavasena*), now, having placed it in the left hand ( *vāmahattha*) and having made it ready for playing ( *vādanasajjaṃ katvā*), he took it (*ādiyi*).

Therefore it was said, "Having taken (*ādāyā*)."

Explanation of the Song of Pañcasikha ( *pañcasikhagītagāthāvaṇṇanā*).
348
<p n="348" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">348</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Ekamantaṃ ṭhito kho pañcasikho gandhabbadevaputto beluvapaṇḍuvīṇaṃ <note>veḷuvapaṇḍuvīṇaṃ ādāya (syā.)</note> assāvesi, imā ca gāthā abhāsi buddhūpasañhitā dhammūpasañhitā saṅghūpasañhitā arahantūpasañhitā kāmūpasañhitā –</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Vande te pitaraṃ bhadde, timbaruṃ sūriyavacchase;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Yena jātāsi kalyāṇī, ānandajananī mama.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Vātova sedataṃ kanto, pānīyaṃva pipāsato;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Aṅgīrasi piyāmesi, dhammo arahatāmiva.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Āturasseva <pb ed="P" n="2.0266"/> bhesajjaṃ, bhojanaṃva jighacchato;</p> <pb ed="P" n="2.0266"/> <p rend="gathalast">Parinibbāpaya maṃ bhadde, jalantamiva vārinā.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Sītodakaṃ <pb ed="M" n="2.0213"/><pb ed="V" n="2.0196"/> pokkharaṇiṃ, yuttaṃ kiñjakkhareṇunā;</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0213"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0196"/> <p rend="gathalast">Nāgo ghammābhitattova, ogāhe te thanūdaraṃ.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Accaṅkusova nāgova, jitaṃ me tuttatomaraṃ;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Kāraṇaṃ nappajānāmi, sammatto lakkhaṇūruyā.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Tayi <pb ed="T" n="2.0301"/> gedhitacittosmi, cittaṃ vipariṇāmitaṃ;</p> <pb ed="T" n="2.0301"/> <p rend="gathalast">Paṭigantuṃ na sakkomi, vaṅkaghastova ambujo.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Vāmūru saja maṃ bhadde, saja maṃ mandalocane;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Palissaja maṃ kalyāṇi, etaṃ me abhipatthitaṃ.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Appako vata me santo, kāmo vellitakesiyā;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Anekabhāvo samuppādi, arahanteva dakkhiṇā.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Yaṃ me atthi kataṃ puññaṃ, arahantesu tādisu;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Taṃ me sabbaṅgakalyāṇi, tayā saddhiṃ vipaccataṃ.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Yaṃ <pb ed="P" n="2.0267"/> me atthi kataṃ puññaṃ, asmiṃ pathavimaṇḍale;</p> <pb ed="P" n="2.0267"/> <p rend="gathalast">Taṃ me sabbaṅgakalyāṇi, tayā saddhiṃ vipaccataṃ.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Sakyaputtova jhānena, ekodi nipako sato;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Amataṃ muni jigīsāno <note>jigiṃsāno (sī. syā. pī.)</note>, tamahaṃ sūriyavacchase.</p> <note>jigiṃsāno (sī. syā. pī.)</note> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Yathāpi muni nandeyya, patvā sambodhimuttamaṃ;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Evaṃ nandeyyaṃ kalyāṇi, missībhāvaṃ gato tayā.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Sakko ce me varaṃ dajjā, tāvatiṃsānamissaro;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Tāhaṃ bhadde vareyyāhe, evaṃ kāmo daḷho mama.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Sālaṃva <pb ed="V" n="2.0197"/> na ciraṃ phullaṃ, pitaraṃ te sumedhase;</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0197"/> <p rend="gathalast">Vandamāno namassāmi, yassā setādisī pajā’’ti.</p>
348. Then, Pañcasikha, the Gandharva-deva's son, stood to one side and played the *beluvapaṇḍuvīṇa* , accompanying it with these verses, connected to the Buddha, connected to the *dharma*, connected to the *saṃgha*, connected to the *arhats*, connected to sensual pleasure:

"I venerate your father, blessed one, Timburu, Sūriyavacchase;

Through whom you were born, auspicious one, the mother generating joy for me.

Like the wind delightful to one hot with sweat, like water to one who is thirsty;

We love Aṅgīrasa, like the *dharma* to the *arhats*.

Like medicine for one who is ill, like food for one who is hungry;

Bring me, gracious lady, sweet release with water cool from my consuming flames.

Like a cool lotus pond, filled with the pollen of the kiñjakkha flower;

A Nāga, as if scorched by the heat, plunges into your breasts and belly.

Like an elephant with a goad, my goad-like dart is conquered;

I do not understand the reason, intoxicated by your auspicious thighs.

In you, I am one whose mind is seized by longing, my mind is completely transformed;

I am unable to return, like a lotus caught in a bent reed.

Embrace me on your left thigh, blessed one, embrace me, large-eyed one;

Encircle me, auspicious one, this is what I desire.

Small indeed, it seems, is my joy, O you of the curly hair;

A manifold state has arisen, like an offering to the *arhats*.

Whatever merit there is done by me, among such *arhats*;

May that ripen for me together with you, O beautiful in every limb.

Whatever merit there is done by me, in this world-circle;

May that ripen for me together with you, O beautiful in every limb.

Like a Sakyan son in *dhyāna*, solitary, wise, mindful;

Seeking the nectar of immortality, a sage, Sūriyavacchase.

Just as a sage would rejoice, having attained the unsurpassed perfect awakening;

So would I rejoice, auspicious one, having gone to a state of union with you.

If Śakra would grant me a boon, the lord of the Thirty-three;

Then I, blessed one, would choose this: thus, my desire is firm.

Like a *śāla* tree not long in bloom, your father, of good intellect;

Venerating, I bow down, to whom such progeny belongs.”
<p n="348" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">348</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Assāvesī</hi>ti sāvesi. <hi rend="bold">Buddhūpasañhitā</hi>ti buddhaṃ ārabbha buddhaṃ nissayaṃ katvā pavattāti attho. Sesapadesupi eseva nayo.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Vande te pitaraṃ bhadde, timbaruṃ sūriyavacchase</hi>ti ettha <hi rend="bold">sūriyavacchasā</hi>ti sūriyasamānasarīrā. Tassā kira devadhītāya pādantato rasmi uṭṭhahitvā kesantaṃ ārohati, tasmā bālasūriyamaṇḍalasadisā khāyati, iti naṃ ‘‘sūriyavacchasā’’ti sañjānanti. Taṃ sandhāyāha – ‘‘bhadde, sūriyavacchase, tava pitaraṃ timbaruṃ gandhabbadevarājānaṃ vandāmī’’ti. <hi rend="bold">Yena jātāsi kalyāṇī</hi>ti yena <pb ed="M" n="2.0294"/> kāraṇabhūtena yaṃ timbaruṃ devarājānaṃ nissāya tvaṃ jātā, kalyāṇī sabbaṅgasobhanā. <hi rend="bold">Ānandajananī mamā</hi>ti mayhaṃ pītisomanassavaḍḍhanī.</p> <hi rend="bold">Vande te pitaraṃ bhadde, timbaruṃ sūriyavacchase</hi> <hi rend="bold">sūriyavacchasā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Yena jātāsi kalyāṇī</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0294"/> <hi rend="bold">Ānandajananī mamā</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Vātova sedataṃ sedataṃ kanto</hi> <hi rend="bold">Pānīyaṃva pipāsato</hi> <hi rend="bold">Aṅgīrasī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Dhammo arahatāmivā</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Jighacchato</hi>ti <pb ed="V" nibbāpehīti vadati.</p> <hi rend="bold">Jighacchato</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0264"/> <hi rend="bold">Jalantamiva vārinā</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Yuttaṃ kiñjakkhareṇunā</hi>ti padumakesarareṇunā yuttaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Nāgo ghammābhitatto vā</hi>ti ghammābhitattahatthī viya. <hi rend="bold">Ogāhe te thanūdara</hi>nti yathā so nāgo pokkharaṇiṃ ogāhitvā pivitvā aggasoṇḍamattaṃ paññāyamānaṃ katvā nimuggo sukhaṃ sātaṃ vindati, evaṃ kadā nu kho te thanūdaraṃ thanavemajjhaṃ <pb ed="P" n="3.0702"/> udarañca otaritvā ahaṃ sukhaṃ sātaṃ paṭilabhissāmīti vadati.</p> <hi rend="bold">Yuttaṃ kiñjakkhareṇunā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Nāgo ghammābhitatto vā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Ogāhe te thanūdara</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0702"/> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Accaṅkusova nāgova, jitaṃ me tuttatomaraṃ;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Kāraṇaṃ nappajānāmi, sammatto lakkhaṇūruyā’’ti. –</p> <p rend="bodytext">Ettha <hi rend="bold">tuttaṃ</hi> vuccati kaṇṇamūle vijjhanaayadaṇḍako. <hi rend="bold">Tomara</hi>nti pādādīsu vijjhanadaṇḍatomaraṃ. <hi rend="bold">Aṅkuso</hi>ti matthake vijjhanakuṭilakaṇṭako. Yo ca nāgo pabhinnamatto accaṅkuso hoti, aṅkusaṃ atīto; aṅkusena vijjhiyamānopi vasaṃ na gacchati, so ‘‘jitaṃ mayā tuttatomaraṃ, yo ahaṃ aṅkusassapi vasaṃ na gacchāmī’’ti madadappena kiñci kāraṇaṃ na bujjhati. Yathā so accaṅkuso nāgo ‘‘jitaṃ me tuttatomara’’nti kiñci kāraṇaṃ nappajānāti, evaṃ ahampi lakkhaṇasampannaūrutāya lakkhaṇūruyā sammatto matto pamatto ummatto viya kiñci kāraṇaṃ nappajānāmīti vadati. Atha vā accaṅkusova nāgo ahampi sammatto lakkhaṇūruyā kiñci tato <pb ed="M" n="2.0295"/> virāgakāraṇaṃ nappajānāmi. Kasmā? ‘‘Ghaso’’tipi pāṭho, ayamevattho.</p> <hi rend="bold">Tayi gedhitacittosmī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Gedhitacitto</hi> <hi rend="bold">Cittaṃ vipariṇāmita</hi> <hi rend="bold">Paṭigantuṃ na sakkomī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Vaṅkaghastova ambujo</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Vāmūrū</hi>ti vāmākārena saṇṭhitaūru, kadalikkhandhasadisaūrūti vā attho. <hi rend="bold">Sajā</hi>ti āliṅga. <hi āliṅga. <hi rend="bold">Etaṃ me abhipatthita</hi>nti etaṃ mayā abhiṇhaṃ patthitaṃ.</p> <hi rend="bold">Vāmūrū</hi> <hi rend="bold">Sajā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Mandalocane</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0265"/> <hi rend="bold">Palissajā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Etaṃ me abhipatthita</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Appako vata me santo</hi>ti pakatiyāva mando samāno. <hi rend="bold">Vellitakesiyā</hi>ti kesā muñcitvā piṭṭhiyaṃ vissaṭṭhakāle sappo <pb ed="P" n="3.0703"/> viya vellantā gacchantā assāti vellitakesī, tassā vellitakesiyā. <hi rend="bold">Anekabhāvo samuppādī</hi>ti anekavidho jāto. <hi rend="bold">Vellitakesiyā</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0703"/> <hi rend="bold">Anekabhāvo samuppādī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Anekabhāgo</hi> <hi rend="bold">Arahanteva dakkhiṇā</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Yaṃ me atthi kataṃ puñña</hi>nti yaṃ mayā kataṃ puññamatthi. <hi rend="bold">Arahantesu tādisū</hi>ti tādilakkhaṇappattesu arahantesu. <hi rend="bold">Tayā saddhiṃ vipaccata</hi>nti sabbaṃ tayā saddhimeva vipākaṃ detu.</p> <hi rend="bold">Yaṃ me atthi kataṃ puñña</hi> <hi rend="bold">Arahantesu tādisū</hi> <hi rend="bold">Tayā saddhiṃ vipaccata</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Ekodī</hi>ti ekībhāvaṃ gato. <hi rend="bold">Nipako sato</hi>ti nepakkaṃ vuccati paññā, tāya samannāgatoti nipako. Satiyā samannāgatattā sato. <hi rend="bold">Amataṃ muni jigīsāno</hi>ti yathā so buddhamuni amataṃ nibbānaṃ jigīsati pariyesati, evaṃ taṃ ahaṃ sūriyavacchase jigīsāmi pariyesāmi. Yathā vā so amataṃ jigīsāno esanto gavesanto vicarati, evāhaṃ taṃ esanto gavesanto vicarāmītipi attho.</p> <hi rend="bold">Ekodī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Nipako sato</hi> <hi rend="bold">Amataṃ muni jigīsāno</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Yathāpi muni nandeyya, patvā sambodhimuttama</hi>nti yathā buddhamuni bodhipallaṅke nisinno sabbaññutaññāṇaṃ patvā nandeyya toseyya. <hi rend="bold">Evaṃ nandeyya</hi>nti <pb ed="M" n="2.0296"/> evameva ahampi tayā missībhāvaṃ gato nandeyyaṃ, pītisomanassajāto bhaveyyanti vadati.</p> <hi rend="bold">Yathāpi muni nandeyya, patvā sambodhimuttama</hi> <hi rend="bold">Evaṃ nandeyya</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0296"/> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Tāhaṃ bhadde vareyyāhe</hi>ti <hi rend="bold">ahe</hi>ti āmantanaṃ, ahe bhadde sūriyavacchase, sakkena devānamindena ‘‘kiṃ dvīsu devalokesu devarajjaṃ gaṇhasi, suriyavacchasa’’nti, evaṃ vare dinne devarajjaṃ pahāya ‘‘sūriyavacchasaṃ gaṇhāmī’’ti evaṃ taṃ ahaṃ vareyyaṃ iccheyyaṃ gaṇheyyanti attho.</p> <hi rend="bold">Tāhaṃ bhadde vareyyāhe</hi> <hi rend="bold">ahe</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Sālaṃva na ciraṃ phulla</hi>nti tava pitu nagaradvāre naciraṃ pupphito sālo atthi. So ativiya manoharo. Taṃ naciraṃ phullasālaṃ viya. <hi rend="bold">Pitaraṃ te sumedhase</hi>ti atisassirīkaṃ tava pitaraṃ <hi rend="bold">vandamāno namassāmi</hi> namo karomi. <hi rend="bold">Yassāsetādisī pajā</hi>ti yassa āsi etādisī dhītā.</p> <hi rend="bold">Sālaṃva na ciraṃ phulla</hi> <hi rend="bold">Pitaraṃ te sumedhase</hi> <hi rend="bold">vandamāno namassāmi</hi> <hi rend="bold">Yassāsetādisī pajā</hi>
348. *Assāvesī*: Sāvesi means he caused to be heard.

*Buddhūpasañhitā*: It means commencing with the buddha, having made the buddha the basis, it proceeds.

The same method of explanation applies to the other clauses too.

*Vande te pitaram bhadde, timbarum sūriyavacchase*: Here, *sūriyavacchasā* means having a body like the sun.

It is said that a ray rises from the end of the feet of that daughter of a god and ascends to the end of the hair; therefore, she appears like a young solar disc; thus, they recognize her as "Sūriyavacchasā".

Referring to that, he said, "O auspicious Sūriyavacchase, I venerate your father, Timburu, the Gandharva divine king."

*Yena jātāsi kalyāṇī*: By whom, having the nature of a cause, you were born, that Timburu, relying on the divine king, you were born, O auspicious one, beautiful in all limbs.

*Ānandajananī mamā*: It increases my joy and happiness.

*Vande te pitaram bhadde, timbarum sūriyavacchase sūriyavacchasā yena jātāsi kalyāṇī ānandajananī mamā*

*Vātova sedatam kanto*: Just as the wind is desirable and pleasing for the removal of accumulated sweat, such is its meaning.

*Pānīyamva pipāsato*: To one who desires to drink, to a thirsty person, one overcome by thirst.

*Aṅgīrasī*: Aṅgīrasī means having rays in the limbs, and addressing that, he speaks.

*Dhammo arahatāmivā*: Like the noble transcendental dharma of the *arhats*.

*Vātova sedatam kanto pānīyamva pipāsato aṅgīrasī dhammo arahatāmivā*

*Jighacchato*: To one who desires to eat, one overcome by hunger.

*Jalantamiva vārinā*: Just as someone would extinguish a blazing fire with a pot of water, so extinguish my burning of lust born from you, he says.

*Jighacchato jalantamiva vārinā*

*Yuttam kiñjakkhareṇunā*: Joined with the pollen of the lotus flower.

*Nāgo ghammābhitatto vā*: Like an elephant afflicted by the heat.

*Ogāhe te thanūdara*: Just as that elephant, having entered a pond, having drunk and having made the tip of its trunk appear, plunges in and finds ease and pleasure, so when will I, having descended into your breasts and abdomen, obtain ease and pleasure, he says.

*Yuttam kiñjakkhareṇunā nāgo ghammābhitatto vā ogāhe te thanūdara*

"Just as an elephant goaded with a goad, I have conquered my goad and lance;

I do not understand the cause, I am intoxicated by the thighs marked with auspicious signs."

Here *tuttaṃ* is said to be an iron rod for piercing the ear.

*tomara* is a piercing rod in the feet and so on.

*aṅkuso* is a curved thorn for piercing the head.

And the elephant that is in rut and beyond the goad, even when pierced by the goad, does not come under control, that one, with the pride of intoxication, "I have conquered my goad and lance, because I do not come under the control even of the goad," does not understand any cause.

Just as that elephant goaded with a goad does not understand any cause, saying, "I have conquered my goad and lance," so I too, intoxicated by the thighs endowed with auspicious marks, am inebriated, negligent, maddened, as if, I do not understand any cause, he says.

Or else, just as that elephant goaded with a goad, I too, intoxicated by the thighs marked with auspicious signs, do not understand any cause of detachment from that.

Why? Because, just as by that elephant, "I have conquered my goad and lance," I do not heed the words of anyone speaking.

*Tuttaṃ tomara aṅkuso*

*Tayi gedhitacittosmī*: O auspicious one, O thighs marked with auspicious signs, I am one whose mind is attached to you.

*Gedhitacitto*: Or, one whose mind has approached greed.

*Cittaṃ vipariṇāmita*: The mind, having abandoned its nature, remains still.

*Paṭigantum na sakkomī*: I am not able to turn back.

*Vaṅkaghastova ambujo*: Like a fish that has swallowed a hook and remains still.

*Ghaso'tipi pāṭho*, Ayamevattho: *ghaso* is also a reading; this is the meaning.
I am possessed with longing in you; with longing, my mind is transformed; I am unable to resist; like a hook-handed lotus.

*vāmūrū* means thighs shaped beautifully, or thighs resembling banana stalks; that is the meaning.

*sajā* means embrace.

*mandalocane* means women do not intensely meditate or reflect with dullness; therefore, they are called "dull-eyed."

*palissajā* means embrace with all parts.

*etaṃ me abhipatthita* means this is what I have always desired.

*sajā*
*mandalocane*
*palissajā*
*etaṃ me abhipatthita*

*appako vata me santo* means by nature, being slow, he is the same.

*vellitakesiyā* means letting loose the hair and at the time of scattering it on the back, like a snake going wavering, she has wavering hair; of her with wavering hair.

*anekabhāvo samuppādī* means born of various kinds.

*anekabhāgo* or it is a reading.

*arahanteva dakkhiṇā* means like a gift given to an *arhat*, it is distinct from various forms.
