*yaṃ me atthi kataṃ puñña* means whatever merit I have done, exists.

*arahantesu tādisū* means among the *arhats* who have attained such characteristics.

*tayā saddhiṃ vipaccata* means may everything ripen with you.

*yaṃ me atthi kataṃ puñña*
*arahantesu tādisū*
*tayā saddhiṃ vipaccata*

*ekodī* means having gone to oneness.

*nipako sato* means wisdom is called skillfulness, by that, he is endowed with skillfulness.

Through being endowed with mindfulness, he is mindful.

*amataṃ muni jigīsāno* means just as that Buddha, the sage, desires immortality, nirvāṇa, seeks, so too, I, in Sūriyavacchasa, desire, seek.

Or, just as he, desiring immortality, seeking, searching, wanders, so too, I, seeking, searching, wander about, is also the meaning.

*ekodī*
*nipako sato*
*amataṃ muni jigīsāno*

*yathāpi muni nandeyya, patvā sambodhimuttama* means just as the Buddha sage, sitting on the *bodhi* platform, having attained omniscience, would rejoice, would be pleased.

*evaṃ nandeyya* means just so, I too, having gone to union with you, would rejoice, would become born of joy and gladness, he says.

*yathāpi muni nandeyya, patvā sambodhimuttama*
*evaṃ nandeyya*

*tāhaṃ bhadde vareyyāhe* means *ahe* is an address, O beautiful Sūriyavacchase, Sakka, the lord of the gods, [said], "Why do you take the kingdom of gods in two heavens, Sūriyavacchase?", thus, when a boon was given, abandoning the kingdom of gods, [she said], "I will take Sūriyavacchasa," thus, that I would choose, would desire, would take, is the meaning.

*tāhaṃ bhadde vareyyāhe*
*ahe*

*sālaṃva na ciraṃ phulla* means near the city gate of your father, there is a *sāla* tree that has recently blossomed.

It is exceedingly charming.

Like that *sāla* tree which has recently blossomed.

*pitaraṃ te sumedhase* means I bow in homage, I pay reverence to your father, who is exceedingly splendid,

*yassāsetādisī pajā* means to whom there was such a daughter.

*sālaṃva na ciraṃ phulla*
*pitaraṃ te sumedhase*
*vandamāno namassāmi*
*yassāsetādisī pajā*
349
<p n="349" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">349</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Evaṃ vutte bhagavā pañcasikhaṃ gandhabbadevaputtaṃ etadavoca – ‘‘saṃsandati <pb ed="T" n="2.0302"/> kho te, pañcasikha, tantissaro gītassarena, gītassaro ca tantissarena; na ca pana <note>neva pana (syā.)</note> te pañcasikha, tantissaro gītassaraṃ ativattati, gītassaro ca tantissaraṃ. Kadā saṃyūḷhā pana te, pañcasikha, imā gāthā buddhūpasañhitā dhammūpasañhitā <pb ed="M" n="2.0214"/> saṅghūpasañhitā arahantūpasañhitā kāmūpasañhitā’’ti? ‘‘Ekamidaṃ, bhante, samayaṃ bhagavā uruvelāyaṃ viharati najjā nerañjarāya tīre ajapālanigrodhe paṭhamābhisambuddho <pb ed="P" n="2.0268"/>. Tena kho panāhaṃ, bhante, samayena bhaddā nāma sūriyavacchasā timbaruno gandhabbarañño dhītā, tamabhikaṅkhāmi. Sā kho pana, bhante, bhaginī parakāminī hoti; sikhaṇḍī nāma mātalissa saṅgāhakassa putto, tamabhikaṅkhati. Yato kho ahaṃ, bhante, taṃ bhaginiṃ nālatthaṃ kenaci pariyāyena. Athāhaṃ beluvapaṇḍuvīṇaṃ ādāya yena timbaruno gandhabbarañño nivesanaṃ tenupasaṅkamiṃ; upasaṅkamitvā beluvapaṇḍuvīṇaṃ assāvesiṃ, imā ca gāthā abhāsiṃ buddhūpasañhitā dhammūpasañhitā saṅghūpasañhitā arahantūpasañhitā kāmūpasañhitā –</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Vande te pitaraṃ bhadde, timbaruṃ sūriyavacchase;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Yena jātāsi kalyāṇī, ānandajananī mama. …pe…</p> <p rend="gatha1">Sālaṃva na ciraṃ phullaṃ, pitaraṃ te sumedhase;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Vandamāno namassāmi, yassā setādisī pajā’’ti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Evaṃ vutte, bhante, bhaddā sūriyavacchasā maṃ etadavoca – ‘na kho me, mārisa, so bhagavā sammukhā diṭṭho api ca sutoyeva me so bhagavā <pb ed="T" n="2.0303"/> devānaṃ tāvatiṃsānaṃ sudhammāyaṃ sabhāyaṃ upanaccantiyā. Yato kho tvaṃ, mārisa, taṃ bhagavantaṃ kittesi, hotu no ajja samāgamo’ti. Soyeva <pb ed="P" n="2.0269"/> no, bhante, tassā bhaginiyā saddhiṃ samāgamo ahosi. Na ca dāni tato pacchā’’ti.</p> <pb ed="T" n="2.0303"/> <pb ed="P" n="2.0269"/> <p rend="subhead">Sakkūpasaṅkama</p>
Having said this, the Lord said this to Pañcaśikha (Panchashikha), the son of a Gandharva deity: "Pañcasikha (Panchashikha), the sound of your lute blends with the sound of your song, and the sound of your song blends with the sound of your lute; and Pañcasikha (Panchashikha), the sound of your lute does not surpass the sound of your song, nor does the sound of your song surpass the sound of your lute. When, Pañcasikha (Panchashikha), were these verses, connected with the Buddha, connected with the Dharma, connected with the Saṅgha, connected with the Arhats, and connected with sensual desire, composed by you?"

"Venerable sir, on one occasion the Lord was dwelling at Uruvelā, on the bank of the Nerañjarā River, at the foot of the Ajapāla Banyan tree, just after his Awakening. At that time, venerable sir, I was in love with Bhaddā (Bhadra), also named Sūriyavacchasā (Suryavarchasa), the daughter of Timbaru (Timbaru), king of the Gandharvas. But that lady, venerable sir, was in love with another; she favored Sikhaddi (Shikhandi), the son of Mātali (Matali), the charioteer. And when, venerable sir, I found that I could not win the lady by any means, then I took my Beluvapaṇḍu lute and went to the abode of Timbaru (Timbaru), king of the Gandharvas; having gone there, I played my Beluvapaṇḍu lute, and recited these verses connected with the Buddha, connected with the Dharma, connected with the Saṅgha, connected with the Arhats, and connected with sensual desire:

‘I praise your father, Bhaddā (Bhadra), Timbaru (Timbaru) of the radiant splendor, by whom you were born, auspicious one, the source of my joy. …pe… I, full of good sense, bow down and pay homage to your father, Sumedhase (Sumedhase), like a Sāla tree in full bloom for only a short time, whose progeny is so bright.’

"When this was said, venerable sir, Bhaddā (Bhadra) Sūriyavacchasā (Suryavarchasa) said this to me: ‘Sir, that Lord has not been seen by me face to face, but I have only heard of that Lord while dancing in the Sudhammā assembly of the Tāvatiṃsa (Tavatimsa) gods. And since, sir, you praise that Blessed Lord so highly, let us meet today.’ And so, Lord, I met the lady. Not then but later."

The Approach to Sakka (Shakra)
<p n="349" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">349</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Saṃsandatī</hi>ti <pb ed="V" n="2.0266"/> kasmā gītasaddassa ceva vīṇāsaddassa <pb ed="P" n="3.0704"/> ca vaṇṇaṃ kathesi? Kiṃ tattha bhagavato sārāgo atthīti? Natthi. Chaḷaṅgupekkhāya upekkhako bhagavā etādisesu ṭhānesu, kevalaṃ iṭṭhāniṭṭhaṃ jānāti, na tattha rajjati. Vuttampi cetaṃ ‘‘saṃvijjati kho, āvuso, bhagavato cakkhu, passati bhagavā cakkhunā rūpaṃ, chandarāgo bhagavato natthi, suvimuttacitto bhagavā. Saṃvijjati kho, āvuso, bhagavato sota’’ntiādi (saṃ. ni. 4.232). Sace pana vaṇṇaṃ na katheyya, pañcasikho ‘‘okāso me kato’’ti na jāneyya. Atha sakko ‘‘bhagavatā pañcasikhassa okāso na kato’’ti devatā gahetvā tatova paṭinivatteyya, tato mahājāniyo bhaveyya. Vaṇṇe pana kathite ‘‘kato bhagavatā pañcasikhassa okāso’’ti devatāhi saddhiṃ upasaṅkamitvā pañhaṃ pucchitvā vissajjanāvasāne asītiyā devatāsahassehi saddhiṃ sotāpattiphale patiṭṭhahissatīti ñatvā vaṇṇaṃ kathesi.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Tattha <hi rend="bold">kadā saṃyūḷhā</hi>ti kadā ganthitā piṇḍitā. <hi rend="bold">Tena kho panāhaṃ, bhante, samayenā</hi>ti tena samayena tasmiṃ tumhākaṃ sambodhippattito paṭṭhāya aṭṭhame sattāhe. <hi rend="bold">Bhaddā nāma sūriyavacchasā</hi>ti nāmato bhaddā sarīrasampattiyā sūriyavacchasā. <hi rend="bold">Bhaginī</hi>ti vohāravacanametaṃ, devadhītāti attho. <hi rend="bold">Parakāminī</hi>ti paraṃ kāmeti abhikaṅkhati.</p> <hi rend="bold">kadā saṃyūḷhā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Tena kho panāhaṃ, bhante, samayenā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Bhaddā nāma sūriyavacchasā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Bhaginī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Parakāminī</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Upanaccantiyā</hi>ti <pb ed="M" n="2.0297"/> naccamānāya. Sā kira ekasmiṃ samaye cātumahārājikadevehi saddhiṃ sakkassa devarājassa naccaṃ dassanatthāya gatā, tasmiñca khaṇe sakko tathāgatassa aṭṭha yathābhucce guṇe payirudāhāsi. Evaṃ tasmiṃ divase gantvā naccantī assosi.</p> <hi rend="bold">Upanaccantiyā</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0297"/> <p rend="subhead">Sakkūpasaṅkamavaṇṇanā</p>
349. *Saṃsandati* means: From whence did he speak of the praise/color (*vaṇṇaṃ*) of the sound of song and the sound of the lute? Does the Blessed One have attachment (*sārāgo atthīti*) there? No.

The Blessed One, with equanimity towards the six factors (*chaḷaṅgupekkhāya*), is indifferent in such places. He simply knows what is desirable (*iṭṭha*) and undesirable (*aniṭṭhaṃ*), and is not attached (*rajjati*) there.

It was also said, "There exists in the Blessed One the eye (*cakkhu*). The Blessed One sees form (*rūpaṃ*) with the eye. There is no desire or attachment (*chandarāgo*) in the Blessed One; the Blessed One is well-liberated in mind (*suvimuttacitto*).

There exists in the Blessed One the ear (*sota*)", etc. (Saṃ. Ni. 4.232).

If, however, he did not speak praise/color (*vaṇṇaṃ*), Pañcasikha would not know, "An opportunity (*okāso*) has been made for me."

Then Śakra, taking the deities (*devatā*) thinking, "An opportunity (*okāso*) has not been made for Pañcasikha by the Blessed One", would turn back from there. From that, there would be great understanding (*mahājāniyo*).

However, when praise/color (*vaṇṇe*) has been spoken, knowing "An opportunity (*okāso*) has been made for Pañcasikha by the Blessed One," going together with the deities (*devatāhi saddhiṃ*), asking a question (*pañhaṃ pucchitvā*), at the end of the explanation (*vissajjanāvasāne*), with eighty thousand deities (*asītiyā devatāsahassehi saddhiṃ*) he will be established in the fruit of stream-entry (*sotāpattiphale patiṭṭhahissatīti ñatvā*) he spoke praise/color (*vaṇṇaṃ kathesi*).

There, *kadā saṃyūḷhā* means: when joined, when tied, when compacted (*kadā ganthitā piṇḍitā*).

*Tena kho panāhaṃ, bhante, samayenā* means: at that time (*tena samayena*), in the eighth week (*aṭṭhame sattāhe*) from the attainment of your enlightenment (*tumhākaṃ sambodhippattito paṭṭhāya*).

*Bhaddā nāma sūriyavacchasā* means: by name Bhaddā, with the splendor of the sun through the perfection of her body (*sarīrasampattiyā sūriyavacchasā*).

*Bhaginī* means: this is a conventional expression (*vohāravacanametaṃ*); it means "daughter of a god" (*devadhītāti attho*).

*Parakāminī* means: she desires another (*paraṃ kāmeti abhikaṅkhati*).

When joined (*kadā saṃyūḷhā*) at that time (*tena kho panāhaṃ, bhante, samayenā*), Bhaddā named with the splendor of the sun (*bhaddā nāma sūriyavacchasā*), sister (*bhaginī*), she desires another (*parakāminī*) because of dancing (*upanaccantiyā*), to the dancing one (*naccamānāya*).

It is said that at one time, together with the Cātumahārājika gods (*cātumahārājikadevehi saddhiṃ*), she went to see the dance (*naccaṃ dassanatthāya gatā*) of Śakra, the king of the gods (*sakkassa devarājassa*), and at that moment (*khaṇe*) Śakra praised the eight true qualities (*aṭṭha yathābhucce guṇe payirudāhāsi*) of the Tathāgata.

Thus, on that day, having gone, she heard the dancing (*gantvā naccantī assosi*).

Because of dancing (*upanaccantiyā*)

Explanation of Approaching Śakra (*sakkūpasaṅkamavaṇṇanā*)
350
<p n="350" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">350</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Atha <pb ed="V" n="2.0198"/> kho sakkassa devānamindassa etadahosi – ‘‘paṭisammodati pañcasikho gandhabbadevaputto bhagavatā, bhagavā ca pañcasikhenā’’ti. Atha kho sakko devānamindo pañcasikhaṃ gandhabbadevaputtaṃ āmantesi – ‘‘abhivādehi me tvaṃ, tāta pañcasikha, bhagavantaṃ – ‘sakko, bhante, devānamindo sāmacco saparijano bhagavato pāde sirasā vandatī’ti’’. ‘‘Evaṃ bhaddantavā’’ti kho pañcasikho gandhabbadevaputto sakkassa devānamindassa paṭissutvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādeti – ‘‘sakko, bhante, devānamindo sāmacco saparijano bhagavato <pb ed="M" n="2.0215"/> pāde sirasā vandatī’’ti. ‘‘Evaṃ sukhī hotu, pañcasikha, sakko devānamindo sāmacco saparijano; sukhakāmā hi devā manussā asurā nāgā gandhabbā ye caññe santi puthukāyā’’ti.</p>
Then Sakka, the lord of the gods, had this thought: "Pañcasikha, the gandharva, the son of a god, is conversing amicably with the Blessed One, and the Blessed One with Pañcasikha."

Then Sakka, the lord of the gods, addressed Pañcasikha, the gandharva, the son of a god, saying: "Go and greet the Blessed One for me, dear Pañcasikha, saying: 'Sakka, lord of the gods, together with his ministers and retinue, pays homage with his head at the feet of the Blessed One.'"

"Very well, lord," replied Pañcasikha, the gandharva, the son of a god, to Sakka, the lord of the gods. He then greeted the Blessed One, saying: "Sakka, lord of the gods, together with his ministers and retinue, pays homage with his head at the feet of the Blessed One."

"[May it be] Thus, may Sakka, lord of the gods, together with his ministers and retinue, be happy, Pañcasikha; for the gods, humans, asuras, nāgas, gandharvas, and whatever other diverse beings there are, all desire happiness."
<p n="350" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">350</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Paṭisammodatī</hi>ti ‘‘saṃsandati kho te’’tiādīni vadanto bhagavā sammodati, pañcasikho paṭisammodati. Gāthā ca bhāsanto pañcasikho sammodati, bhagavā paṭisammodati. <hi rend="bold">Āmantesī</hi>ti jānāpesi. Tassa kirevaṃ ahosi ‘‘ayaṃ pañcasikho mayā mama kammena pesito attano kammaṃ karoti. Evarūpassa satthu santike ṭhatvā kāmaguṇūpasañhitaṃ ananucchavikaṃ kathesi, naṭā nāma nillajjā honti, kathento vippakārampi dasseyya, handa naṃ mama kammaṃ jānāpemī’’ti cintetvā āmantesi.</p>
<p n="350" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">350</hi><hi rend="dot">. </hi><hi rend="bold">paṭisammodatī</hi> means that while saying "saṃsandati kho te" etc., the Blessed One rejoices ( *sammodati* ), Pañcasikha (name of a Gandharva in Buddhist literature) *paṭisammodati*.

While reciting the *gāthā* (verse), Pañcasikha rejoices (*sammodati*), the Blessed One (*bhagavā*) *paṭisammodati*.

<hi rend="bold">āmantesī</hi> means he informs (*jānāpesi*).

To him, it occurred thus: "This Pañcasikha was sent by me through my work (*kammena*), and he does his own work (*kammaṃ karoti*).

Having stood near a teacher (*satthu*) of such form (*evarūpassa*), he spoke (*kathesi*) something unsuitable (*ananucchavikaṃ*) connected with the qualities of desire (*kāmaguṇūpasañhitaṃ*). Actors (*naṭā*) are indeed shameless (*nillajjā honti*). While speaking, he might show (*dasseyya*) distortion (*vippakārampi*). Now, I will make him know (*jānāpemī*) my work (*kammaṃ*," thinking thus, he addressed (*āmantesi*).
</p>
351
<p n="351" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">351</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Evañca pana tathāgatā evarūpe mahesakkhe yakkhe abhivadanti. Abhivadito sakko devānamindo bhagavato indasālaguhaṃ pavisitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhāsi. Devāpi tāvatiṃsā indasālaguhaṃ pavisitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhaṃsu. Pañcasikhopi gandhabbadevaputto indasālaguhaṃ pavisitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhāsi.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Tena kho pana samayena indasālaguhā <pb ed="T" n="2.0304"/> visamā santī samā samapādi, sambādhā santī urundā <note>uruddā (ka.)</note> samapādi, andhakāro guhāyaṃ antaradhāyi, āloko udapādi yathā taṃ devānaṃ <pb ed="P" n="2.0270"/> devānubhāvena.</p> <pb ed="T" n="2.0304"/> <note>uruddā (ka.)</note> <pb ed="P" n="2.0270"/>
351. Furthermore, the *tathāgatas* greet such mighty *yakṣas* in this way.

Having been greeted, Śakka, the lord of the gods, entered the Indasāla Cave, saluted the Blessed One, and stood to one side.

And the Thirty-Three Gods (*devāpi tāvatiṃsā*) also entered the Indasāla Cave, saluted the Blessed One, and stood to one side.

Pañcaśikha, the son of the *gandharva* gods, also entered the Indasāla Cave, saluted the Blessed One, and stood to one side.

Then, at that time, the Indasāla Cave, which was uneven (*visamā santī*), became even (*samā samapādi*); the narrow places (*sambādhā santī*) became spacious (*urundā samapādi*), the darkness (*andhakāro*) in the cave disappeared (*antaradhāyi*), and light (*āloko*) arose (*udapādi*), through the power of the gods (*devānubhāvena*).
<p n="351" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">351</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Evañca</hi><pb ed="P" n="3.0705"/><pb ed="V" n="2.0267"/><hi rend="bold">pana tathāgatā</hi>ti dhammasaṅgāhakattherehi ṭhapitavacanaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Abhivadantī</hi>ti abhivādanasampaṭicchanena vaḍḍhitavacanena vadanti. <hi rend="bold">Abhivadito</hi>ti vaḍḍhitavacanena vutto.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Urundā samapādī</hi>ti mahantā vivaṭā ahosi, andhakāro guhāyaṃ antaradhāyi. <hi rend="bold">Āloko udapādī</hi>ti yo pakatiyā guhāyaṃ andhakāro, so antarahito, āloko jāto. Sabbametaṃ dhammasaṅgāhakānaṃ vacanaṃ.</p> <hi rend="bold">Urundā samapādī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Āloko udapādī</hi>
351.
And so, the words established by the compilers of the Dharma, the elders, [are as follows]:
"Abhivadantī" means they speak with speech that is increased by respectful salutation.
"Abhivadito" means spoken with increased speech.

Urundā samapādī means it was greatly opened up; darkness disappeared in the cave.
Āloko udapādī means that darkness which was naturally in the cave, that disappeared, and light arose.
All of this is the word of the compilers of the Dharma.

Urundā samapādī, āloko udapādī.
352
<p n="352" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">352</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Atha kho bhagavā sakkaṃ devānamindaṃ etadavoca – ‘‘acchariyamidaṃ āyasmato kosiyassa, abbhutamidaṃ āyasmato kosiyassa tāva bahukiccassa bahukaraṇīyassa yadidaṃ idhāgamana’’nti. ‘‘Cirapaṭikāhaṃ, bhante, bhagavantaṃ dassanāya upasaṅkamitukāmo; api ca devānaṃ tāvatiṃsānaṃ kehici kehici <note>kehici (syā.)</note> kiccakaraṇīyehi byāvaṭo; evāhaṃ nāsakkhiṃ bhagavantaṃ dassanāya upasaṅkamituṃ. Ekamidaṃ, bhante, samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati salaḷāgārake. Atha khvāhaṃ, bhante, sāvatthiṃ agamāsiṃ bhagavantaṃ dassanāya. Tena kho pana, bhante, samayena bhagavā aññatarena samādhinā nisinno hoti, bhūjati <note>bhuñjatī ca (sī. pī.), bhujagī (syā.)</note> ca nāma vessavaṇassa mahārājassa paricārikā bhagavantaṃ paccupaṭṭhitā hoti, pañjalikā namassamānā tiṭṭhati. Atha khvāhaṃ, bhante, bhūjatiṃ etadavocaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0199"/> – ‘abhivādehi me tvaṃ, bhagini, bhagavantaṃ – ‘‘sakko, bhante, devānamindo <pb ed="T" n="2.0305"/> sāmacco saparijano bhagavato pāde sirasā vandatī’’ti. Evaṃ vutte, bhante, sā bhūjati maṃ etadavoca – ‘akālo kho, mārisa, bhagavantaṃ dassanāya; paṭisallīno bhagavā’ti. ‘Tena <pb ed="P" n="2.0271"/> hī, bhagini, yadā bhagavā tamhā samādhimhā vuṭṭhito hoti, atha mama vacanena bhagavantaṃ abhivādehi – ‘‘sakko, bhante, devānamindo <pb ed="M" n="2.0216"/> sāmacco saparijano bhagavato pāde sirasā vandatī’’ti. Kacci me sā, bhante, bhaginī bhagavantaṃ abhivādesi? Sarati bhagavā tassā bhaginiyā vacana’’nti? ‘‘Abhivādesi maṃ sā, devānaminda, bhaginī, sarāmahaṃ tassā bhaginiyā vacanaṃ. Api cāhaṃ āyasmato nemisaddena <note>cakkanemisaddena (syā.)</note> tamhā samādhimhā vuṭṭhito’’ti. ‘‘Ye te, bhante, devā amhehi paṭhamataraṃ tāvatiṃsakāyaṃ upapannā, tesaṃ me sammukhā sutaṃ sammukhā paṭiggahitaṃ – ‘yadā tathāgatā loke uppajjanti arahanto sammāsambuddhā, dibbā kāyā paripūrenti, hāyanti asurakāyā’ti. Taṃ me idaṃ, bhante, sakkhidiṭṭhaṃ yato tathāgato loke uppanno arahaṃ sammāsambuddho, dibbā kāyā paripūrenti, hāyanti asurakāyāti.</p> <p rend="subhead">Gopakavatthu</p>
Then the Lord said to Śakra, the lord of the gods: “It is wonderful, venerable Kośiya, it is marvelous, venerable Kośiya, that you, with so much to do, so many duties, have come here!”

“Lord, I have long desired to visit the Blessed One, but I have been preoccupied with various duties of the gods of the Heaven of the Thirty-Three (Trāyastriṃśa). Thus, I have not been able to visit the Blessed One.

Once, Lord, the Blessed One was staying at Śrāvastī (Sāvatthī) in the Salala hut.

Then, Lord, I went to Śrāvastī (Sāvatthī) to see the Blessed One.

At that time, Lord, the Blessed One was sitting in a certain state of meditative absorption (samādhi), and Bhūjati, the attendant of King Vaiśravaṇa (Vessavaṇa), the great king, was attending upon the Blessed One, standing with her hands folded in reverence (pañjalikā).

Then, Lord, I said to Bhūjati: ‘Pay homage to the Blessed One for me, sister, saying: "Śakra (Sakka), Lord, the lord of the gods, together with his ministers and retinue, pays homage with his head at the feet of the Blessed One."’

When this was said, Lord, Bhūjati said to me: ‘It is not the right time to see the Blessed One; the Blessed One is in retreat.’

‘Then, sister, when the Blessed One arises from that meditative absorption (samādhi), please salute the Blessed One with my words, saying: "Śakra (Sakka), Lord, the lord of the gods, together with his ministers and retinue, pays homage with his head at the feet of the Blessed One."’

Lord, did that sister pay homage to the Blessed One for me? Does the Blessed One remember the words of that sister?” “That sister did pay homage to me, Śakra (Sakka), lord of the gods, and I remember the words of that sister.

Moreover, I arose from that meditative absorption (samādhi) at the sound of Āyasmato Nemi.”

“Those gods, Lord, who arose in the Heaven of the Thirty-Three (Trāyastriṃśa) before us, have heard and received directly from me: ‘When the *Tathāgatas*, worthy ones, perfectly awakened ones, arise in the world, the hosts of gods increase, and the hosts of *asuras* decline.’

That is what I have directly witnessed, Lord, that when the *Tathāgata* arose in the world, a worthy one, a perfectly awakened one, the hosts of gods increase, and the hosts of *asuras* decline.”

Gopaka’s story.
<p n="352" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">352</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Cirapaṭikāhaṃ, bhante</hi>ti cirato ahaṃ, cirato paṭṭhāyāhaṃ dassanakāmoti attho. <hi rend="bold">Kehici kehici kiccakaraṇīyehī</hi>ti devānaṃ dhītā ca puttā ca aṅke nibbattanti, pādaparicārikā itthiyo sayane nibbattanti, tāsaṃ maṇḍanapasādhanakārikā devatā sayanaṃ parivāretvā nibbattanti, veyyāvaccakarā antovimāne nibbattanti, etesaṃ atthāya aḍḍakaraṇaṃ nāma natthi. Ye pana sīmantare nibbattanti, te ‘‘tava santakā, mama santakā’’ti nicchetuṃ asakkontā aḍḍaṃ karonti, sakkaṃ devarājānaṃ pucchanti. So ‘‘yassa vimānaṃ āsannataraṃ, tassa santakā’’ti vadati. Sace <pb ed="M" n="2.0298"/> dvepi samaṭṭhāne honti, ‘‘yassa vimānaṃ olokentī ṭhitā, tassa santakā’’ti vadati. Sace ekampi na oloketi, taṃ ubhinnaṃ kalahupacchedanatthaṃ attano santakaṃ karoti. Kīḷādīnipi kiccāni karaṇīyāneva. Evarūpāni tāni karaṇīyāni sandhāya ‘‘kehici kehici kiccakaraṇīyehī’’ti āha.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Salaḷāgārake</hi>ti salaḷamayagandhakuṭiyaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Aññatarena samādhinā</hi>ti tadā kira bhagavā sakkasseva aparipākagataṃ ñāṇaṃ viditvā okāsaṃ akāretukāmo phalasamāpattivihārena nisīdi. Taṃ esa ajānanto ‘‘aññatarena samādhinā’’ti āha. <hi rend="bold">Bhūjati ca nāmā</hi>ti <hi rend="bold">bhūjatī</hi>ti tassā nāmaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Paricārikā</hi>ti pādaparicārikā devadhītā. Sā kira dve phalāni pattā, tenassā devaloke abhiratiyeva natthi. Niccaṃ bhagavato upaṭṭhānaṃ āgantvā añjaliṃ sirasi ṭhapetvā bhagavantaṃ namassamānā tiṭṭhati. <hi rend="bold">Nemisaddena tamhā samādhimhā vuṭṭhito</hi>ti ‘‘samāpanno saddaṃ suṇātī’’ti no vata re vattabbe, nanu bhagavā sakkassa devānamindassa <pb ed="P" n="3.0706"/> ‘‘apicāhaṃ āyasmato cakkanemisaddena tamhā samādhimhā vuṭṭhito’’ti bhaṇatīti. Tiṭṭhatu nemisaddo, samāpanno nāma antosamāpattiyaṃ kaṇṇamūle dhamamānassa saṅkhayugaḷassāpi asanisannipātassāpi saddaṃ na suṇāti. Bhagavā pana ‘‘ettakaṃ kālaṃ sakkassa okāsaṃ na karissāmī’’ti paricchinditvā kālavasena phalasamāpattiṃ samāpanno. Sakko ‘‘na dāni me satthā okāsaṃ karotī’’ti gandhakuṭiṃ padakkhiṇaṃ katvā rathaṃ nivattetvā devalokābhimukhaṃ pesesi. Gandhakuṭipariveṇaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0268"/> rathasaddena samohitaṃ pañcaṅgikatūriyaṃ viya ahosi. Bhagavato yathāparicchinnakālavasena samāpattito vuṭṭhitassa rathasaddeneva paṭhamāvajjanaṃ uppajji, tasmā evamāha.</p> <hi rend="bold">Salaḷāgārake</hi> <hi rend="bold">Aññatarena samādhinā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Bhūjati ca nāmā</hi> <hi rend="bold">bhūjatī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Paricārikā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Nemisaddena tamhā samādhimhā vuṭṭhito</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0706"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0268"/> <p rend="subhead">Gopakavatthuvaṇṇanā</p>
352. "I am long-expecting, venerable sir" means, "For a long time I, starting from a long time ago, am desirous of seeing," is the meaning.

"By some, by some things to be done" means, the daughters and sons of the gods are born on the lap, the women who attend to the feet are born in the bed, the deities who are makers of ornaments and decorations for them are born surrounding the bed, those who do service are born in the inner mansion; for these, there is no such thing as dividing in half.

Those who are born on the borderland, being unable to decide "These are yours, these are mine," make a division in half, they ask Śakra, the king of the gods.

He says, "Whose celestial mansion is nearer, those are theirs."

If on the other hand they are at the same level, he says, "Whose celestial mansion they are looking at, those are theirs."

If [the disputed object] does not look at either, he makes it belong to himself, for the sake of ending the quarrel of both.

Things to be done are just things to be done, such as playing, etc.

Having considered those kinds of things to be done, he said, "By some, by some things to be done."

"In a Sāla tree house" means in a perfumed house made of Sāla wood.

"By some meditative absorption" means, then it seems that the Blessed One, having known that Śakra's knowledge had not yet reached maturity, wanting to make an opportunity, sat in the enjoyment of the fruition of meditative absorption (phalasamāpatti).

Not knowing that, this one said, "By some meditative absorption."

"And Bhūjatī by name" means, "Bhūjatī" is her name.

"A female attendant" means a goddess who attends to the feet.

It seems she has attained two fruits, therefore she has no delight in the world of the gods.

Always coming to the Blessed One's presence, placing her joined palms on her head, she stands venerating the Blessed One.

"By the sound of the wheel rim, he arose from that meditative absorption" means, "One who has attained is hearing a sound," that is not to be said, surely the Blessed One to Śakra, the lord of gods,

"Moreover, I arose from that meditative absorption by the sound of the wheel rim of the venerable one," he says.

Let the sound of the wheel rim be, one who has attained, namely, in inner attainment, does not hear the sound even of the conch shell being blown at the root of the ear, or even of the crash of thunder.

The Blessed One, on the other hand, having decided, "I will not make an opportunity for Śakra for this long," attained the fruition of meditative absorption according to the time.

Śakra, "Now the teacher is not making an opportunity for me," having circumambulated the perfumed hut, turning the chariot back, sent it facing the world of the gods.

The enclosure of the perfumed hut, with the sound of the chariot, was like a five-piece orchestra being played.

For the Blessed One, having arisen from meditative absorption according to the time that had been determined, the first attention arose just by the sound of the chariot, therefore he said this.

In a Sāla tree house, by some meditative absorption, and Bhūjatī by name, Bhūjatī, a female attendant, by the sound of the wheel rim, he arose from that meditative absorption.

The narration of the story of the cowherd.
353
<p n="353" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">353</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> ‘‘Idheva, bhante, kapilavatthusmiṃ gopikā nāma sakyadhītā ahosi buddhe pasannā dhamme pasannā saṅghe pasannā sīlesu paripūrakārinī. Sā itthittaṃ <note>itthicittaṃ (syā.)</note> virājetvā purisattaṃ <note>purisacittaṃ (syā.)</note> bhāvetvā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā sugatiṃ saggaṃ lokaṃ upapannā. Devānaṃ tāvatiṃsānaṃ sahabyataṃ amhākaṃ puttattaṃ ajjhupagatā. Tatrapi naṃ evaṃ jānanti – ‘gopako devaputto, gopako devaputto’ti. Aññepi, bhante, tayo bhikkhū bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ caritvā hīnaṃ gandhabbakāyaṃ upapannā. Te pañcahi kāmaguṇehi samappitā samaṅgībhūtā paricārayamānā amhākaṃ upaṭṭhānaṃ āgacchanti amhākaṃ pāricariyaṃ. Te amhākaṃ upaṭṭhānaṃ āgate amhākaṃ pāricariyaṃ gopako devaputto paṭicodesi <pb ed="P" n="2.0272"/> – ‘kutomukhā nāma tumhe <pb ed="T" n="2.0306"/>, mārisā, tassa bhagavato dhammaṃ assuttha <note>āyuhittha (syā.)</note> – ahañhi nāma itthikā samānā buddhe pasannā dhamme pasannā saṅghe pasannā sīlesu paripūrakārinī itthittaṃ virājetvā purisattaṃ bhāvetvā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā sugatiṃ saggaṃ lokaṃ upapannā, devānaṃ tāvatiṃsānaṃ sahabyataṃ sakkassa devānamindassa puttattaṃ ajjhupagatā. Idhāpi maṃ evaṃ jānanti ‘‘gopako devaputto gopako devaputto’ti. Tumhe pana, mārisā, bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ caritvā hīnaṃ gandhabbakāyaṃ upapannā. Duddiṭṭharūpaṃ vata, bho, addasāma, ye mayaṃ addasāma <pb ed="M" n="2.0217"/> sahadhammike hīnaṃ gandhabbakāyaṃ upapanne’ti. Tesaṃ, bhante, gopakena devaputtena paṭicoditānaṃ dve <pb ed="V" n="2.0200"/> devā diṭṭheva dhamme satiṃ paṭilabhiṃsu kāyaṃ brahmapurohitaṃ, eko pana devo kāme ajjhāvasi.</p>
<p n="353" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">353</hi><hi rend="dot">.
</hi> "Here, Lord (bhante), in Kapilavatthu, there was a Sakyan girl called Gopikā who had faith in the Buddha, faith in the Dharma (dhamma), faith in the Saṅgha, and who observed the precepts scrupulously (sīlesu paripūrakārinī).
Having abandoned her womanhood (itthittaṃ) and developed her manhood (purisattaṃ), at the break-up of the body (kāyassa bhedā), after death (paraṃ maraṇā), she re-appeared in a good destination (sugatiṃ), a heavenly world (saggaṃ lokaṃ), among the company of the gods of the Thirty-three (devānaṃ tāvatiṃsānaṃ sahabyataṃ), having undertaken the state of being our son (amhākaṃ puttattaṃ ajjhupagatā).
Even there they know him thus: ‘Gopaka the devas’ son (gopako devaputto), Gopaka the devas’ son (gopako devaputto)’.
Also, Lord (bhante), there were three monks (bhikkhū) who, having observed the holy life (brahmacariyaṃ caritvā) under the Blessed Lord (bhagavati), had been reborn in the inferior condition of gandhabbas (hīnaṃ gandhabbakāyaṃ upapannā).
They, endowed with the five objects of sensual pleasure (pañcahi kāmaguṇehi samappitā samaṅgībhūtā), attending and serving (paricārayamānā), come to our presence (amhākaṃ upaṭṭhānaṃ āgacchanti), our attendance (amhākaṃ pāricariyaṃ).
When they had come to our presence (te amhākaṃ upaṭṭhānaṃ āgate), our attendance (amhākaṃ pāricariyaṃ), Gopaka the devas’ son (gopako devaputto) rebuked (paṭicodesi) them – ‘From what source (kutomukhā nāma) are you, venerable sirs (mārisā), that you have not heard (assuttha) the Dharma (dhammaṃ) of that Blessed Lord (tassa bhagavato)?’ For I, being a woman (itthikā samānā), having faith in the Buddha (buddhe pasannā), faith in the Dharma (dhamme pasannā), faith in the Saṅgha (saṅghe pasannā), observing the precepts scrupulously (sīlesu paripūrakārinī), having abandoned my womanhood (itthittaṃ virājetvā) and developed my manhood (purisattaṃ bhāvetvā), at the break-up of the body (kāyassa bhedā), after death (paraṃ maraṇā), I re-appeared in a good destination (sugatiṃ), a heavenly world (saggaṃ lokaṃ), among the company of the gods of the Thirty-three (devānaṃ tāvatiṃsānaṃ sahabyataṃ), having undertaken the state of being Sakka’s, the deva-lord Indra’s (sakkassa devānamindassa) son (puttattaṃ ajjhupagatā).
Even here they know me thus: ‘Gopaka the devas’ son (gopako devaputto), Gopaka the devas’ son (gopako devaputto)’.
But you (tumhe pana), venerable sirs (mārisā), having observed the holy life (brahmacariyaṃ caritvā) under the Blessed Lord (bhagavati), have been reborn in the inferior condition of gandhabbas (hīnaṃ gandhabbakāyaṃ upapannā).
A sorry sight (duddiṭṭharūpaṃ vata), indeed, have we seen (addasāma), that we have seen (ye mayaṃ addasāma) our fellows in the Dhamma (sahadhammike) reborn in the inferior condition of gandhabbas (hīnaṃ gandhabbakāyaṃ upapanne)’.
Being thus rebuked (tesaṃ, bhante, gopakena devaputtena paṭicoditānaṃ) by Gopaka the devas’ son (gopakena devaputtena), two (dve) of those devas (devā) immediately (diṭṭheva) developed mindfulness (dhamme satiṃ paṭilabhiṃsu), the Brahma-retinue body (kāyaṃ brahmapurohitaṃ), but one deva (eko pana devo) remained addicted to sensual pleasures (kāme ajjhāvasi).
</p>
<p n="353" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">353</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Sīlesu paripūrakārinī</hi>ti pañcasu sīlesu paripūrakārinī. <hi rend="bold">Itthittaṃ virājetvā</hi>ti itthittaṃ nāma alaṃ, na hi itthitte ṭhatvā cakkavattisiriṃ, na sakkamārabrahmasiriyo paccanubhavituṃ, na paccekabodhiṃ, na sammāsambodhiṃ gantuṃ sakkāti evaṃ itthittaṃ virājeti nāma. Mahantamidaṃ purisattaṃ nāma seṭṭhaṃ uttamaṃ, ettha ṭhatvā sakkā etā sampattiyo pāpuṇitunti evaṃ <pb ed="M" n="2.0299"/> pana purisattaṃ bhāveti nāma. Sāpi evamakāsi. Tena vuttaṃ – ‘‘itthittaṃ virājetvā purisattaṃ bhāvetvā’’ti. <hi rend="bold">Hīnaṃ gandhabbakāya</hi>nti hīnaṃ lāmakaṃ gandhabbanikāyaṃ. Kasmā pana te parisuddhasīlā tattha uppannāti? Pubbanikantiyā. Pubbepi kira nesaṃ etadeva vasitaṭṭhānaṃ, tasmā nikantivasena tattha uppannā. <hi rend="bold">Upaṭṭhāna</hi>nti upaṭṭhānasālaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Pāricariya</hi>nti paricaraṇabhāvaṃ. Gītavāditehi amhe paricarissāmāti āgacchanti.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Paṭicodesī</hi>ti sāresi. So kira te disvā ‘‘ime devaputtā ativiya virocenti ativaṇṇavanto, kiṃ nu kho kammaṃ katvā āgatā’’ti āvajjanto ‘‘bhikkhū ahesu’’nti addasa. Tato ‘‘bhikkhū hontu, sīlesu paripūrakārino’’ti upadhārento ‘‘paripūrakārino’’ti addasa. ‘‘Paripūrakārino hontu, añño guṇo atthi <pb ed="P" n="3.0707"/> natthī’’ti upadhārento ‘‘jhānalābhino’’ti addasa. ‘‘Jhānalābhino hontu, kuhiṃ vāsikā’’ti upadhārento ‘‘mayhaṃva kulūpakā’’ti addasa. Parisuddhasīlā nāma chasu devalokesu yatthicchanti, tattha nibbattanti. Ime pana uparidevaloke ca na nibbattā. Jhānalābhino nāma brahmaloke nibbattanti, ime ca brahmaloke na nibbattā. Ahaṃ pana etesaṃ ovāde ṭhatvā devalokasāmikassa sakkassa devānamindassa pallaṅke putto hutvā nibbatto, ime hīne gandhabbakāye nibbattā. Aṭṭhivedhapuggalā nāmete vaṭṭetvā vaṭṭetvā gāḷhaṃ vijjhitabbāti cintetvā <hi rend="bold">kutomukhā nāmā</hi>tiādīhi vacanehi paṭicodesi.</p> <hi rend="bold">Paṭicodesī</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0707"/> <hi rend="bold">kutomukhā nāmā</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Tattha <hi rend="bold">kutomukhā</hi>ti bhagavati abhimukhe dhammaṃ desente tumhe kutomukhā kiṃ aññā vihitā ito cito ca olokayamānā udāhu niddāyamānā? <hi rend="bold">Duddiṭṭharūpa</hi>nti duddiṭṭhasabhāvaṃ daṭṭhuṃ ayuttaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0269"/>. <hi rend="bold">Sahadhammike</hi>ti ekassa satthu sāsane samāciṇṇadhamme katapuññe. <hi rend="bold">Tesaṃ bhante</hi>ti tesaṃ gopakena devaputtena evaṃ vatvā puna ‘‘aho tumhe nillajjā ahirikā’’tiādīhi vacanehi paṭicoditānaṃ dve devā diṭṭheva dhamme satiṃ paṭilabhiṃsu.</p> <hi rend="bold">kutomukhā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Duddiṭṭharūpa</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0269"/> <hi rend="bold">Sahadhammike</hi> <hi rend="bold">Tesaṃ bhante</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Kāyaṃ brahmapurohita</hi>nti te kira cintayiṃsu – ‘‘naṭehi nāma naccantehi gāyantehi vādentehi āgantvā dāyo nāma labhitabbo assa, ayaṃ pana <pb ed="M" n="2.0300"/> amhākaṃ diṭṭhakālato paṭṭhāya pakkhittaloṇaṃ uddhanaṃ viya taṭataṭāyateva, kiṃ nu kho ida’’nti āvajjantā attano samaṇabhāvaṃ parisuddhasīlataṃ jhānalābhitaṃ tasseva kulūpakabhāvañca disvā ‘‘parisuddhasīlā nāma chasu devalokesu yathārucite ṭhāne nibbattanti, jhānalābhino brahmaloke. Mayaṃ uparidevalokepi brahmalokepi nibbattituṃ nāsakkhimha. Amhākaṃ ovāde ṭhatvā ayaṃ itthikā upari nibbattā, mayaṃ bhikkhū samānā bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ caritvā hīne gandhabbakāye nibbattā. Tena no ayaṃ evaṃ niggaṇhātī’’ti ñatvā tassa kathaṃ suṇantāyeva tesu dve janā paṭhamajjhānasatiṃ paṭilabhitvā jhānaṃ pādakaṃ katvā saṅkhāre sammasantā anāgāmiphaleyeva patiṭṭhahiṃsu. Atha nesaṃ so paritto <pb ed="P" n="3.0708"/> kāmāvacarattabhāvo dhāretuṃ nāsakkhi. Tasmā tāvadeva cavitvā brahmapurohitesu nibbattā. So ca nesaṃ kāyo tattha ṭhitānaṃyeva nibbatto. Tena vuttaṃ – ‘‘tesaṃ, bhante, gopakena devaputtena paṭicoditānaṃ dve devā diṭṭheva dhamme satiṃ paṭilabhiṃsu kāyaṃ brahmapurohita’’nti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Kāyaṃ brahmapurohita</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0300"/> <pb ed="P" n="3.0708"/> <p rend="bodytext">Tattha <hi rend="bold">diṭṭheva dhamme</hi>ti tasmiññeva attabhāve jhānasatiṃ paṭilabhiṃsu. Tattheva ṭhatvā cutā pana kāyaṃ brahmapurohitaṃ brahmapurohitasarīraṃ paṭilabhiṃsūti evamattho daṭṭhabbo. <hi rend="bold">Eko pana devo</hi>ti eko devaputto nikantiṃ chindituṃ asakkonto kāme ajjhavasi, tattheva āvāsiko ahosi.</p> <hi rend="bold">diṭṭheva dhamme</hi> <hi rend="bold">Eko pana devo</hi>
353. “*Śīlesu paripūrakārinī*” means fulfilling the precepts by means of five types of morality.

“*Itthittaṃ virājetvā*” means that womanhood is sufficient. For, having taken the state of being a woman, it is not possible to experience the glory of a universal monarch, nor the glory of Śakra, Māra, or Brahmā, nor to attain the state of a solitary buddha, nor to attain perfect and complete enlightenment. Thus, it is said that one should reject womanhood.

This great state of manhood is considered the best and highest. Having taken this state, it is possible to attain these accomplishments. Thus, one cultivates manhood.

She also did thus.

Therefore, it was said, "Having rejected womanhood and cultivated manhood."

"*Hīnaṃ gandhabbakāya*" means the inferior, wretched host of gandharvas.

Why were they reborn there with pure morality? Because of past inclination.

Previously, that was their dwelling place. Therefore, they were reborn there due to their inclination.

"*Upaṭṭhāna*" means the service hall.

"*Pāricariya*" means the state of attendance.

They come thinking, "We will attend with songs and music."

"*Paṭicodesī*" means he rebuked.

He saw them and thought, "These gods shine exceedingly and are very beautiful. What merit have they performed to have come here?" Reflecting thus, he saw, "They were monks."

Then, thinking, "May they be monks, fulfilling the precepts," he saw, "They are fulfillers of the precepts."

"May they be fulfillers of the precepts. Is there any other quality or not?" Thinking thus, he saw, "They have attained absorption."

"May they have attained absorption. Where do they dwell?" Thinking thus, he saw, "They are attendants to me."

Those with pure morality are reborn in any of the six heavens as they please.

But these were not reborn in the upper heavens.

Those who have attained absorption are reborn in the Brahmā world. These were not reborn in the Brahmā world.

I, however, having stood in their advice, was reborn as a son on the throne of Śakra, lord of the gods, the owner of the heaven realm, while these were reborn in the inferior host of gandharvas.

Thinking that the eight types of individuals worthy of offerings should be tightly pierced by whirling and whirling them around, he rebuked them with words beginning "*kutomukhā nāmā*."

*Paṭicodesī*.

*Kutomukhā nāmā*. There, "*kutomukhā*" means, when the Blessed One is teaching the Dharma face-to-face, where are your faces turned? What else is arranged? Are you looking here and there, or are you drowsing? "*Duddiṭṭharūpa*" means unsuitable to see due to a badly seen nature.

*Sahadhammike* means those who have accumulated Dharma and performed meritorious deeds in the teaching of one teacher.

"*Tesaṃ bhante*" means, having said thus to them, the god Gopaka, and again, having rebuked them with words beginning "Alas, you are shameless and without conscience!", two gods regained mindfulness in that very Dharma.

*Kutomukhā*. *Duddiṭṭharūpa*.

*Sahadhammike*. *Tesaṃ bhante*. "*Kāyaṃ brahmapurohita*" means they thought, "A gift should be obtained by coming with actors, dancers, singers, and musicians. But this one, from the time we saw him, is just crackling like salt thrown into a hot pan. What is this?" Turning their minds, seeing their own state of being a renunciate, their pure morality, their attainment of absorption, and also their state of being attendants to him, they thought, "Those with pure morality are reborn in the six heavens in a place as they please. Those who have attained absorption [are reborn] in the Brahmā world.

We were not able to be reborn in the upper heaven or in the Brahmā world.

Having stood in our advice, this woman was reborn above. We, being monks, having practiced the holy life in the presence of the Blessed One, were reborn in the inferior host of gandharvas.

Knowing that "This one thus rebukes us," and just hearing his words, two of them, having regained mindfulness of the first dhyāna, having made absorption their foundation, and properly examining conditioned phenomena, were established in the fruit of non-returning.

Then, that small amount of desire-realm existence was not able to be maintained by them.

Therefore, having passed away from there, they were reborn among the Brahmā's retinue.

And their bodies were reborn there while standing.

Therefore, it was said, "Having been rebuked by Gopaka, son of the gods, two gods regained mindfulness in that very Dharma, and [attained] the Brahmā's retinue."
kāyaṃ brahmapurohita

tattha diṭṭheva dhamme ti tasmiññeva attabhāve jhānasatiṃ paṭilabhiṃsu.
tattheva ṭhatvā cutā pana kāyaṃ brahmapurohitaṃ brahmapurohitasarīraṃ paṭilabhiṃsūti evamattho daṭṭhabbo.
eko pana devo ti eko devaputto nikantiṃ chindituṃ asakkonto kāme ajjhavasi, tattheva āvāsiko ahosi.

diṭṭheva dhamme eko pana devo

354. "I was an *upāsikā* (female lay follower) of the one with the eye (Buddha),
My name was 'Gopikā';
I was fully convinced in the Buddha and the Dharma,
With a clear mind, I attended to the *saṃgha*.

"I am the son of Śakra, possessing great power,
Because of that Buddha's good Dharma;
I am greatly radiant, reborn in the Tavatimsa heaven,
Even here, they know me as 'Gopaka.'

"Then I saw monks, seen before,
Dwelling in a *gandharva* body;
These are those disciples of Gotama,
Who formerly were human beings.

"We attended with food and drink,
Having approached their feet in our own dwelling;
From what direction have these venerable ones come,
[Who] received the Buddha's *dharmas*."

"The Dharma should be known individually,
Well-expounded [and] realized by the one with the eye (Buddha);
Indeed, I, worshiping you,
Having heard the well-spoken words of the noble ones."

"I am the son of Śakra, possessing great power,
I am greatly radiant, reborn in the Tavatimsa heaven;
But you, worshiping the best,
Having practiced the unsurpassed *brahmacarya* (celibate life)."

"Venerable ones, you have attained a low body,
May your attainment not be conforming;
Indeed, we have seen a poorly formed appearance,
[For those] possessing the same *dharmas*, [but] attained to a low body."

"Venerable ones, having attained a *gandharva* body,
You come to serve the gods;
Living in my house,
See this distinction."

"Having been a woman, today I am a male deity,
Endowed with divine pleasures";
They, urged by a disciple of Gotama,
Experienced agitation, having met Gopaka.

"Come, let us exert ourselves,
Lest we be servants of others";
Those two began exertion,
Remembering the teachings of Gotama.

"Having delighted their minds right here,
They saw the danger in sensual pleasures;
Those bonds of sensual attachment,
Difficult to overcome, connected with evil."

Like an elephant, having cut through the ropes,
pī.
), santāni guṇāni (syā.
)</note>
deve tāvatiṃse atikkamiṃsu;
saindā devā sapajāpatikā,
sabbe sudhammāya sabhāyupaviṭṭhā.

nisinnānaṃ abhikkamiṃsu,

vīrā virāgā virajaṃ karontā;
te disvā saṃvegamakāsi vāsavo,
devābhibhū devagaṇassa majjhe.

te hīnakāyūpapannā,

deve tāvatiṃse abhikkamanti’;
saṃvegajātassa vaco nisamma,
so gopako vāsavamajjhabhāsi.

buddho janindatthi manussaloke,
kāmābhibhū sakyamunīti ñāyati;
tasseva te puttā satiyā vihīnā,
coditā mayā te satimajjhalatthuṃ.

nitiṇṇaoghaṃ vicikicchachinnaṃ,
buddhaṃ namassāma jinaṃ janindaṃ’.

<p n="354" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">354</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Saṅghañcupaṭṭhāsi</hi>nti saṅghañca upaṭṭhāsiṃ.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Sudhammatāyā</hi>ti dhammassa sundarabhāvena. <hi rend="bold">Tidivūpapanno</hi>ti tidive tidasapure upapanno. <hi rend="bold">Gandhabbakāyūpagate vasīne</hi>ti gandhabbakāyaṃ āvāsiko hutvā upagate. <hi rend="bold">Ye ca mayaṃ pubbe manussabhūtā</hi>ti ye pubbe manussabhūtā mayaṃ annena pānena upaṭṭhahimhāti iminā saddhiṃ yojetvā attho veditabbo.</p> <hi rend="bold">Sudhammatāyā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Tidivūpapanno</hi> <hi rend="bold">Gandhabbakāyūpagate vasīne</hi> <hi rend="bold">Ye ca mayaṃ pubbe manussabhūtā</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Pādūpasaṅgayhā</hi>ti <pb ed="V" n="2.0270"/> pāde upasaṅgayha pādadhovanapādamakkhanānuppadānena pūjetvā ceva vanditvā ca. <hi rend="bold">Sake nivesane</hi>ti attano ghare. Imassāpi padassa upaṭṭhahimhāti imināva sambandho.</p> <hi rend="bold">Pādūpasaṅgayhā</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0270"/> <hi rend="bold">Sake nivesane</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Paccattaṃ veditabbo</hi>ti attanāva veditabbo. <hi rend="bold">Ariyāna subhāsitānī</hi>ti tumhehi vuccamānāni buddhānaṃ bhagavantānaṃ subhāsitāni.</p> <hi rend="bold">Paccattaṃ veditabbo</hi> <hi <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Etādisī dhammappakāsanetthā</hi>ti ettha sāsane evarūpā dhammappakāsanā, yāya sāvakā etehi guṇehi samannāgatā honti. <hi rend="bold">Na</hi><pb ed="P" Idaṃ vuttaṃ hoti – yaṃ tava dhammaṃ jānitvā tesaṃ tiṇṇaṃ janānaṃ te dve visesagū amhākaṃ passantānaṃyeva kāyaṃ brahmapurohitaṃ adhigantvā maggaphalavisesaṃ ajjhagaṃsu, mayampi tassa dhammassa pattiyā āgatamhāsi mārisāti. <hi rend="bold">Āgatamhase</hi>ti sampattamha. <hi rend="bold">Katāvakāsā bhagavatā, pañhaṃ pucchemu mārisā</hi>ti sace no bhagavā okāsaṃ karoti, atha bhagavatā katāvakāsā hutvā pañhaṃ, mārisa, puccheyyāmāti attho.</p> <hi rend="bold">Yaṃ te dhamma</hi> <hi rend="bold">Ajjhagaṃsu te</hi> <hi rend="bold">Kāyaṃ brahmapurohita</hi> <hi rend="bold">Āgatamhase</hi> <hi rend="bold">Katāvakāsā bhagavatā, pañhaṃ pucchemu mārisā</hi> <p rend="subhead">Maghamāṇavavatthu</p>
354. *saṅghañcupaṭṭhāsi* means *saṅghañca upaṭṭhāsiṃ*.

*sudhammatāyā* means due to the beauty of the Dharma (*dhammassa sundarabhāvena*).
*tidivūpapanno* means he arose in heaven (*tidive tidasapure upapanno*).
*gandhabbakāyūpagate vasīne* means he lived in the Gandharva body (*gandhabbakāyaṃ āvāsiko hutvā upagate*).
*ye ca mayaṃ pubbe manussabhūtā* means those who were formerly human beings (*ye pubbe manussabhūtā mayaṃ annena pānena upaṭṭhahimhāti iminā saddhiṃ yojetvā attho veditabbo*); the meaning should be understood by connecting this with "we attended with food and drink."

*sudhammatāyā*, *tidivūpapanno*, *gandhabbakāyūpagate vasīne*, *ye ca mayaṃ pubbe manussabhūtā*.
*pādūpasaṅgayhā* means having approached the feet (*pāde upasaṅgayha pādadhovanapādamakkhanānuppadānena pūjetvā ceva vanditvā ca*), having venerated and worshiped by not giving the washing of the feet and massaging of the feet.
*sake nivesane* means in his own house (*attano ghare*).
*imassāpi padassa upaṭṭhahimhāti imināva sambandho* means association with this, [since] I attended to even this verse.

*pādūpasaṅgayhā*, *sake nivesane*.
*paccattaṃ veditabbo* means it should be known individually (*attanāva veditabbo*).
*ariyāna subhāsitānī* means well-spoken words of the noble ones (*tumhehi vuccamānāni buddhānaṃ bhagavantānaṃ subhāsitāni*), which are spoken by you, well-spoken words of the Buddhas, the Blessed Ones.

*paccattaṃ veditabbo*, *ariyāna subhāsitānī*.
*tumhe pana seṭṭhamupāsamānā* means you, venerating the best (*uttamaṃ buddhaṃ bhagavantaṃ upāsamānā anuttare buddhasāsane vā*) either in the unsurpassed Buddha's dispensation, venerating the supreme Buddha, the Blessed One.
*brahmacariya* means the best conduct (*seṭṭhacariyaṃ*).
*bhavatūpapattī* means the arising of you (*bhavantānaṃ upapatti*).

*tumhe pana seṭṭhamupāsamānā*, *brahmacariya*, *bhavatūpapattī*.
*agāre vasato mayha* means to me, living in the house (*gharamajjhe vasantassa mayhaṃ*).

*agāre vasato mayha*.
*svajjā* means that one today (*so ajja*).
*gotamasāvakenā* means here, the cowherd is said to be a disciple of Gotama (*idha gopako gotamasāvakoti vutto*).
*sameccā* means having come together (*samāgantvā*).

*svajjā*, *gotamasāvakenā*, *sameccā*.
*handa viyāyāma byāyāmā* means come, let us strive, let us exert (*handa uyyamāma byāyamāma*).
*mā no mayaṃ parapessā ahumhā* means *no* is merely a particle; may we not be messengers of another (*mā mayaṃ parassa pesanakārakāva ahumhāti attho*).
*gotamasāsanānī* means here, the first jhāna itself, which is penetrated by nature, is said to be the teachings of Gotama (*idha pakatiyā paṭividdhaṃ paṭhamajjhānameva gotamasāsanānīti vuttaṃ, taṃ anussaraṃ anussaritvāti attho*), remembering that, having recollected.

*handa viyāyāma byāyāmā*, *mā no mayaṃ parapessā ahumhā*, *no gotamasāsanānī*.
*cittāni virājayitvā* means having detached the minds from the five strands of sense pleasure (*pañcakāmaguṇikacittāni virājayitvā*).
*kāmesu ādīnava* means the danger in sensual pleasures (*vikkhambhanavasena paṭhamajjhānena kāmesu ādīnavaṃ addasaṃsu, samucchedavasena tatiyamaggena*) by way of temporary suppression, they saw the danger in sensual pleasures by means of the first jhāna, and by way of complete destruction, by means of the third path.
*kāmasaṃyojanabandhanānī* means bonds of sensual attachment (*kāmasaññojanāni ca kāmabandhanāni ca*), both sensual attachments and sensual bonds.
*pāpimayogānī* means the bonds of the Evil One, Māra (*pāpimato mārassa yogabhūtāni, bandhanabhūtānīti attho*), which are connected to, are bonds of the Evil One, Māra.
*duraccayānī* means difficult to overcome (*duratikkamāni*).
*saindā devā sapajāpatikā* means the gods together with Indra (*indaṃ jeṭṭhakaṃ katvā upaviṭṭhā saindā pajāpatiṃ devarājānaṃ jeṭṭhakaṃ katvā upaviṭṭhā sapajāpatikā*), having made Indra their chief, sat down, and the gods together with Pajāpati, having made Pajāpati, the king of gods, their chief, sat down.
*sabhāyupaviṭṭhā* means having sat down in the assembly (*sabhāyaṃ upaviṭṭhā, nisinnāti attho*), having sat down, is the meaning.

*cittāni virājayitvā*, *kāmesu ādīnava*, *kāmasaṃyojanabandhanānī*, *pāpimayogānī*, *duraccayānī*, *saindā devā sapajāpatikā*, *sabhāyupaviṭṭhā*.
*vīrā* means heroes (*sūrā*).
*virāgā* means devoid of passion (*vītarāgā*).
*virajaṃ karontā* means making it stainless (*virajaṃ anāgāmimaggaṃ karontā uppādentā*), producing the stainless path of the non-returner.
*nāgova sannāni guṇānī* means like a serpent shedding its skin (*kāmasaññojanabandhanāni chetvā deve tāvatiṃse atikkamiṃsu*), having cut off the bonds of sensual attachment, they transcended the Tāvatiṃsa gods.
*saṃvegajātassā* means to Sakka, who is born of agitation (*jātasaṃvegassa sakkassa*).

*vīrā*, *virāgā*, *virajaṃ karontā*, *nāgova sannāni guṇānī*, *saṃvegajātassā*.
*kāmābhibhū*.
0271"/> Overcoming the two kinds of desires.
Lacking mindfulness means without mindfulness of *dhyāna*.

Overcoming desires.
0271"/> Lacking mindfulness. *Tiṇṇaṃ tesa* means among those three people.
*Āvasinettha eko* means there, in the inferior body, only one dwelling was born.
*Sambodhipathānusārino* means following the path to complete Awakening.
*Devepi hīḷentī* means in the two heavens.
0302"/> Ridiculing, humiliating, being endowed with attentiveness because of the meditative absorption of approach and application, scattering his own foot dust on the heads of the gods, having flown up into the sky, they went.

*Tiṇṇaṃ tesa*, *āvasinettha eko*, *sambodhipathānusārino*, *devepi hīḷentī*.
0302"/> *Etādisī dhammappakāsanetthā* means in this teaching, such a Dhamma teaching, by which disciples are endowed with these qualities, exists.
Not.
0710"/> *Tattha kiṃ kaṅkhati koci sāvako* means why there, among those disciples, does any single disciple doubt the Buddhas, etc., or the state of being oriented in all directions, saying, "He dwells without clinging, without grasping in all directions"?
Now, while speaking of the Blessed One's virtues, he said, "We salute the Buddha, the conqueror, the lord of beings, who has crossed the flood, who has cut off uncertainty."
There, *vicikicchachinna* means cutting off uncertainty.
*Janinda* means the highest of all the world.

*Etādisī dhammappakāsanetthā*, not.
0710"/> *Tattha kiṃ kaṅkhati koci sāvako*, *vicikicchachinna*, *janinda*. *Yaṃ te dhamma* means whatever your Dharma.
*Ajjhagaṃsu te* means those sons of gods attained.
*Kāyaṃ brahmapurohita* means the body of the *brahmapurohita* right before us seeing [them].
This was said: "Having known your Dharma, those two who were especially skilled among those three people, having attained the body of the *brahmapurohita* right before us seeing [them], attained a special fruit of the path. We too have come for the sake of attainment of that Dharma, oh venerable ones."
*Āgatamhase* means we have arrived.
*Katāvakāsā bhagavatā, pañhaṃ pucchemu mārisā* means "If the Blessed One does not make an opportunity for us, then having had the opportunity made by the Blessed One, we would ask a question, venerable ones," is the meaning.

*Yaṃ te dhamma*, *ajjhagaṃsu te*, *kāyaṃ brahmapurohita*, *āgatamhase*, *katāvakāsā bhagavatā, pañhaṃ pucchemu mārisā*. The Maghamāṇavavatthu.
355
<p n="355" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">355</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Atha kho bhagavato etadahosi – ‘‘dīgharattaṃ visuddho kho ayaṃ yakkho <note>sakko (sī. syā. pī.)</note>, yaṃ kiñci maṃ pañhaṃ pucchissati, sabbaṃ taṃ atthasañhitaṃyeva pucchissati, no anatthasañhitaṃ. Yañcassāhaṃ puṭṭho byākarissāmi, taṃ khippameva ājānissatī’’ti.</p>
Then the Lord thought: ‘Sakka has lived a pure life for a long time. Whatever questions he may ask will be to the point ( *arthasañhita*) and not frivolous (*anarthasañhita*). And whatever I answer when asked, he will quickly understand.’
<p n="355" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">355</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Dīgharattaṃ visuddho kho ayaṃ yakkho</hi>ti cirakālato pabhuti visuddho. Kīva cirakālato? Anuppanne buddhe magadharaṭṭhe macalagāmake maghamāṇavakālato paṭṭhāya. Tadā kiresa ekadivasaṃ kālasseva vuṭṭhāya gāmamajjhe manussānaṃ gāmakammakaraṇaṭṭhānaṃ gantvā attano ṭhitaṭṭhānaṃ pādanteneva paṃsukacavaraṃ apanetvā ramaṇīyamakāsi, añño āgantvā tattha aṭṭhāsi. So tāvatakeneva satiṃ paṭilabhitvā majjhe gāmassa khalamaṇḍalamattaṃ ṭhānaṃ sodhetvā vālukaṃ okiritvā <pb ed="V" n="2.0272"/> dārūni āharitvā sītakāle aggiṃ karoti, daharā ca mahallakā ca āgantvā tattha nisīdanti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Athassa <pb ed="M" n="2.0303"/> ekadivasaṃ etadahosi – ‘‘mayaṃ nagaraṃ gantvā rājarājamahāmattādayo passāma, imesupi candimasūriyesu ‘cando nāma devaputto, sūriyo nāma devaputto’ti <pb ed="P" n="3.0711"/> vadanti. Kiṃ nu kho katvā ete etā sampattiyo adhigatā’’ti? Tato ‘‘nāññaṃ kiñci, puññakammameva katvā’’ti cintetvā ‘‘mayāpi evaṃvidhasampattidāyakaṃ puññakammameva kattabba’’nti cintesi.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0303"/> <pb ed="P" n="3.0711"/> <p rend="bodytext">So kālasseva vuṭṭhāya yāguṃ pivitvā vāsipharasukudālamusalahattho catumahāpathaṃ gantvā musalena pāsāṇe uccāletvā pavaṭṭeti, yānānaṃ akkhapaṭighātarukkhe harati, visamaṃ samaṃ karoti, catumahāpathe sālaṃ karoti, pokkharaṇiṃ khaṇati, setuṃ bandhati, evaṃ divasaṃ kammaṃ katvā atthaṅgate sūriye gharaṃ eti. Taṃ añño pucchi – ‘‘bho, magha, tvaṃ pātova nikkhamitvā sāyaṃ araññato esi, kiṃ kammaṃ karosī’’ti? Puññakammaṃ karomi. Saggagāmimaggaṃ sodhemīti. Kimidaṃ, bho, puññaṃ nāmāti? Tvaṃ na jānāsīti? Āma, na jānāmīti. Nagaraṃ gatakāle diṭṭhapubbā te rājarājamahāmattādayoti? Āma, diṭṭhapubbāti. Puññakammaṃ katvā tehi taṃ ṭhānaṃ laddhaṃ, ahampi evaṃvidhasampattidāyakaṃ kammaṃ karomi. ‘‘Cando nāma devaputto, sūriyo nāma devaputto’’ti sutapubbaṃ tayāti? Āma sutapubbanti. Etassa saggassa gamanamaggaṃ ahaṃ sodhemīti. Idaṃ pana puññakammaṃ kiṃ taveva vaṭṭati, aññassa na vaṭṭatīti? Na kassacetaṃ vāritanti. Yadi evaṃ sve araññaṃ gamanakāle mayhampi saddaṃ dehīti. Punadivase taṃ gahetvā gato, evaṃ tasmiṃ gāme tettiṃsa manussā taruṇavayā sabbe tasseva anuvattakā ahesuṃ. Te ekacchandā hutvā puññakammāni karontā vicaranti. Yaṃ disaṃ gacchanti, maggaṃ samaṃ karontā ekadivaseneva karonti, pokkharaṇiṃ khaṇantā, sālaṃ karontā, setuṃ bandhantā ekadivaseneva niṭṭhāpenti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Atha nesaṃ gāmabhojako cintesi – ‘‘ahaṃ pubbe etesu suraṃ pivantesu pāṇaghātādīni karontesu ca kahāpaṇādivasena ceva daṇḍabalivasena ca dhanaṃ labhāmi. Idāni etesaṃ puññakaraṇakālato paṭṭhāya ettako āyo natthi, handa ne rājakule paribhindāmī’’ti rājānaṃ upasaṅkamitvā <pb ed="P" n="3.0712"/> core, mahārāja, passāmīti. Kuhiṃ, tātāti? Mayhaṃ gāmeti. Kiṃ corā nāma, tātāti? Rājāparādhikā devāti. Kiṃ jātikāti? Gahapatijātikā devāti. Gahapatikā kiṃ karissanti, tayā <pb ed="M" n="2.0304"/> jānamānena kasmā mayhaṃ na kathitanti? Bhayena, mahārāja, na <pb ed="V" n="2.0273"/> kathemi, idāni mā mayhaṃ dosaṃ kareyyāthāti. Atha rājā ‘‘ayaṃ mayhaṃ mahāravaṃ ravatī’’ti saddahitvā ‘‘tena hi gaccha, tvameva ne ānehī’’ti balaṃ datvā pesesi. So gantvā divasaṃ araññe kammaṃ katvā sāyamāsaṃ bhuñjitvā gāmamajjhe nisīditvā ‘‘sve kiṃ kammaṃ karissāma, kiṃ maggaṃ samaṃ karoma, pokkharaṇiṃ khaṇāma, setuṃ bandhāmā’’ti mantayamāneyeva te parivāretvā ‘‘mā phandittha, rañño āṇā’’ti bandhitvā pāyāsi. Atha kho nesaṃ itthiyo ‘‘sāmikā kira vo ‘rājāparādhikā corā’ti bandhitvā niyyantī’’ti sutvā ‘‘aticirena kūṭā ete ‘puññakammaṃ karomā’ti divase divase araññeva acchanti, sabbakammantā parihīnā, gehe na kiñci vaḍḍhati, suṭṭhu baddhā suṭṭhu gahitā’’ti vadiṃsu.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0712"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0304"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0273"/> <p rend="bodytext">Gāmabhojakopi te netvā rañño dassesi. Rājā anupaparikkhitvāyeva ‘‘hatthinā maddāpethā’’ti āha. Tesu nīyamānesu magho itare āha – ‘‘bho, sakkhissatha mama vacanaṃ kātu’’nti? Tava vacanaṃ karontāyevamha imaṃ bhayaṃ pattā, evaṃ santepi tava vacanaṃ karoma, bhaṇa bho, kiṃ karomāti? Ettha bho vaṭṭe carantānaṃ nāma nibaddhaṃ etaṃ, kiṃ pana tumhe corāti? Na coramhāti. Imassa lokassa saccakiriyā nāma avassayo, tasmā sabbepi ‘‘yadi amhe corā, hatthī maddatu, atha na corā, mā maddatū’’ti saccakiriyaṃ karothāti. Te tathā akaṃsu. Hatthī upagantumpi na sakkoti, viravanto palāyati, hatthiṃ tuttatomaraṅkusehi koṭṭentāpi upanetuṃ na sakkonti. ‘‘Hatthiṃ upanetuṃ na sakkomā’’ti rañño ārocesuṃ. Tena hi upari kaṭena paṭicchādetvā maddāpethāti. Upari kaṭe dinne diguṇaravaṃ viravanto palāyati.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Rājā sutvā pesuññakārakaṃ pakkosāpetvā āha – ‘‘tāta, hatthī maddituṃ na icchatī’’ti? Āma, deva, jeṭṭhakamāṇavo mantaṃ jānāti, mantasseva ayamānubhāvoti. Rājā taṃ pakkosāpetvā ‘‘manto kira te atthī’’ti pucchi? Natthi, deva, mayhaṃ <pb ed="P" n="3.0713"/> manto, saccakiriyaṃ pana mayaṃ karimha – ‘‘yadi amhe rañño corā, maddatu, atha na corā, mā maddatū’’ti, saccakiriyāya no esa ānubhāvoti. Kiṃ pana, tāta, tumhe kammaṃ karothāti? Amhe, deva, maggaṃ samaṃ karoma, catumahāpathe sālaṃ karoma <pb ed="M" n="2.0305"/>, pokkharaṇiṃ khaṇāma, setuṃ bandhāma, evarūpāni puññakammāni karontā vicarimhāti.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0713"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0305"/> <p rend="bodytext">Ayaṃ tumhe kimatthaṃ pisuṇesīti? Amhākaṃ pamattakāle idañcidañca labhati, appamattakāle taṃ natthi, etena kāraṇenāti. Tāta, ayaṃ hatthī nāma tiracchāno, sopi tumhākaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0274"/> guṇe jānāti. Ahaṃ manusso hutvāpi na jānāmi, tumhākaṃ vasanagāmaṃ tumhākaṃyeva puna aharaṇīyaṃ katvā demi, ayampi hatthī tumhākaṃyeva hotu, pesuññakārakopi tumhākaṃyeva dāso hotu. Ito paṭṭhāya mayhampi puññakammaṃ karothāti dhanaṃ datvā vissajjesi. Te dhanaṃ gahetvā vārena vārena hatthiṃ āruyha gacchantā mantayanti ‘‘bho puññakammaṃ nāma anāgatabhavatthāya kariyati, amhākaṃ pana antoudake pupphitaṃ nīluppalaṃ viya imasmiññeva attabhāve vipākaṃ deti. Idāni atirekaṃ puññaṃ karissāmā’’ti, kiṃ karomāti? Catumahāpathe thāvaraṃ katvā mahājanassa vissamanasālaṃ karoma, itthīhi pana saddhiṃ apattikaṃ katvā karissāma, amhesu hi ‘‘corā’’ti gahetvā nīyamānesu itthīnaṃ ekāpi cintāmattakampi akatvā ‘‘subaddhā sugahitā’’ti uṭṭhahiṃsu, tasmā tāsaṃ pattiṃ na dassāmāti. Te attano gehāni gantvā hatthino tettiṃsapiṇḍaṃ denti, tettiṃsa tiṇamuṭṭhiyo āharanti, taṃ sabbaṃ hatthissa kucchipūraṃ jātaṃ. Te araññaṃ pavisitvā rukkhe chindanti, chinnaṃ chinnaṃ hatthī kaḍḍhitvā sakaṭapathe ṭhapesi. Te rukkhe tacchetvā sālāya kammaṃ ārabhiṃsu.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0274"/> <p rend="bodytext">Maghassa gehe sujātā, sudhammā, cittā, nandāti catasso bhariyāyo ahesuṃ. Sudhammā vaḍḍhakiṃ pucchati – ‘‘tāta, ime sahāyā kālasseva gantvā sāyaṃ enti, kiṃ kammaṃ karontī’’ti? ‘‘Sālaṃ karonti, ammā’’ti. ‘‘Tāta, mayhampi sālāya pattiṃ katvā dehī’’ti. ‘‘Itthīhi apattikaṃ <pb ed="P" n="3.0714"/> karomā’’ti ete vadantīti. Sā vaḍḍhakissa aṭṭha kahāpaṇe datvā ‘‘tāta, yena kenaci upāyena mayhaṃ pattikaṃ karohī’’ti āha. So ‘‘sādhu ammā’’ti vatvā puretaraṃ vāsipharasuṃ gahetvā gāmamajjhe ṭhatvā ‘‘kiṃ bho ajja imasmimpi kāle na nikkhamathā’’ti uccāsaddaṃ katvā ‘‘sabbe maggaṃ āruḷhā’’ti ñatvā ‘‘gacchatha tāva tumhe, mayhaṃ papañco atthī’’ti te <pb ed="M" n="2.0306"/> purato katvā aññaṃ maggaṃ āruyha kaṇṇikūpagaṃ rukkhaṃ chinditvā tacchetvā maṭṭhaṃ katvā āharitvā sudhammāya gehe ṭhapesi – ‘‘mayā dehīti vuttadivase nīharitvā dadeyyāsī’’ti.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0714"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0306"/> <p rend="bodytext">Atha niṭṭhite dabbasambhārakamme bhūmikammato paṭṭhāya cayabandhanathambhussāpana saṅghāṭayojana kaṇṇikamañcabandhanesu katesu so vaḍḍhakī kaṇṇikamañce nisīditvā catūhi disāhi gopānasiyo ukkhipitvā ‘‘bho ekaṃ pamuṭṭhaṃ atthī’’ti āha. Kiṃ bho pamuṭṭhaṃ, sabbameva tvaṃ pamussasīti. Imā bho gopānasiyo kattha patiṭṭhahissantīti? Kaṇṇikā nāma laddhuṃ vaṭṭatīti. Kuhiṃ bho idāni sakkā laddhunti? Kulānaṃ gehe sakkā laddhunti. Āhiṇḍantā pucchathāti. Te antogāmaṃ pavisitvā pucchitvā sudhammāya gharadvāre ‘‘imasmiṃ ghare kaṇṇikā atthī’’ti <pb ed="V" n="2.0275"/> āhaṃsu. Sā ‘‘atthī’’ti āha. Handa mūlaṃ gaṇhāhīti. Mūlaṃ na gaṇhāmi, sace mama pattiṃ karotha, dassāmīti. Etha bho mātugāmassa pattiṃ na karoma, araññaṃ gantvā rukkhaṃ chindissāmāti nikkhamiṃsu.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0275"/> <p rend="bodytext">Tato vaḍḍhakī ‘‘kiṃ na laddhā, tāta, kaṇṇikā’’ti pucchi. Te tamatthaṃ ārocayiṃsu. Vaḍḍhakī kaṇṇikamañce nisinnova ākāsaṃ ulloketvā ‘‘bho ajja nakkhattaṃ sundaraṃ, idaṃ aññaṃ saṃvaccharaṃ atikkamitvā sakkā laddhuṃ, tumhehi ca dukkhena ābhatā dabbasambhārā, te sakalasaṃvaccharena imasmiññeva ṭhāne pūtikā bhavissanti. Devaloke nibbattakāle tassāpi ekasmiṃ koṇe sālā hotu, āharatha na’’nti āha. Sāpi yāva te na puna āgacchanti, tāva kaṇṇikāya heṭṭhimatale ‘‘ayaṃ sālā <hi rend="bold">sudhammā</hi> nāmā’’ti akkharāni chindāpetvā ahatena vatthena veṭhetvā ṭhapesi. Kammikā āgantvā – ‘‘āhara, re kaṇṇikaṃ <pb ed="P" n="3.0715"/>, yaṃ hotu taṃ hotu. Tuyhampi pattiṃ karissāmā’’ti āhaṃsu. Sā nīharitvā ‘‘tātā, yāva aṭṭha vā soḷasa vā gopānasiyo na ārohanti, tāva imaṃ vatthaṃ mā nibbeṭhayitthā’’ti vatvā adāsi. Te ‘‘sādhū’’ti sampaṭicchitvā gahetvā gopānasiyo āropetvāva vatthaṃ nibbeṭhesuṃ.</p> <hi rend="bold">sudhammā</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0715"/> <p rend="bodytext">Eko mahāgāmikamanusso uddhaṃ ullokento akkharāni disvā ‘‘kiṃ, bho, ida’’nti akkharaññuṃ manussaṃ pakkosāpetvā dassesi. So ‘‘sudhammā <pb ed="M" n="2.0307"/> nāma ayaṃ sālā’’ti āha. ‘‘Haratha, bho, mayaṃ ādito paṭṭhāya sālaṃ katvā nāmamattampi na labhāma, esā ratanamattena kaṇṇikarukkhena sālaṃ attano nāmena kāretī’’ti viravanti. Vaḍḍhakī tesaṃ viravantānaṃyeva gopānasiyo pavesetvā āṇiṃ datvā sālākammaṃ niṭṭhāpesi.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0307"/> <p rend="bodytext">Sālaṃ tidhā vibhajiṃsu, ekasmiṃ koṭṭhāse issarānaṃ vasanaṭṭhānaṃ akaṃsu, ekasmiṃ duggatānaṃ, ekasmiṃ gilānānaṃ. Tettiṃsa janā tettiṃsa phalakāni paññapetvā hatthissa saññaṃ adaṃsu – ‘‘āgantuko āgantvā yassa atthate phalake nisīdati, taṃ gahetvā phalakasāmikasseva gehe patiṭṭhapehi. Tassa pādaparikammapiṭṭhiparikammakhādanīyabhojanīyasayanāni sabbāni phalakasāmikasseva bhāro bhavissatī’’ti. Hatthī āgatāgataṃ gahetvā phalakasāmikassa gehaṃ neti, so tassa taṃ divasaṃ kattabbaṃ karoti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Maghamāṇavo sālato avidūre ṭhāne koviḷārarukkhaṃ ropāpesi, mūle cassa pāsāṇaphalakaṃ atthari. <hi rend="bold">Nandā</hi> nāmassa bhariyā avidūre pokkharaṇiṃ khaṇāpesi, <hi rend="bold">cittā</hi> mālāvacche <pb ed="V" n="2.0276"/> ropāpesi, sabbajeṭṭhikā pana ādāsaṃ gahetvā attabhāvaṃ maṇḍayamānāva vicarati. Magho taṃ āha – ‘‘bhadde, sudhammā, sālāya pattikā jātā, nandā pokkharaṇiṃ khaṇāpesi, cittā mālāvacche ropāpesi. Tava pana puññakammaṃ nāma natthi, ekaṃ puññaṃ karohi, bhadde’’ti sā ‘‘tvaṃ kassa kāraṇā karosi, nanu tayā kataṃ mayhamevā’’ti vatvā attabhāvamaṇḍanameva anuyuñjati.</p> <hi rend="bold">Nandā</hi> <hi rend="bold">cittā</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0276"/> <p rend="bodytext">Magho yāvatāyukaṃ ṭhatvā tato cavitvā tāvatiṃsabhavane sakko hutvā nibbatti, tepi tettiṃsa gāmikamanussā <pb ed="P" n="3.0716"/> kālaṅkatvā tettiṃsa devaputtā hutvā tasseva santike nibbattā. Sakkassa vejayanto nāma pāsādo satta yojanasatāni uggacchi, dhajo tīṇi yojanasatāni uggacchi, koviḷārarukkhassa nissandena samantā tiyojanasataparimaṇḍalo pañcadasayojanapariṇāhakkhandho pāricchattako nibbatti, pāsāṇaphalakassa <pb ed="M" n="2.0308"/> nissandena pāricchattakamūle saṭṭhiyojanikā paṇḍukambalasilā nibbatti. Sudhammāya kaṇṇikarukkhassa nissandena tiyojanasatikā sudhammā devasabhā nibbatti. Nandāya pokkharaṇiyā nissandena paññāsayojanā nandā nāma pokkharaṇī nibbatti. Cittāya mālāvacchavatthunissandena saṭṭhiyojanikaṃ cittalatāvanaṃ nāma uyyānaṃ nibbatti.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0716"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0308"/> <p rend="bodytext">Sakko devarājā sudhammāya devasabhāya yojanike suvaṇṇapallaṅke nisinno tiyojanike setacchatte dhāriyamāne tehi devaputtehi tāhi devakaññāhi aḍḍhatiyāhi nāṭakakoṭīhi dvīsu devalokesu devatāhi ca parivārito mahāsampattiṃ olokento tā tisso itthiyo disvā ‘‘imā tāva paññāyanti, sujātā kuhi’’nti olokento ‘‘ayaṃ mama vacanaṃ akatvā girikandarāya bakasakuṇikā hutvā nibbattā’’ti disvā devalokato otaritvā tassā santikaṃ gato. Sā disvāva sañjānitvā adhomukhā jātā. ‘‘Bāle, idāni kiṃ sīsaṃ na ukkhipasi? Tvaṃ mama vacanaṃ akatvā attabhāvameva maṇḍayamānā vītināmesi. Sudhammāya ca nandāya ca cittāya ca mahāsampatti nibbattā, ehi amhākaṃ sampattiṃ passā’’ti devalokaṃ netvā nandāya pokkharaṇiyā pakkhipitvā pallaṅke nisīdi.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Nāṭakitthiyo ‘‘kuhiṃ gatattha, mahārājā’’ti pucchiṃsu. So anārocetukāmopi tāhi nippīḷiyamāno ‘‘sujātāya santika’’nti āha. Kuhiṃ nibbattā, mahārājāti? Kandarapādeti. Idāni kuhinti? Nandāpokkharaṇiyaṃ me vissaṭṭhāti. Etha, bho, amhākaṃ ayyaṃ passāmāti sabbā tattha agamaṃsu. Sā pubbe sabbajeṭṭhikā hutvā tā avamaññittha. Idāni <pb ed="V" n="2.0277"/> tāpi taṃ disvā – ‘‘passatha, bho amhākaṃ ayyāya mukhaṃ kakkaṭakavijjhanasūlasadisa’’ntiādīni vadantiyo keḷiṃ akaṃsu. Sā ativiya aṭṭiyamānā sakkaṃ devarājānaṃ āha – ‘‘mahārāja, imāni <pb ed="P" n="3.0717"/> suvaṇṇarajatamaṇivimānāni vā nandāpokkharaṇī vā mayhaṃ kiṃ karissati, jātibhūmiyeva mahārāja sattānaṃ sukhā, maṃ tattheva kandarapāde vissajjehī’’ti. Sakko taṃ tattha vissajjetvā ‘‘mama vacanaṃ karissasī’’ti āha. Karissāmi, mahārājāti. Pañca sīlāni gahetvā akhaṇḍāni katvā rakkha, katipāhena taṃ etāsaṃ jeṭṭhikaṃ karissāmīti. Sā tathā akāsi.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0277"/> <pb ed="P" n="3.0717"/> <p rend="bodytext">Sakko <pb ed="M" n="2.0309"/> katipāhassa accayena ‘‘sakkā nu kho sīlaṃ rakkhitu’’nti gantvā maccharūpena uttānako hutvā tassā purato udakapiṭṭhe osarati, sā ‘‘matamacchako bhavissatī’’ti gantvā sīse aggahesi. Maccho naṅguṭṭhaṃ cālesi. Sā ‘‘jīvati maññe’’ti udake vissajjesi. Sakko ākāse ṭhatvā ‘‘sādhu, sādhu, rakkhasi sikkhāpadaṃ, evaṃ taṃ rakkhamānaṃ katipāheneva nāṭakānaṃ jeṭṭhikaṃ karissāmī’’ti āha. Tassāpi pañca vassasatāni āyu ahosi. Ekadivasampi udarapūraṃ nālatthaṃ, sukkhitvā parisukkhitvā milāyamānāpi sīlaṃ akhaṇḍetvā kālaṅkatvā bārāṇasiyaṃ kumbhakāragehe nibbatti.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0309"/> <p rend="bodytext">Sakko ‘‘kuhiṃ nibbattā’’ti olokento disvā ‘‘tato idha ānetuṃ na sakkā, jīvitavuttimassā dassāmī’’ti suvaṇṇaeḷālukānaṃ yānakaṃ pūretvā majjhe gāmassa mahallakavesena nisīditvā ‘‘eḷālukāni gaṇhathā’’ti ukkuṭṭhimakāsi. Samantā gāmavāsikā āgantvā ‘‘dehi, tātā’’ti āhaṃsu. Ahaṃ sīlarakkhakānaṃ demi, tumhe sīlaṃ rakkhathāti. Tāta mayaṃ sīlaṃ nāma kīdisantipi na jānāma, mūlena dehīti. ‘‘Sīlarakkhakānaṃyeva dammī’’ti āha. ‘‘Etha, re kosi ayaṃ eḷālukamahallako’’ti sabbe nivattiṃsu.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Sā dārikā pucchi – ‘‘amma, tumhe eḷālukatthāya gatā tucchahatthāva āgatā’’ti. Kosi, amma, eḷālukamahallako ‘‘ahaṃ sīlarakkhakānaṃ dammī’’ti vadati, nūnimassa dārikā sīlaṃ khāditvā vattanti, mayaṃ sīlameva na jānāmāti. Sā ‘‘mayhaṃ ānītaṃ bhavissatī’’ti gantvā ‘‘eḷālukaṃ, tāta, dehī’’ti āha. ‘‘Tvaṃ sīlāni rakkhasi ammā’’ti? ‘‘Āma, tāta rakkhāmī’’ti. Idaṃ mayā tuyhameva ābhatanti gehadvāre yānena saddhiṃ ṭhapetvā pakkāmi. Sāpi yāvajīvaṃ sīlaṃ rakkhitvā cavitvā vepacittiasurassa dhītā hutvā nibbatti. Sīlanissandena <pb ed="V" n="2.0278"/> pāsādikā ahosi. So <pb ed="P" n="3.0718"/> ‘‘dhītuvivāhamaṅgalaṃ karissāmī’’ti asure sannipātesi.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0278"/> <pb ed="P" n="3.0718"/> <p rend="bodytext">Sakko ‘‘kuhiṃ nibbattā’’ti olokento ‘‘asurabhavane nibbattā, ajjassā vivāhamaṅgalaṃ karissantī’’ti disvā ‘‘idāni yaṃkiñci katvā ānetabbā mayā’’ti asuravaṇṇaṃ nimminitvā gantvā asurānaṃ antare aṭṭhāsi. ‘‘Tava <pb ed="M" n="2.0310"/> sāmikaṃ vadehī’’ti tassā hatthe pitā pupphadāmaṃ adāsi ‘‘yaṃ icchasi, tassūpari khipāhī’’ti. Sā olokentī sakkaṃ disvā pubbasannivāsena sañjātasinehā ‘‘ayaṃ me sāmiko’’ti tassūpari dāmaṃ khipi. So taṃ bāhāya gahetvā ākāse uppati, tasmiṃ khaṇe asurā sañjāniṃsu. Te ‘‘gaṇhatha, gaṇhatha, jarasakkaṃ, veriko amhākaṃ, na mayaṃ etassa dārikaṃ dassāmā’’ti anubandhiṃsu. Vepacitti pucchi ‘‘kenāhaṭā’’ti? ‘‘Jarasakkena mahārājā’’ti. ‘‘Avasesesu ayameva seṭṭho, apethā’’ti āha. Sakko naṃ netvā aḍḍhatiyakoṭināṭakānaṃ jeṭṭhikaṭṭhāne ṭhapesi. Sā sakkaṃ varaṃ yāci – ‘‘mahārāja, mayhaṃ imasmiṃ devaloke mātā vā pitā vā bhātā vā bhaginī vā natthi, yattha yattha gacchasi, tattha tattha maṃ gahetvāva gaccha mahārājā’’ti. Sakko ‘‘sādhū’’ti paṭiññaṃ adāsi.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0310"/> <p rend="bodytext">Evaṃ macalagāmake maghamāṇavakālato paṭṭhāya visuddhabhāvamassa sampassanto bhagavā ‘‘dīgharattaṃ visuddho kho ayaṃ yakkho’’ti āha. <hi rend="bold">Atthasañhita</hi>nti atthanissitaṃ kāraṇanissitaṃ.</p> <hi rend="bold">Atthasañhita</hi> <p rend="subhead">Pañhaveyyākaraṇavaṇṇanā</p>
355. “For a long time this *yakṣa* was pure,” thus, he was pure from a long time.

How long ago? From the time of Magha, the youth, in Macala village in the Magadha country, when the Buddha had not yet arisen.

Then one day, having arisen early in the morning, Kiresa went to the place in the middle of the village where the people were doing village work, and having removed the dust and rubbish from his own standing place with his foot, he made it delightful. Another came and stood there.

Having regained mindfulness with just that much, having cleaned a spot in the middle of the village the size of a threshing floor, having sprinkled sand, having brought wood, and having made a fire in the cold season, both young and old came and sat there.

Then one day he thought, "Let us go to the city and see the kings, great ministers, and so forth. Even among these moons and suns, they say, 'The moon is called a *devaputra*, the sun is called a *devaputra*.'"

Having thought, "Having done what, have these obtained these riches?" then, having reflected, "Without doing anything else, having done only meritorious deeds," he thought, "I also must do only meritorious deeds that give such riches."

He, having arisen early in the morning, having drunk rice gruel, with a razor, hoe, spade, and pestle in his hand, having gone to the great crossroads, having raised the stones with the pestle, rolls them away, removes the trees that obstruct the axles of the vehicles, makes the uneven places even, builds a hall at the great crossroads, digs a pond, builds a bridge, and thus, having done work during the day, he goes home when the sun has set.

Another asked him, "Hey, Magha, you go out early in the morning and come from the forest in the evening. What work do you do?" "I do meritorious work.

I am clearing the path to the heavenly village."

"What is this meritorious deed?" "Don't you know?" "Yes, I don't know."

"At the time when you went to the city, were those kings, great ministers, and so forth seen before?" "Yes, they were seen before."

"Having done meritorious work, they obtained that place. I also do work that gives such riches.

Have you heard before, 'The moon is called a *devaputra*, the sun is called a *devaputra*’?" "Yes, I have heard it before."

"I am clearing this path of going to heaven."

"Does this meritorious work only suit you, and does it not suit another?" "This is not forbidden to anyone."

"If so, tomorrow at the time of going to the forest, give me a call also."

The next day, having taken him, he went. Thus, in that village, thirty-three men of youthful age all became followers of him.

Having become of one mind, they wander about doing meritorious deeds.

Whichever direction they go, making the path even, they do it in one day. Digging a pond, building a hall, building a bridge, they complete it in one day.

Then the village headman thought, "Formerly, when these were drinking liquor and doing things like killing living beings, I obtained wealth by way of fines and by way of the power of punishment.

Now, from the time of their doing meritorious deeds, there is not so much income. Come, let me divide them in the royal family." Having approached the king, "I see thieves, Great King.

"Where, daddy?" "In my village."

"What are the thieves called, daddy?" "They are those who have offended the king, *devas*."

"What is their caste?" "They are householder caste, *devas*."

"What will the householders do? Why was it not told to me, knowing that?" "Out of fear, Great King, I did not tell it. Now, may you not be angry with me."

Then the king, believing "This one is making a great noise at me," having given an army, sent him, "Then go, you yourself bring them."

Having gone, having done work in the forest during the day, having eaten evening meals, having sat down in the middle of the village, "Tomorrow what work shall we do? Shall we make the path even? Shall we dig a pond? Shall we build a bridge?" Even as they were consulting, having surrounded them, "Do not tremble, it is the king's command," having bound them, he went away.

Then, having heard, "Your husbands, it seems, having been bound as 'thieves who have offended the king,' are being led away," their women said, "For a long time these fools have been staying only in the forest day after day, saying 'Let us do meritorious work.' All their work is ruined. Nothing increases in the house. They are well bound, they are well seized."
The village headman also brought them and showed them to the king. The king, without examining them, said, "Have them trampled by an elephant!"

As they were being led away, Magha said to the others, "Friends, will you do what I say?" They replied, "Because we did what you said, we have come to this danger. Even so, we will do what you say. Tell us, friend, what shall we do?" "Here in this place," he said, "there is a necessity for an act of truth for those who go around in a circle. Therefore, let all of us perform an act of truth, saying, 'If we are thieves of the king, let the elephant trample us; but if we are not thieves, let it not trample us.'"

They did just that.

The elephant is not able to approach, it cries out and flees. Even when they goad the elephant with goads, spears, and hooks, they are not able to bring it near.

"We are not able to bring the elephant near," they announced to the king.

"Then cover them over with a mat of reeds and have them trampled!"

When the mat of reeds was placed on them, it cried out with twice the sound and fled.

The king, hearing this, summoned the tale-bearer and said, "Friend, the elephant does not want to trample them!" "Yes, your majesty, the eldest youth knows a spell. This is the power of the spell."

The king summoned him and asked, "Is it true that you have a spell?" "No, your majesty, I do not have a spell. But we performed an act of truth, saying, 'If we are thieves of the king, let the elephant trample us; but if we are not thieves, let it not trample us.' This is the power of the act of truth."

"Then, friend, what work do you do?" "We, your majesty, make the road even. We build a hall in the four-way intersection, we dig a pond, we build a bridge. We live by doing such meritorious deeds."

"Why do you tell tales about these people?" "When we are inattentive, they obtain this and that. When we are attentive, they do not obtain that. For this reason."

"Friend, this elephant is an animal, but even it knows your qualities. I, even though I am a human being, did not know. I give back to you your village, which cannot be taken away. Let this elephant also be yours. Let the tale-bearer also be your slave."

"From now on, you also should do meritorious deeds!" Giving them wealth, he dismissed them.

Taking the wealth, as they were going, mounting the elephant in turn, they conferred, "Friends, a meritorious deed is said to be done for the sake of a future existence, but for us, like a blue lotus blooming in the water, it gives its result in this very existence. Now we will do an additional meritorious deed!" "What shall we do?" "We will make a permanent resting hall for the great multitude at the four-way intersection. But we will not do it together with the women, because when we were being led away, having been seized as 'thieves,' not one of the women, even to the slightest extent, had any concern, but exclaimed, 'Well-bound, well-seized!' Therefore, we will not give them a share in that merit."

Going to their own houses, they give the elephant thirty-three balls of food. They bring thirty-three bundles of grass. All of that became a bellyful for the elephant.

Entering the forest, they cut down trees. The elephant, having dragged each piece that was cut down, placed it on the cart road. They trimmed the trees and began the work on the hall.

In Magha's house there were four wives: Sujātā, Sudhammā, Cittā, and Nandā.

Sudhammā asks the carpenter, "Friend, these companions go out early and come back in the evening. What work do they do?" "They are building a hall, mother."

"Friend, give me also a share in the hall!"

"We will not do meritorious deeds with the women!" they say.

She gave eight *kahāpaṇas* to the carpenter, saying, "Friend, by whatever means, make a share for me!"

He, saying, "Good, mother!" took a chisel and an axe earlier and, standing in the middle of the village, exclaimed loudly, "Friends, why do you not come out even at this time?" Knowing that all had gone onto the road, and having gone ahead of them, he climbed onto another road, cut down a *kaṇṇikūpaga* tree, trimmed it, made it smooth, brought it, and placed it in Sudhammā's house, saying, "On the day I tell you to give it, take it out and give it!"
