Then, when the work of the materials was finished, from the earthwork onward, when the piling up, binding, setting up of the pillars, joining of the rafters, and the binding of the *kaṇṇikā* couch were done, that carpenter, sitting on the *kaṇṇikā* couch, having raised up the *gopānasīs* in the four directions, exclaimed, "Friends, there is one thing missing!"

"What is missing, friend? Do you always forget everything?"

"Where will these *gopānasīs* be set?" "It is fitting to obtain a *kaṇṇikā*!"
Where, sir, can it be obtained now? Can it be obtained in the families' houses?
Wandering around, ask.
They, having entered the village, having asked at Sudhammā’s house door, said, “Is there a *kaṇṇikā* in this house?”
She said, “There is.”
“Come, take the price!”
“I will not take the price; if you do a *patti* for me, I will give [it].”
“Come, sir, we will not do a *patti* for a woman. Having gone to the forest, we will cut down a tree,” they left.

Then the carpenter asked, “Has the *kaṇṇikā* not been obtained, dear?”
They reported the matter to him.
The carpenter, having sat on the *kaṇṇikāmañca*, looking up at the sky, said, “Sir, today the asterism is beautiful. This can be obtained having passed another year. And the goods and materials brought by you with difficulty, those will become rotten in this very place in a whole year.
May there be a hall in one corner of it at the time of rebirth in the world of the gods, bring [it], no,” he said.
She too, until they come again, having had the letters “This hall is called *sudhammā*” carved on the lower surface of the *kaṇṇikā*, having wrapped it with a new cloth, placed it.
The workers, having come, [said] – “Bring, hey, the *kaṇṇika*, whatever will be, let that be.
We will also do a *patti* for you,” they said.
She, having taken it out, said, “Dears, until eight or sixteen *gopānasīs* are climbed, do not unwrap this cloth,” having said [this] she gave [it].
They, having agreed, saying “Good!”, having taken [it], having placed the *gopānasīs*, unwrapped the cloth.

One great villager, looking upwards, having seen the letters, having had a man who knew letters summoned, showed [it].
He said, “This hall is called *sudhammā*.”
“Take [it] away, sir, we, from the beginning, having made the hall, do not obtain even the name alone; this one, with a jewel-measure, is having a hall made with a *kaṇṇikarukkha* in her own name,” they cried.
The carpenter, while they were crying, having put the *gopānasīs* in, having given the peg, finished the hall work.

They divided the hall into three parts; in one compartment they made a dwelling place for the lords, in one for the poor, in one for the sick.
Thirty-three people, having prepared thirty-three planks, gave a sign to the elephant – “A newcomer, having come, whomever sits on the prepared plank, having taken him, establish [him] in the house of the plank owner himself.
All the foot-tending, back-tending, foods, eatables, and beds for him will be the burden of the plank owner himself,” [they said].
The elephant, having taken whoever came, leads [him] to the house of the plank owner, he does what is to be done for him that day.

Magha, not far from the hall, had a *koviḷārarukkha* planted in a place, and at its root he spread a stone slab.
His wife, named Nandā, not far away, had a pond dug, Cittā had a flower garden planted, but the eldest of all, taking a mirror, only wanders around beautifying herself.
Magha said to her – “Good lady, a *pattika* has arisen for the *sālā*, Nandā had a pond dug, Cittā had a flower garden planted.
But there is no meritorious action named for you; do one meritorious deed, good lady,” she [said], “For whose sake do you do [this], surely what has been done by you is for me!”, having said [this], she only pursues beautifying herself.

Magha, having stood for as long as his life lasted, then having passed away from there, having become Śakra in the Tāvatiṃsa heaven, was reborn, those thirty-three villagers too, having passed away, having become thirty-three sons of gods, were reborn near him.
For Śakra, a palace named Vejayanta rose seven hundred *yojanas*, the flag rose three hundred *yojanas*, by the sap of the *koviḷārarukkha*, all around, a *parimaṇḍala* of three hundred *yojanas*, a Pāricchattaka arose with a trunk of fifteen *yojanas* in circumference, by the sap of the stone slab, at the base of the Pāricchattaka, a *paṇḍukambalasilā* of sixty *yojanas* arose.
By the *nissanda* (oozing or outflow) of the Kaṇṇikarukkhassa tree, a Sudhammā devasabhā (assembly hall of the gods) of one hundred three *yojanas* came into existence.
By the *nissanda* of the Nandā pond, a Nandā pond of fifty *yojanas* came into existence.
By the *nissanda* of the Cittā mālāvaccha object, a Cittalatāvana grove of sixty *yojanas* came into existence.

Sakka, the king of the gods (devatā), sitting on a golden couch of a *yojana* in the Sudhammā assembly hall of the gods, with a white umbrella of three *yojanas* being held, surrounded by those *devaputtas* (sons of gods), those *devakaññās* (maidens of gods), by two and a half *nāṭakakoṭīs* (hundreds of thousands of dramas), and by the divinities in the two *devalokas* (worlds of gods), looking at the great wealth, saw those three women and, looking [to see] "these are visible, where is Sujātā?", seeing "this one, not doing my word, having become a bakasakuṇikā (heron bird) in a mountain cave, has come into existence," having seen [this], having descended from the world of gods, went to her presence.
She, having seen and recognized [him], became downcast.
"Girl, why now do you not lift up your head? You, not doing my word, are merely adorning yourself, and you pass [the time]."
A great wealth came into existence for Sudhammā, Nandā, and Cittā. "Come, see our wealth," having led [her] to the world of gods, having thrown [her] into the Nandā pond, he sat on the couch.

The *nāṭakitthiyos* (actresses) asked, "Where has the great king gone?"
He, not wanting to reveal it, being pressed by them, said "To Sujātā's presence."
"Where has she come into existence, great king?" "In the Kandara foot (mountain cave)."
"Where now?" "She has been released into the Nandā pond."
"Come, friends, let us see our lady," all went there.
She, having been the eldest of all before, despised them.
Now, those also, having seen her, saying - "See, friends, the face of our lady is like a *kakkaṭakavijjhanasūla* (crab-piercing stake)" - they made sport with such things.
She, exceedingly oppressed, said to Sakka, the king of the gods, "Great king, what will these golden, silver, and jeweled *vimānas* (mansions) or the Nandā pond do for me? The happiness of beings is in their birthplace, great king. Release me there, in the Kandara foot."
Sakka, having released her there, said, "Will you do my word?"
"I will do [it], great king."
"Having taken the five precepts, having made [them] unbroken, protect [them]. In a few days, I will make you the eldest of these."
She did so.

Sakka, after the passing of a few days, going [to see] "Is it possible to protect the precepts?", having become a fish, lying on its back, was floating in the water before her. She, thinking "It will be a dead fish," went and grasped [it by] the head.
The fish moved its tail.
She, thinking "It is alive, I think," released [it] in the water.
Sakka, standing in the sky, said, "Good, good, you protect the *sikkhāpada* (training precepts). Thus, while you are protecting [it], in a few days I will make you the eldest of the actresses."
For her also, there was a life span of five hundred years.
Without obtaining even one day's full belly, having become thin, exceedingly thin, and fading away, without breaking the precepts, having made [an end of her life], she came into existence in a potter's house in Bārāṇasī.

Sakka, looking [to see] "Where has she come into existence?", having seen [her], [thought] "It is not possible to bring [her] from there to here. I will give her a means of livelihood." Having filled a cart with golden *eḷālukas* (a kind of sweet potato), sitting in the middle of the village in the guise of an old man, he made an announcement, "Take *eḷālukas*!"
Villagers from all around came and said, "Give [them to us], old man."
"I give to those who protect the precepts. You protect the precepts."
"Old man, we do not even know what the precepts are. Give [them to us] for a price."
"I give only to those who protect the precepts," he said.
"Come, who is this *eḷālukamahallaka* (sweet potato old man)?" all turned back.

The girl asked, "Mother, you went for the sweet potatoes, [but] you have come back with empty hands."
Kosi, amma, that old man of Eḷāluka says, "I will give [this] to the guardians of morality." Surely, his daughters are behaving having eaten morality, we do not know morality itself.
She, thinking, "It will be brought to me," went and said, "Eḷāluka, father, give [it to me]."
"Do you protect the precepts, mother?" "Yes, father, I protect [them]."
This, having placed [it] near the door of the house with the carriage, saying, "It has been brought by me for you," he departed.
She too, having protected morality as long as she lived, having passed away, was reborn as a daughter of Vepacitti, lord of the *asuras*.
Through the stream of morality, she became pleasing.
He, "I will perform a daughter's wedding celebration," assembled the *asuras*.
The *Śakka*, looking around, "Where has she been reborn?" [and seeing] "She has been reborn in the *asura* mansion, today they will perform her wedding celebration," seeing [this], "Now, having done something, she must be brought by me," having created an *asura* form, having gone, he stood among the *asuras*.
"Tell [me] your husband," her father gave a garland of flowers into her hand, "[Throw it] upon whomever you wish."
She, looking around, seeing Śakka, with affection born of a past dwelling together, "This is my husband," threw the garland upon him.
He, having taken her by the arm, rose into the sky. At that moment, the *asuras* recognized [him].
They, "Seize [him], seize [him], old Śakka, he is our enemy, we will not give the girl to him," pursued [him].
Vepacitti asked, "By whom was she brought?" "By old Śakka, great king."
"Among those remaining, this one is the best, depart!" he said.
Śakka, having led her away, established her in the chief place of thirty-five million actors.
She asked a boon of Śakka, "Great king, in this divine world, I have no mother or father or brother or sister. Wherever you go, there, there, having taken me, go, great king."
Śakka gave his promise, "Good."
Thus, in Macalagamaka, from the time of the youth Magha onwards, the Blessed One, seeing his state of purity, said, "For a long time, this *yaksha* has been pure."
*Atthasañhita* means dependent on meaning, dependent on cause.
*Atthasañhita*: Explanation of questions and grammar.
356
<p n="356" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">356</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Atha <pb ed="M" n="2.0220"/> kho bhagavā sakkaṃ devānamindaṃ gāthāya ajjhabhāsi –</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Puccha vāsava maṃ pañhaṃ, yaṃ kiñci manasicchasi;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Tassa tasseva pañhassa, ahaṃ antaṃ karomi te’’ti.</p> <p rend="centre">Paṭhamabhāṇavāro niṭṭhito.</p>
356. Then, indeed, the Blessed One addressed Śakra, the lord of the gods, in verse:

"Ask, Vāsava, me a question, whatever you wish in your mind;
For each and every question, I will make an end for you."

The first recitation section is finished.
357
<p n="357" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">357</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Katāvakāso <pb ed="P" n="2.0276"/> sakko devānamindo bhagavatā imaṃ bhagavantaṃ <note>devānamindo bhagavantaṃ imaṃ (sī. pī.)</note> paṭhamaṃ pañhaṃ apucchi –</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Kiṃ saṃyojanā nu kho, mārisa, devā manussā asurā nāgā gandhabbā ye <pb ed="T" n="2.0311"/> caññe santi puthukāyā, te – ‘averā adaṇḍā asapattā abyāpajjā viharemu averino’ti iti ca nesaṃ hoti, atha ca pana saverā sadaṇḍā sasapattā sabyāpajjā viharanti saverino’’ti? Itthaṃ sakko devānamindo bhagavantaṃ pañhaṃ <note>imaṃ paṭhamaṃ pañhaṃ (sī. pī.)</note> apucchi. Tassa bhagavā pañhaṃ puṭṭho byākāsi –</p> <pb ed="T" n="2.0311"/> <note>imaṃ paṭhamaṃ pañhaṃ (sī. pī.)</note> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Issāmacchariyasaṃyojanā kho, devānaminda, devā manussā asurā nāgā gandhabbā ye caññe <pb ed="V" n="2.0204"/> santi puthukāyā, te – ‘averā adaṇḍā asapattā abyāpajjā viharemu averino’ti iti ca nesaṃ hoti, atha ca pana saverā sadaṇḍā sasapattā sabyāpajjā viharanti saverino’’ti. Itthaṃ bhagavā sakkassa devānamindassa pañhaṃ puṭṭho byākāsi. Attamano sakko devānamindo bhagavato bhāsitaṃ abhinandi anumodi – ‘‘evametaṃ, bhagavā, evametaṃ, sugata. Tiṇṇā mettha kaṅkhā vigatā kathaṃkathā bhagavato pañhaveyyākaraṇaṃ sutvā’’ti.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0204"/>
357. Having made space, Sakka, ruler of the gods (devānāmindo), put his first question (paṭhamaṃ pañhaṃ) to the Blessed One (bhagavatā):

“Lord (mārisa), what are the fetters (saṃyojanā) of gods (devā), humans (manussā), asuras, nāgas, gandharvas, and other beings (caññe) who have diverse bodies (puthukāyā), whereby, although they wish to live without hate (averā), harming (adaṇḍā), hostility (asapattā) or malignity (abyāpajjā), and in peace (averino), they yet live in hate (saverā), harming one another (sadaṇḍā), hostile (sasapattā) and malign (sabyāpajjā)?”

Thus, Sakka, the ruler of the gods (devānamindo), asked the Blessed One (bhagavantaṃ) a question (pañhaṃ).

To him, the Blessed One (bhagavā), being asked (puṭṭho), answered (byākāsi) the question (pañhaṃ):

“The fetters (saṃyojanā), O ruler of the gods (devānaminda), are jealousy (issā) and envy (macchariya), of gods (devā), humans (manussā), asuras, nāgas, gandharvas, and other beings (caññe) who have diverse bodies (puthukāyā), whereby, although they wish to live without hate (averā), harming (adaṇḍā), hostility (asapattā) or malignity (abyāpajjā), and in peace (averino), they yet live in hate (saverā), harming one another (sadaṇḍā), hostile (sasapattā) and malign (sabyāpajjā).”

Thus (itthaṃ), the Blessed One (bhagavā) answered (byākāsi) the question (pañhaṃ) when asked (puṭṭho) by Sakka (sakkassa), the ruler of the gods (devānamindassa).

Delighted (attamano), Sakka (sakko), the ruler of the gods (devānamindo), rejoiced at and approved (abhinandi anumodi) the Blessed One’s (bhagavato) words (bhāsitaṃ): “So it is, Blessed One (bhagavā), so it is, Well-Farer (sugata)! Through the Blessed One’s (bhagavato) answer to the question (pañhaveyyākaraṇaṃ sutvā), my doubt (kaṅkhā) is overcome (vigatā) and uncertainty (kathaṃkathā) is dispelled (tiṇṇā)!”
<p n="357" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">357</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Kiṃ saṃyojanā</hi>ti kiṃ bandhanā, kena bandhanena baddhā hutvā. <hi rend="bold">Puthukāyā</hi>ti bahujanā. <hi rend="bold">Averā</hi>ti appaṭighā. <hi rend="bold">Adaṇḍā</hi>ti āvudhadaṇḍadhanadaṇḍavinimuttā. <hi rend="bold">Asapattā</hi>ti apaccatthikā. <hi rend="bold">Abyāpajjā</hi>ti vigatadomanassā. <hi rend="bold">Viharemu averino</hi>ti aho vata kenaci saddhiṃ averino vihareyyāma, katthaci kopaṃ na uppādetvā accharāya gahitakaṃ jaṅghasahassena saddhiṃ paribhuñjeyyāmāti dānaṃ datvā pūjaṃ katvā ca patthayanti. <hi rend="bold">Iti ca nesaṃ hotī</hi>ti evañca nesaṃ ayaṃ patthanā hoti. <hi rend="bold">Atha ca panā</hi>ti evaṃ patthanāya satipi.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Issāmacchariyasaṃyojanā</hi>ti parasampattikhīyanalakkhaṇā issā, attasampattiyā parehi sādhāraṇabhāvassa <pb ed="V" n="2.0279"/> asahanalakkhaṇaṃ <pb ed="P" n="3.0719"/> macchariyaṃ, issā ca macchariyañca saṃyojanaṃ etesanti issāmacchariyasaṃyojanā. Ayamettha saṅkhepo. Vitthārato pana issāmacchariyāni abhidhamme vuttāneva.</p> <hi rend="bold">Issāmacchariyasaṃyojanā</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0279"/> <pb ed="P" n="3.0719"/> <p rend="bodytext">Āvāsamacchariyena <pb ed="M" n="2.0311"/> panettha yakkho vā peto vā hutvā tasseva āvāsassa saṅkāraṃ sīsena ukkhipitvā vicarati. Kulamacchariyena tasmiṃ kule aññesaṃ dānādīni karonte disvā ‘‘bhinnaṃ vatidaṃ kulaṃ mamā’’ti cintayato lohitampi mukhato uggacchati, kucchivirecanampi hoti, antānipi khaṇḍākhaṇḍāni hutvā nikkhamanti. Lābhamacchariyena saṅghassa vā gaṇassa vā santake lābhe maccharāyitvā puggalikaparibhogena paribhuñjitvā yakkho vā peto vā mahāajagaro vā hutvā nibbattati. Sarīravaṇṇaguṇavaṇṇamacchariyena pana pariyattidhammamacchariyena ca attanova vaṇṇaṃ vaṇṇeti, na paresaṃ vaṇṇaṃ, ‘‘kiṃ vaṇṇo eso’’ti taṃ taṃ dosaṃ vadanto pariyattiñca kassaci kiñci adento dubbaṇṇo ceva eḷamūgo ca hoti.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0311"/> <p rend="bodytext">Apica āvāsamacchariyena lohagehe paccati. Kulamacchariyena appalābho hoti. Lābhamacchariyena gūthaniraye nibbattati. Vaṇṇamacchariyena bhave nibbattassa vaṇṇo nāma na hoti. Dhammamacchariyena kukkuḷaniraye nibbattati. Idaṃ pana issāmacchariyasaṃyojanaṃ sotāpattimaggena pahīyati. Yāva taṃ nappahīyati, tāva devamanussā averatādīni patthayantāpi verādīhi na parimuccantiyeva.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Tiṇṇā mettha kaṅkhā</hi>ti etasmiṃ pañhe mayā tumhākaṃ vacanaṃ sutvā kaṅkhā tiṇṇāti vadati, na maggavasena tiṇṇakaṅkhataṃ dīpeti. <hi rend="bold">Vigatā kathaṃkathā</hi>ti idaṃ kathaṃ, idaṃ kathanti ayampi kathaṃkathā vigatā.</p> <hi rend="bold">Tiṇṇā mettha kaṅkhā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Vigatā kathaṃkathā</hi>
357. *Kiṃ saṃyojanā*ti, what is a fetter? What is a bond? By what bond, having been bound?
*Puthukāyā*ti, [it means] many people.
*Averā*ti, [it means] without hostility.
*Adaṇḍā*ti, [it means] liberated from the rod of weapons and the rod of wealth-punishment.
*Asapattā*ti, [it means] without enemies.
*Abyāpajjā*ti, [it means] devoid of dejection.
*Viharemu averino*ti, "Oh, that we might live without hostility with anyone!" Having given alms, having made offerings, they pray, "May we enjoy a handful of *accharā* together with a thousand shanks, without producing anger anywhere!"
*Iti ca nesaṃ hotī*ti, thus, this aspiration of theirs occurs.
*Atha ca panā*ti, moreover, even with such an aspiration.

*Issāmacchariyasaṃyojanā*ti, envy (*īrṣyā*) is characterized by displeasure at the prosperity of others; stinginess (*mātsarya*) is characterized by intolerance of others sharing one's own prosperity. *Issā ca macchariyañca saṃyojanaṃ etesanti issāmacchariyasaṃyojanā*: Envy and stinginess are fetters; therefore, they are called the "fetter of envy and stinginess."
This is a brief explanation here.
But in detail, envy and stinginess have been described in the *Abhidhamma*.

*Issāmacchariyasaṃyojanā*
Through stinginess regarding dwellings, in this case, a *yakṣa* or a *preta*, having become [one of these], wanders about, sweeping the rubbish of that very dwelling with his head.
Through stinginess regarding family, having seen others in that family performing almsgiving, etc., thinking, "This family of mine is ruined!" blood also comes forth from his mouth, and there is also purging of the stomach; even the intestines come out in pieces.
Through stinginess regarding gain, having been miserly with gain belonging to the *saṅgha* or to a group, having enjoyed it through individual consumption, a *yakṣa* or a *preta* or a great python comes to be reborn.
Through stinginess regarding the beauty of body, complexion, and qualities, on the other hand, and through stinginess regarding the teaching of the scriptures, he praises the beauty of himself alone, not the beauty of others, saying, "What beauty is this?" disparaging that particular fault, and not giving any of the scriptures to anyone, he is both ugly and a mute.

Moreover, through stinginess regarding dwellings, he is cooked in a metal house.
Through stinginess regarding family, there is little gain.
Through stinginess regarding gain, he is reborn in a filth hell.
Through stinginess regarding beauty, for one reborn in existence, there is no such thing as beauty.
Through stinginess regarding the *dharma*, he is reborn in a charcoal hell.
But this fetter of envy and stinginess is abandoned by the path of stream-entry (*sotāpatti-mārga*).
As long as that is not abandoned, even though gods and humans aspire to non-enmity, etc., they are not truly liberated from enmity, etc.

*Tiṇṇā mettha kaṅkhā*ti, in this question, having heard your words, I say, "Doubt is crossed over," but it does not indicate the crossing over of doubt by way of the path (*mārga*).
*Vigatā kathaṃkathā*ti, "How is this? How is that?" This kind of doubt is gone.

*Tiṇṇā mettha kaṅkhā* *vigatā kathaṃkathā*
358
<p n="358" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">358</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Itiha sakko devānamindo bhagavato bhāsitaṃ abhinanditvā <pb ed="P" n="2.0277"/> anumoditvā bhagavantaṃ uttariṃ <note>uttariṃ (sī. syā. pī.)</note> pañhaṃ apucchi –</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Issāmacchariyaṃ pana, mārisa, kiṃnidānaṃ kiṃsamudayaṃ kiṃjātikaṃ kiṃpabhavaṃ; kismiṃ sati issāmacchariyaṃ hoti; kismiṃ asati issāmacchariyaṃ na hotī’’ti? ‘‘Issāmacchariyaṃ kho, devānaminda, piyāppiyanidānaṃ piyāppiyasamudayaṃ piyāppiyajātikaṃ piyāppiyapabhavaṃ; piyāppiye sati issāmacchariyaṃ hoti, piyāppiye asati issāmacchariyaṃ na hotī’’ti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Piyāppiyaṃ <pb ed="M" n="2.0221"/> kho pana, mārisa, kiṃnidānaṃ kiṃsamudayaṃ kiṃjātikaṃ kiṃpabhavaṃ; kismiṃ sati piyāppiyaṃ hoti; kismiṃ asati piyāppiyaṃ na hotī’’ti? ‘‘Piyāppiyaṃ kho, devānaminda, chandanidānaṃ chandasamudayaṃ chandajātikaṃ chandapabhavaṃ; chande sati piyāppiyaṃ hoti; chande asati piyāppiyaṃ na hotī’’ti.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0221"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Chando kho pana, mārisa, kiṃnidāno kiṃsamudayo kiṃjātiko kiṃpabhavo; kismiṃ sati chando <pb ed="T" n="2.0312"/> hoti; kismiṃ asati chando na hotī’’ti? ‘‘Chando kho, devānaminda, vitakkanidāno vitakkasamudayo vitakkajātiko vitakkapabhavo; vitakke sati chando hoti; vitakke asati chando na hotī’’ti.</p> <pb ed="T" n="2.0312"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Vitakko kho pana, mārisa, kiṃnidāno kiṃsamudayo kiṃjātiko kiṃpabhavo; kismiṃ sati vitakko hoti; kismiṃ asati vitakko na hotī’’ti? ‘‘Vitakko kho, devānaminda, papañcasaññāsaṅkhānidāno papañcasaññāsaṅkhāsamudayo papañcasaññāsaṅkhājātiko papañcasaññāsaṅkhāpabhavo; papañcasaññāsaṅkhāya sati vitakko hoti; papañcasaññāsaṅkhāya asati vitakko na hotī’’ti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Kathaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0205"/> paṭipanno pana, mārisa, bhikkhu papañcasaññāsaṅkhānirodhasāruppagāminiṃ paṭipadaṃ paṭipanno hotī’’ti?</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0205"/> <p rend="subhead">Vedanākammaṭṭhānaṃ</p>
358. Thus, Śakra, the lord of the gods, having rejoiced at the Blessed One's words and having approved, asked the Blessed One a further question:

“But, sir, what is the cause (*nidāna*), origin (*samudaya*), nature (*jātika*), and condition (*pabhava*) of envy and avarice (*īrṣyāmātsarya*)? When what exists do envy and avarice exist? When what does not exist do envy and avarice not exist?”

“Lord of the Gods, envy and avarice have like and dislike (*priyāpriya*) as their cause, origin, nature, and condition. When like and dislike exist, envy and avarice exist; when like and dislike do not exist, envy and avarice do not exist.”

“But, sir, what is the cause, origin, nature, and condition of like and dislike? When what exists do like and dislike exist; when what does not exist do like and dislike not exist?”

“Lord of the Gods, like and dislike have desire (*chanda*) as their cause, origin, nature, and condition. When desire exists, like and dislike exist; when desire does not exist, like and dislike do not exist.”

“But, sir, what is the cause, origin, nature, and condition of desire? When what exists does desire exist; when what does not exist does desire not exist?”

“Lord of the Gods, desire has thinking (*vitakka*) as its cause, origin, nature, and condition. When thinking exists, desire exists; when thinking does not exist, desire does not exist.”

“But, sir, what is the cause, origin, nature, and condition of thinking? When what exists does thinking exist; when what does not exist does thinking not exist?”

“Lord of the Gods, thinking has the perceptions and notions of proliferation (*papañcasaññāsaṅkhā*) as its cause, origin, nature, and condition. When perceptions and notions of proliferation exist, thinking exists; when perceptions and notions of proliferation do not exist, thinking does not exist.”

“But, sir, how does a *bhikṣu* who has undertaken the course (*pratipad*) that accords with the cessation (*nirodha*) of the perceptions and notions of proliferation, proceed (*pratipanna*)?”

Topic: Feeling as the object of meditation (*vedanākammaṭṭhānaṃ*).
<p n="358" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">358</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Nidānā</hi>dīni vuttatthāneva. <hi rend="bold">Piyāppiyanidāna</hi>nti piyasattasaṅkhāranidānaṃ macchariyaṃ, appiyasattasaṅkhāranidānā issā. Ubhayaṃ vā ubhayanidānaṃ. Pabbajitassa hi saddhivihārikādayo, gahaṭṭhassa puttādayo hatthiassādayo vā sattā piyā honti keḷāyitā mamāyitā, muhuttampi te apassanto adhivāsetuṃ na sakkoti. So aññaṃ tādisaṃ piyasattaṃ labhantaṃ disvā issaṃ karoti. ‘‘Iminā amhākaṃ kiñci kammaṃ atthi, muhuttaṃ tāva <pb ed="P" n="3.0720"/> naṃ dethā’’ti tameva aññehi yācito ‘‘na sakkā dātuṃ, kilamissati vā ukkaṇṭhissati vā’’tiādīni <pb ed="V" n="2.0280"/> vatvā macchariyaṃ karoti. Evaṃ tāva ubhayampi piyasattanidānaṃ hoti. Bhikkhussa pana pattacīvaraparikkhārajātaṃ, gahaṭṭhassa vā alaṅkārādiupakaraṇaṃ piyaṃ hoti manāpaṃ, so aññassa <pb ed="M" n="2.0312"/> tādisaṃ uppajjamānaṃ disvā ‘‘aho vatassa evarūpaṃ na bhaveyyā’’ti issaṃ karoti, yācito vāpi ‘‘mayampetaṃ mamāyantā na paribhuñjāma, na sakkā dātu’’nti macchariyaṃ karoti. Evaṃ ubhayampi piyasaṅkhāranidānaṃ hoti. Appiye pana te vuttappakāre satte ca saṅkhāre ca labhitvā sacepissa te amanāpā honti, tathāpi kilesānaṃ viparītavuttitāya ‘‘ṭhapetvā maṃ ko añño evarūpassa lābhī’’ti issaṃ vā karoti, yācito tāvakālikampi adadamāno macchariyaṃ vā karoti. Evaṃ ubhayampi appiyasattasaṅkhāranidānaṃ hoti.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Chandanidāna</hi>nti ettha pariyesanachando, paṭilābhachando, paribhogachando, sannidhichando, vissajjanachandoti pañcavidho chando.</p> <hi rend="bold">Chandanidāna</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Katamo <hi rend="bold">pariyesanachando?</hi> Idhekacco atitto chandajāto rūpaṃ pariyesati, saddaṃ. Gandhaṃ. Rasaṃ. Phoṭṭhabbaṃ pariyesati, dhanaṃ pariyesati. Ayaṃ pariyesanachando.</p> <hi rend="bold">pariyesanachando?</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Katamo <hi rend="bold">paṭilābhachando?</hi> Idhekacco atitto chandajāto rūpaṃ paṭilabhati, saddaṃ. Gandhaṃ. Rasaṃ. Phoṭṭhabbaṃ paṭilabhati, dhanaṃ paṭilabhati. Ayaṃ paṭilābhachando.</p> <hi rend="bold">paṭilābhachando?</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Katamo <hi rend="bold">paribhogachando?</hi> Idhekacco atitto chandajāto rūpaṃ paribhuñjati, saddaṃ. Gandhaṃ. Rasaṃ. Phoṭṭhabbaṃ paribhuñjati, dhanaṃ paribhuñjati. Ayaṃ paribhogachando.</p> <hi rend="bold">paribhogachando?</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Katamo <hi rend="bold">sannidhichando?</hi> Idhekacco atitto chandajāto dhanasannicayaṃ karoti ‘‘āpadāsu bhavissatī’’ti. Ayaṃ sannidhichando.</p> <hi rend="bold">sannidhichando?</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Katamo <hi rend="bold">vissajjanachando?</hi> Idhekacco atitto chandajāto dhanaṃ vissajjeti, hatthārohānaṃ, assārohānaṃ, rathikānaṃ, dhanuggahānaṃ – ‘‘ime maṃ rakkhissanti gopissanti mamāyissanti samparivārayissantī’’ti. Ayaṃ vissajjanachando. Ime pañca chandā. Idha taṇhāmattameva, taṃ sandhāya idaṃ vuttaṃ.</p> <hi rend="bold">vissajjanachando?</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Vitakkanidāno</hi>ti ettha ‘‘lābhaṃ paṭicca vinicchayo’’ti (dī. ni. 2.110) evaṃ vutto <pb ed="V" n="2.0281"/> vinicchayavitakko vitakko nāma. <hi rend="bold">Vinicchayo</hi>ti dve vinicchayā taṇhāvinicchayo <pb ed="M" n="2.0313"/> ca, diṭṭhivinicchayo ca. Aṭṭhasataṃ taṇhāvicaritaṃ taṇhāvinicchayo nāma. Dvāsaṭṭhi <pb ed="P" n="3.0721"/> diṭṭhiyo diṭṭhivinicchayo nāmāti evaṃ vuttataṇhāvinicchayavasena hi iṭṭhāniṭṭhapiyāppiyavavatthānaṃ na hoti. Tadeva hi ekaccassa iṭṭhaṃ hoti, ekaccassa aniṭṭhaṃ paccantarājamajjhimadesarājūnaṃ gaṇḍuppādamigamaṃsādīsu viya. Tasmiṃ pana taṇhāvinicchayavinicchite paṭiladdhavatthusmiṃ ‘‘ettakaṃ rūpassa bhavissati, ettakaṃ saddassa, ettakaṃ gandhassa, ettakaṃ rasassa, ettakaṃ phoṭṭhabbassa bhavissati, ettakaṃ mayhaṃ bhavissati, ettakaṃ parassa bhavissati, ettakaṃ nidahissāmi, ettakaṃ parassa dassāmī’’ti vavatthānaṃ vitakkavinicchayena hoti. Tenāha ‘‘chando kho, devānaminda, vitakkanidāno’’ti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Vitakkanidāno</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0281"/> <hi rend="bold">Vinicchayo</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0313"/> <pb ed="P" n="3.0721"/> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Papañcasaññāsaṅkhānidāno</hi>ti tayo papañcā taṇhāpapañco, mānapapañco, diṭṭhipapañcoti. Tattha aṭṭhasatataṇhāvicaritaṃ taṇhāpapañco nāma. Navavidho māno mānapapañco nāma. Dvāsaṭṭhi diṭṭhiyo diṭṭhipapañco nāma. Tesu idha taṇhāpapañco adhippeto. Kenaṭṭhena papañco? Mattapamattākārapāpanaṭṭhena papañco. Taṃsampayuttā saññā papañcasaññā. Saṅkhā vuccati koṭṭhāso ‘‘saññānidānā hi papañcasaṅkhā’’tiādīsu viya. Iti papañcasaññāsaṅkhānidānoti papañcasaññākoṭṭhāsanidāno vitakkoti attho.</p> <hi rend="bold">Papañcasaññāsaṅkhānidāno</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Papañcasaññāsaṅkhānirodhasāruppagāmini</hi>nti etissā papañcasaññāsaṅkhāya khayā nirodho vūpasamo, tassa sāruppañceva tattha gāminiṃ cāti saha vipassanāya maggaṃ pucchati.</p> <hi rend="bold">Papañcasaññāsaṅkhānirodhasāruppagāmini</hi> <p rend="subhead">Vedanākammaṭṭhānavaṇṇanā</p>
358. The *nidāna*s, etc. are only in the places where their meanings have been stated.

*Piyāppiyanidānaṃ*: *macchariya* (stinginess) has as its *nidāna* the *saṅkhāra* (preparation) of beloved beings, and *issā* (envy) has as its *nidāna* the *saṅkhāra* of unloved beings. Or, both have both *nidāna*s.

For an ascetic, students and so on, and for a householder, sons and so on, elephants, horses and so on, are beings that are loved, played with, and clung to. Even not seeing them for a moment, he cannot bear it. Seeing another obtaining such a beloved being, he feels envy. Saying, "There is some work for us with this; give it to us for just a moment," when asked for that very thing by others, he feels stinginess, saying, "It is not possible to give it; he will get tired or be anxious," and so on. Thus, both have as their *nidāna* beloved beings.

For a *bhikkhu*, a set of bowl, robe, and requisites; or for a householder, ornaments and so on, are loved and pleasing. Seeing another having such things arise, he feels envy, saying, "Oh, would that he would not have such a thing!" Or, when asked, he feels stinginess, saying, "We ourselves, clinging to this, do not use it; it is not possible to give it." Thus, both have as their *nidāna* beloved *saṅkhāra*s.

But, having obtained beings and *saṅkhāra*s of the kind described that are unloved, even if they are unpleasant to him, nevertheless, because of the contrary behavior of the *kilesa*s (defilements), he feels envy, saying, "Who other than me is the possessor of such a thing, having kept me aside?" Or, when asked, not giving even for a short time, he feels stinginess. Thus, both have as their *nidāna* unloved beings and *saṅkhāra*s.

*Chandanidānaṃ*: Here, *pariyesanachando* (desire for seeking), *paṭilābhachando* (desire for obtaining), *paribhogachando* (desire for enjoying), *sannidhichando* (desire for accumulating), *vissajjanachando* (desire for giving away)—thus, *chanda* (desire) is fivefold.

*Chandanidānaṃ*: What is *pariyesanachando*? Here, someone, not satisfied, born of desire, seeks form, sound, smell, taste, tactile objects, wealth. This is *pariyesanachando*.

*Pariyesanachando*? What is *paṭilābhachando*? Here, someone, not satisfied, born of desire, obtains form, sound, smell, taste, tactile objects, wealth. This is *paṭilābhachando*.

*Paṭilābhachando*? What is *paribhogachando*? Here, someone, not satisfied, born of desire, enjoys form, sound, smell, taste, tactile objects, wealth. This is *paribhogachando*.

*Paribhogachando*? What is *sannidhichando*? Here, someone, not satisfied, born of desire, makes an accumulation of wealth, thinking, "It will be in times of distress." This is *sannidhichando*.

*Sannidhichando*? What is *vissajjanachando*? Here, someone, not satisfied, born of desire, gives away wealth to elephant riders, horse riders, charioteers, archers—thinking, "These will protect me, guard me, regard me as their own, surround me." This is *vissajjanachando*. These are the five *chanda*s.

Here, it is only the measure of craving. With reference to that, this was said.

*Vissajjanachando*? *Vitakkanidāno*: Here, "Relying upon gain, decision arises" (Dī.
Ni.
2).
Thus it is said, *vinicchayavitakko vitakko* is the name.

*Vinicchayo*: There are two kinds of *vinicchaya*: *taṇhāvinicchayo* and *diṭṭhivinicchayo*.

*Aṭṭhasataṃ taṇhāvicaritaṃ* is called *taṇhāvinicchayo*.

*Dvāsaṭṭhi diṭṭhiyo* is called *diṭṭhivinicchayo*. Thus, because of what is called *taṇhāvinicchaya*, there is no determining of what is desirable, undesirable, pleasing, and unpleasing.

Indeed, that itself is desirable for some, undesirable for others, just as with earthworms, wild game meat, and so on, for kings of border regions and kings of the middle country.

However, in that object obtained after determining by *taṇhāvinicchaya*, the determining "this much will be for form, this much for sound, this much for smell, this much for taste, this much for tactile object, this much will be for me, this much will be for others, this much I will store away, this much I will give to others" is done by *vitakkavinicchaya*.

Therefore, he said, "Desire, ruler of the gods, has thought as its source (*vitakkanidāno*)."

*Vitakkanidāno vinicchayo papañcasaññāsaṅkhānidāno*: There are three kinds of *papañca*: *taṇhāpapañco*, *mānapapañco*, and *diṭṭhipapañco*.

There, *aṭṭhasatataṇhāvicaritaṃ* is called *taṇhāpapañco*.

Nine kinds of conceit (*māna*) are called *mānapapañco*.

Sixty-two views (*diṭṭhiyo*) are called *diṭṭhipapañco*.

Among these, here, *taṇhāpapañco* is intended.

In what sense is it *papañco*? It is *papañco* in the sense of leading to the state of being careless and intoxicated (*mattapamattākārapāpanaṭṭhena*).

The perception (*saññā*) associated with that is *papañcasaññā*.

The term *saṅkhā* means a division, as in "because perception is the source, there are *papañcasaṅkhā*s," and so on.

Thus, *papañcasaññāsaṅkhānidāno* means that *vitakko* has *papañcasaññākoṭṭhāsa* as its source.

*Papañcasaññāsaṅkhānidāno papañcasaññāsaṅkhānirodhasāruppagāmini*: By the destruction (*khayā*) and cessation (*nirodho vūpasamo*) of this *papañcasaññāsaṅkhā*, it is in accordance with that (*sāruppañceva*) and leads to it (*tattha gāminiṃ ca*), thus one asks about the path together with insight (*vipassanā*).

*Papañcasaññāsaṅkhānirodhasāruppagāmini*

Description of the meditation subject of feeling (*vedanākammaṭṭhāna*).
359
<p n="359" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">359</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> ‘‘Somanassaṃpāhaṃ <pb ed="P" n="2.0278"/><note>pahaṃ (sī. pī.), cāhaṃ (syā. kaṃ.)</note>, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi – sevitabbampi, asevitabbampi. Domanassaṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi – sevitabbampi, asevitabbampi. Upekkhaṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi – sevitabbampi, asevitabbampi.</p>
359.
"Ruler of the Gods (devānaminda), I declare that there are two kinds of feeling associated with joy (somanassa): the kind to be pursued (sevitabbam), and the kind to be avoided (asevitabbam).
I declare that there are two kinds of feeling associated with sorrow (domanassa): the kind to be pursued, and the kind to be avoided.
I declare that there are two kinds of feeling associated with equanimity (upekkhā): the kind to be pursued, and the kind to be avoided."
<p n="359" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">359</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Athassa bhagavā <hi rend="bold">somanassaṃpāha</hi>nti tisso vedanā ārabhi. Kiṃ pana bhagavatā pucchitaṃ kathitaṃ, apucchitaṃ, sānusandhikaṃ, ananusandhikanti? Pucchitameva kathitaṃ, no apucchitaṃ, sānusandhikameva, no ananusandhikaṃ. Devatānañhi rūpato arūpaṃ pākaṭataraṃ, arūpepi vedanā pākaṭatarā. Kasmā? Devatānañhi karajakāyaṃ sukhumaṃ, kammajaṃ balavaṃ, karajakāyassa sukhumattā, kammajassa balavattā ekāhārampi atikkamitvā na tiṭṭhanti, uṇhapāsāṇe <pb ed="M" n="2.0314"/> ṭhapitasappipiṇḍi viya vilīyantīti sabbaṃ <hi rend="bold">brahmajāle</hi> vuttanayeneva veditabbaṃ. Tasmā bhagavā sakkassa tisso vedanā ārabhi. Duvidhañhi kammaṭṭhānaṃ – rūpakammaṭṭhānaṃ, arūpakammaṭṭhānañca. Rūpapariggaho, arūpapariggahotipi etadeva <pb ed="P" n="3.0722"/> vuccati. Tattha bhagavā yassa rūpaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0282"/> pākaṭaṃ, tassa saṅkhepamanasikāravasena vā vitthāramanasikāravasena vā catudhātuvavatthānaṃ vitthārento rūpakammaṭṭhānaṃ katheti. Yassa arūpaṃ pākaṭaṃ, tassa arūpakammaṭṭhānaṃ katheti. Kathento ca tassa vatthubhūtaṃ rūpakammaṭṭhānaṃ dassetvāva katheti, devānaṃ pana arūpakammaṭṭhānaṃ pākaṭanti arūpakammaṭṭhānavasena vedanā ārabhi.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Tividho hi arūpakammaṭṭhāne abhiniveso – phassavasena, vedanāvasena, cittavasenāti. Kathaṃ? Ekaccassa hi saṅkhittena vā vitthārena vā pariggahite rūpakammaṭṭhāne tasmiṃ ārammaṇe cittacetasikānaṃ paṭhamābhinipāto taṃ ārammaṇaṃ phusanto uppajjamāno <hi rend="bold">phasso</hi> pākaṭo hoti. Ekaccassa taṃ ārammaṇaṃ anubhavantī uppajjamānā <hi rend="bold">vedanā</hi> pākaṭā hoti. Ekaccassa taṃ ārammaṇaṃ pariggahetvā taṃ vijānantaṃ uppajjamānaṃ <hi rend="bold">viññāṇaṃ</hi> pākaṭaṃ hoti.</p> <hi rend="bold">phasso</hi> <hi rend="bold">vedanā</hi> <hi rend="bold">viññāṇaṃ</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Tattha yassa phasso pākaṭo hoti, sopi na kevalaṃ phassova uppajjati, tena saddhiṃ tadeva ārammaṇaṃ anubhavamānā vedanāpi uppajjati, sañjānamānā saññāpi, cetayamānā cetanāpi, vijānamānaṃ viññāṇampi uppajjatīti phassapañcamakeyeva pariggaṇhāti. Yassa vedanā pākaṭā hoti, sopi na kevalaṃ vedanāva uppajjati, tāya saddhiṃ tadeva ārammaṇaṃ phusamāno phassopi uppajjati, sañjānamānā saññāpi, cetayamānā cetanāpi, vijānamānaṃ viññāṇampi uppajjatīti phassapañcamakeyeva pariggaṇhāti. Yassa viññāṇaṃ pākaṭaṃ hoti, sopi na kevalaṃ viññāṇameva uppajjati, tena saddhiṃ tadevārammaṇaṃ phusamāno phassopi uppajjati, anubhavamānā vedanāpi, sañjānamānā saññāpi, cetayamānā cetanāpi uppajjatīti phassapañcamakeyeva pariggaṇhāti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">So ‘‘ime phassapañcamakā dhammā kiṃ nissitā’’ti upadhārento ‘‘vatthunissitā’’ti pajānāti. Vatthu nāma karajakāyo, yaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ <pb ed="M" n="2.0315"/> – ‘‘idañca pana me viññāṇaṃ ettha sitaṃ ettha paṭibaddha’’nti. So atthato bhūtāni ceva upādārūpāni ca. Evamettha vatthu rūpaṃ, phassapañcamakā nāmanti nāmarūpamattameva passati. Rūpañcettha rūpakkhandho, nāmaṃ cattāro arūpino khandhāti pañcakkhandhamattaṃ hoti. Nāmarūpavinimuttā hi pañcakkhandhā, pañcakkhandhavinimuttaṃ vā nāmarūpaṃ natthi. So ‘‘ime pañcakkhandhā kiṃ <pb ed="P" n="3.0723"/> hetukā’’ti upaparikkhanto ‘‘avijjādihetukā’’ti passati. Tato ‘‘paccayo ceva paccayuppannañca idaṃ, añño satto vā puggalo vā natthi, suddhasaṅkhārapuñjamattamevā’’ti sappaccayanāmarūpavasena tilakkhaṇaṃ āropetvā vipassanāpaṭipāṭiyā ‘‘aniccaṃ dukkhaṃ anattā’’ti sammasanto vicarati <pb ed="V" n="2.0283"/>, so ajja ajjāti paṭivedhaṃ ākaṅkhamāno tathārūpe divase utusappāyaṃ, puggalasappāyaṃ, bhojanasappāyaṃ, dhammasavanasappāyaṃ vā labhitvā ekapallaṅkena nisinnova vipassanaṃ matthakaṃ pāpetvā arahatte patiṭṭhāti. Evamimesampi tiṇṇaṃ janānaṃ yāva arahattā kammaṭṭhānaṃ kathitaṃ hoti.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0315"/> <pb ed="P" n="3.0723"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0283"/> <p rend="bodytext">Idha pana bhagavā arūpakammaṭṭhānaṃ kathento vedanāsīsena kathesi. Phassavasena hi viññāṇavasena vā kathiyamānaṃ etassa na pākaṭaṃ hoti, andhakāraṃ viya khāyati. Vedanāvasena pana pākaṭaṃ hoti. Kasmā? Vedanānaṃ uppattiyā pākaṭatāya. Sukhadukkhavedanānañhi uppatti pākaṭā. Yadā sukhaṃ uppajjati, tadā sakalaṃ sarīraṃ khobhentaṃ maddantaṃ pharamānaṃ abhisandayamānaṃ satadhotasappiṃ khādāpayantaṃ viya, satapākatelaṃ makkhayamānaṃ viya, ghaṭasahassena pariḷāhaṃ nibbāpayamānaṃ viya, ‘‘aho sukhaṃ, aho sukha’’nti vācaṃ nicchārayamānameva uppajjati. Yadā dukkhaṃ uppajjati, tadā sakalasarīraṃ khobhentaṃ maddantaṃ pharamānaṃ abhisandayamānaṃ tattaphālaṃ pavesentaṃ viya, vilīnatambalohena āsiñcantaṃ viya, sukkhatiṇavanappatimhi araññe dāruukkākalāpaṃ khipamānaṃ viya ‘‘aho dukkhaṃ, aho dukkha’’nti vippalāpayamānameva uppajjati. Iti sukhadukkhavedanānaṃ uppatti pākaṭā hoti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Adukkhamasukhā pana duddīpanā andhakārena viya abhibhūtā. Sā sukhadukkhānaṃ apagame sātāsātapaṭikkhepavasena majjhattākārabhūtā adukkhamasukhā vedanāti nayato gaṇhantassa pākaṭā hoti. Yathā kiṃ? Yathā antarā piṭṭhipāsāṇaṃ āruhitvā palātassa migassa anupadaṃ gacchanto <pb ed="M" n="2.0316"/> migaluddako piṭṭhipāsāṇassa orabhāgepi parabhāgepi padaṃ disvā majjhe apassantopi ‘‘ito āruḷho, ito oruḷho, majjhe piṭṭhipāsāṇe iminā padesena gato bhavissatī’’ti nayato jānāti <pb ed="P" n="3.0724"/>. Evaṃ āruḷhaṭṭhāne padaṃ viya hi sukhavedanāya uppatti pākaṭā hoti, oruḷhaṭṭhāne padaṃ viya dukkhavedanāya uppatti pākaṭā hoti, ito āruyha, ito oruyha, majjhe evaṃ gatoti nayato gahaṇaṃ viya sukhadukkhānaṃ apagame sātāsātapaṭikkhepavasena majjhattākārabhūtā adukkhamasukhā vedanāti nayato gaṇhantassa pākaṭā hoti. Evaṃ bhagavā paṭhamaṃ rūpakammaṭṭhānaṃ kathetvā pacchā arūpakammaṭṭhānaṃ vedanāvasena nivattetvā dassesi.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0316"/> <pb ed="P" n="3.0724"/> <p rend="bodytext">Na kevalañca idheva evaṃ dassesi, <hi rend="bold">mahāsatipaṭṭhāne, majjhimanikāyamhi satipaṭṭhāne, cūḷataṇhāsaṅkhaye, mahātaṇhāsaṅkhaye, cūḷavedallasutte, mahāvedallasutte, raṭṭhapālasutte, māgaṇḍiyasutte, dhātuvibhaṅge, āneñjasappāye, sakale vedanāsaṃyutte</hi>ti evaṃ anekesu suttantesu paṭhamaṃ rūpakammaṭṭhānaṃ kathetvā <pb ed="V" n="2.0284"/> pacchā arūpakammaṭṭhānaṃ vedanāvasena nivattetvā dassesi. Yathā ca tesu tesu, evaṃ imasmimpi sakkapañhe paṭhamaṃ rūpakammaṭṭhānaṃ kathetvā pacchā arūpakammaṭṭhānaṃ vedanāvasena nivattetvā dassesi. Rūpakammaṭṭhānaṃ panettha vedanāya ārammaṇamattakaṃyeva saṅkhittaṃ, tasmā pāḷiyaṃ nāruḷhaṃ bhavissati.</p> <hi rend="bold">mahāsatipaṭṭhāne, majjhimanikāyamhi satipaṭṭhāne, cūḷataṇhāsaṅkhaye, mahātaṇhāsaṅkhaye, cūḷavedallasutte, mahāvedallasutte, raṭṭhapālasutte, māgaṇḍiyasutte, dhātuvibhaṅge, āneñjasappāye, sakale vedanāsaṃyutte</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0284"/>
359. Then, the Blessed One aroused three feelings, saying, "somanassaṃpāha."

What was spoken by the Blessed One, having been asked, or not asked, with connection, or without connection? Having been asked, indeed, it was spoken; not having been asked. With connection only, not without connection.

For deities, the formless is more manifest than form; even in the formless, feeling is more manifest.

Why? For deities, indeed, the body born of action is strong, because of the subtlety of the body born of karma, because of the strength of that born of action, they do not remain even having transgressed a single meal, like a lump of ghee placed on a hot stone melts away. All that should be understood in the same manner as explained in the *Brahmajāla*.

Therefore, the Blessed One aroused three feelings in Śakka.

Twofold indeed is the meditation subject – the meditation subject of form, and the meditation subject of the formless.

The laying hold of form, the laying hold of the formless—this itself is what is meant.

There, the Blessed One, for whomever form is manifest, expands the *rūpakammaṭṭhāna*, explaining the establishment of the four elements by way of concise or extended attention.

For whomever the formless is manifest, he tells the *arūpakammaṭṭhāna*.

And while telling, having shown the *rūpakammaṭṭhāna* that is the basis of that, he tells, but for the *devatā*, the *arūpakammaṭṭhāna* is manifest. Therefore, he began feeling by way of the *arūpakammaṭṭhāna*.

Threefold indeed is the devotion to the *arūpakammaṭṭhāna* – by way of contact, by way of feeling, by way of mind.

How? For one person, indeed, when the *rūpakammaṭṭhāna* is laid hold of in brief or in detail, the first impact of mind-with-mental characteristics on that object, the *phassa* that arises contacting that object becomes clear.

For one person, the *vedanā* that arises experiencing that object becomes clear.

For one person, the *viññāṇa* that arises having laid hold of that object, knowing that, becomes clear.

*Phassa*, *Vedanā*, *Viññāṇa*. There, for whomever *phassa* is manifest, not only does *phassa* arise, but together with that, the *vedanā* that experiences that same object also arises, the *saṃjñā* that cognizes, the *cetanā* that intends, the *viññāṇa* that distinguishes also arises. He lays hold of just the five beginning with *phassa*.

For whomever *vedanā* is manifest, not only does *vedanā* arise, but together with that, the *phassa* that contacts that same object also arises, the *saṃjñā* that cognizes, the *cetanā* that intends, the *viññāṇa* that distinguishes also arises. He lays hold of just the five beginning with *phassa*.

For whomever *viññāṇa* is manifest, not only does *viññāṇa* arise, but together with that, the *phassa* that contacts that same object also arises, the *vedanā* that experiences, the *saṃjñā* that cognizes, the *cetanā* that intends also arises. He lays hold of just the five beginning with *phassa*.

That one, considering, "on what are these five *dharmas* beginning with *phassa* based?", understands, "based on a *vatthu*."

*Vatthu* means the body born of karma, with reference to which it was said – "And this my *viññāṇa* is supported here, bound here."

That one sees, in reality, both the elements and the derived forms.

Thus, here, *vatthu* is form, the five beginning with *phassa* are name. He sees only name and form.

And here, form is the aggregate of form, name is the four formless aggregates. There is only the five aggregates.

For the five aggregates are separate from name and form, and name and form separate from the five aggregates do not exist.

That one, examining "what is the cause of these five aggregates?", sees "they are caused by ignorance, etc."

Then, having applied the three characteristics by way of name and form together with conditions, "impermanence, suffering, non-self," according to the gradual succession of insight-producing knowledge, "This is conditioned, and this is produced from conditions, there is no other being or person, there is only a heap of bare formations," he lives contemplating.

That one, desiring realization today, on such a day having obtained suitable climate, suitable person, suitable food, or suitable *dhamma*-hearing, sits down in a single sitting, and having brought insight to the head, he is established in arahantship.

Thus, for these three kinds of people also, the meditation subject up to arahantship has been told.

Here, however, the Blessed One, while telling the *arūpakammaṭṭhāna*, told it with feeling as the head.

For, when telling by way of *phassa* or by way of *viññāṇa*, that is not manifest to him, it appears like darkness.

But by way of *vedanā*, it becomes manifest.

Why? Because of the manifestness of the arising of feelings.
Indeed, the arising of pleasant or painful feeling is clear.

When pleasant feeling arises, then the whole body is agitated, massaged, pervaded, and overflowing, as if causing one to eat fresh clarified butter cooled a hundred times, as if causing one to be massaged with emollient oil worth a hundred pieces, as if causing one to be cooled of a burning fever with a thousand pots of cold water, and as one utters the words, “Ah, ‘tis joy, ah, ‘tis joy.”

When painful feeling arises, then the whole body is agitated, massaged, pervaded, and overflowing, as if inserting a heated plowshare, as if sprinkling molten copper, and as if hurling bundles of wood firebrands into dried grass and trees in the forest, and as one bewails with the words, “Alas, what woe, alas, what woe.”

Thus, the arising of pleasant or painful feeling becomes clear.

But the neither-pleasant-nor-painful feeling is obscure, as if overcome by darkness.

The neither-pleasant-nor-painful feeling becomes clear to one who grasps it methodically, thinking: “At the disappearance of pleasure and pain, the neutral neither-pleasant-nor-painful feeling occurs, which is contrary to the pleasant and the unpleasant.”

How is it? Just as a deer hunter following the hoof marks of a deer, which midway, having gone up a flat rock, is fleeing, after seeing the hoof marks on the hither and thither side of the rock, without seeing any trace in the middle, knows by inference: “Here the animal went up, and here, it went down; in the middle, on the flat rock, possibly it went through this part.”

Like the hoofmark at the place of going up, the arising of pleasurable feeling becomes clear; like the hoofmark at the place of descent, the arising of painful feeling becomes clear; like the grasping through inference of the part traversed over the rock by the deer, is the laying hold of the neither-pleasant-nor-painful feeling methodically with the thought: “At the disappearance of pleasure and pain, the neutral neither-pleasant-nor-painful feeling occurs, which is contrary to the pleasant and the unpleasant.”

In this manner, the Blessed One, having expounded at first the form subject of meditation, later, pointed out the formless subject of meditation, by way of feeling.

Not only here did he point it out thus. In the *Mahāsatipaṭṭhāna*, the *Satipaṭṭhāna* of the *Majjhimanikāya*, the *Cūḷataṇhāsaṅkhaya*, the *Mahātaṇhāsaṅkhaya*, the *Cūḷavedallasutta*, the *Mahāvedallasutta*, the *Raṭṭhapālasutta*, the *Māgaṇḍiyasutta*, the *Dhātuvibhaṅga*, the *Āneñjasappāya*, [and] in the whole of the *Vedanāsaṃyutta*—in this manner, in many discourses, the Master having expounded at first the form subject of meditation, later, pointed out the formless subject of meditation, by way of feeling.

Just as in those, so also in this *Sakkapañha*, having expounded at first the form subject of meditation, later, he pointed out the formless subject of meditation, by way of feeling.

Here, however, the form subject of meditation, is only a summary of the object of feeling; therefore, it will not have been included in the Pāli.
360
<p n="360" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">360</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> ‘‘Somanassaṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi sevitabbampi, asevitabbampīti iti kho panetaṃ vuttaṃ, kiñcetaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ? Tattha yaṃ jaññā somanassaṃ ‘imaṃ kho me somanassaṃ sevato akusalā dhammā abhivaḍḍhanti, kusalā dhammā parihāyantī’ti, evarūpaṃ somanassaṃ na sevitabbaṃ. Tattha yaṃ jaññā somanassaṃ ‘imaṃ kho <pb ed="T" n="2.0313"/> me somanassaṃ sevato <pb ed="M" n="2.0222"/> akusalā dhammā parihāyanti, kusalā dhammā abhivaḍḍhantī’ti, evarūpaṃ somanassaṃ sevitabbaṃ. Tattha yaṃ ce savitakkaṃ savicāraṃ, yaṃ ce avitakkaṃ avicāraṃ, ye avitakke avicāre, te <note>se (sī. pī.)</note> paṇītatare. Somanassaṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi sevitabbampi, asevitabbampīti. Iti yaṃ taṃ vuttaṃ, idametaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ.</p>
360.
"I, O Indra, ruler of the gods, declare that happiness is twofold: the kind that should be pursued and the kind that should not be pursued." Thus was it said. And in reference to what was it said? There, when one knows happiness, "When I pursue this happiness, unwholesome qualities increase and wholesome qualities decrease," such happiness should not be pursued.

There, when one knows happiness, "When I pursue this happiness, unwholesome qualities decrease and wholesome qualities increase," such happiness should be pursued.

There, whatever is with thought and with reflection, and whatever is without thought and without reflection—of those without thought and without reflection, those are more excellent.

"I, O Indra, ruler of the gods, declare that happiness is twofold: the kind that should be pursued and the kind that should not be pursued."

Thus, because of this, this was said.
<p n="360" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">360</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Arūpakammaṭṭhāne yaṃ tassa pākaṭaṃ vedanāvasena abhinivesamukhaṃ, tameva dassetuṃ <hi rend="bold">somanassaṃpāhaṃ, devānamindā</hi>tiādimāha. Tattha <hi rend="bold">duvidhenā</hi>ti dvividhena, dvīhi koṭṭhāsehīti attho. <hi rend="bold">Evarūpaṃ somanassaṃ na sevitabba</hi>nti evarūpaṃ gehasitasomanassaṃ na sevitabbaṃ. Gehasitasomanassaṃ nāma ‘‘tattha katamāni cha gehasitāni somanassāni? Cakkhuviññeyyānaṃ rūpānaṃ iṭṭhānaṃ kantānaṃ manāpānaṃ manoramānaṃ lokāmisapaṭisaṃyuttānaṃ paṭilābhaṃ vā paṭilābhato samanupassato, pubbe vā paṭiladdhapubbaṃ atītaṃ niruddhaṃ vipariṇataṃ samanussarato uppajjati somanassaṃ, yaṃ evarūpaṃ somanassaṃ, idaṃ vuccati gehasitaṃ somanassa’’nti evaṃ chasu dvāresu vuttakāmaguṇanissitaṃ somanassaṃ (ma. ni. 3.306).</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Evarūpaṃ</hi><pb ed="M" n="2.0317"/><hi rend="bold">somanassaṃ sevitabba</hi>nti evarūpaṃ nekkhammasitaṃ somanassaṃ sevitabbaṃ. Nekkhammasitaṃ somanassaṃ nāma – ‘‘tattha katamāni cha nekkhammasitāni somanassāni? Rūpānaṃ <pb ed="P" n="3.0725"/> tveva aniccataṃ viditvā vipariṇāmavirāganirodhaṃ pubbe ceva rūpā etarahi ca sabbe te rūpā aniccā, dukkhā, vipariṇāmadhammāti evametaṃ yathābhūtaṃ sammappaññāya passato uppajjati somanassaṃ, yaṃ evarūpaṃ somanassaṃ, idaṃ vuccati nekkhammasitaṃ somanassa’’nti (ma. ni. 3.308) evaṃ chasu dvāresu iṭṭhārammaṇe āpāthagate aniccādivasena vipassanaṃ paṭṭhapetvā ussukkāpetuṃ sakkontassa ‘‘ussukkitā me vipassanā’’ti somanassajātassa uppannaṃ somanassaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Sevitabba</hi>nti idaṃ nekkhammavasena, vipassanāvasena, anussativasena, paṭhamajjhānādivasena ca uppajjanakasomanassaṃ sevitabbaṃ nāma.</p> <hi rend="bold">Evarūpaṃ</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0317"/> <hi rend="bold">somanassaṃ sevitabba</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0725"/> <hi rend="bold">Sevitabba</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Tattha <hi rend="bold">yaṃ ce savitakkaṃ savicāra</hi>nti tasmimpi nekkhammasite somanasse yaṃ nekkhammavasena, vipassanāvasena, anussativasena, paṭhamajjhānavasena ca uppannaṃ savitakkaṃ savicāraṃ somanassanti jāneyya. <hi rend="bold">Yaṃ ce avitakkaṃ avicāra</hi>nti yaṃ pana dutiyatatiyajjhānavasena uppannaṃ avitakkaṃ avicāraṃ somanassanti jāneyya. <hi rend="bold">Ye avitakke avicāre, te paṇītatare</hi>ti etesupi dvīsu yaṃ avitakkaṃ avicāraṃ, taṃ paṇītataranti attho.</p> <hi rend="bold">yaṃ ce savitakkaṃ savicāra</hi> <hi rend="bold">Yaṃ ce avitakkaṃ avicāra</hi> <hi rend="bold">Ye avitakke avicāre, te paṇītatare</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Iminā <pb ed="V" n="2.0285"/> kiṃ kathitaṃ hoti? Dvinnaṃ arahattaṃ kathitaṃ. Kathaṃ? Eko kira bhikkhu savitakkasavicāre somanasse vipassanaṃ paṭṭhapetvā ‘‘idaṃ somanassaṃ kiṃ nissita’’nti upadhārento ‘‘vatthunissita’’nti pajānātīti phassapañcamake vuttanayeneva anukkamena arahatte patiṭṭhāti. Eko avitakkaavicāre somanasse vipassanaṃ paṭṭhapetvā vuttanayeneva arahatte patiṭṭhāti. Tattha abhiniviṭṭhasomanassesupi savitakkasavicārato avitakkaavicāraṃ paṇītataraṃ. Savitakkasavicārasomanassavipassanātopi avitakkaavicāravipassanā paṇītatarā. Savitakkasavicārasomanassaphalasamāpattitopi avitakkaavicārasomanassaphalasamāpattiyeva paṇītatarā. Tenāha bhagavā ‘‘ye avitakke avicāre, te paṇītatare’’ti.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0285"/>
360. In the realm of the immaterial, to show that which is evident to him as the way of absorption with feeling, he said beginning with, “*Somanassaṃpāhaṃ, devānamindā*."

There, "*duvidhenā*" means in two ways, with two parts.

"*Evarūpaṃ somanassaṃ na sevitabba*," means such joy associated with household life is not to be cultivated.

What is joy associated with household life? "What six kinds of joy are associated with household life? Joy arises in one who obtains or reflects on obtaining forms knowable by the eye that are desired, lovely, pleasing, delightful, connected with worldly pleasures; or in one who recollects something previously obtained, past, stopped, changed. Such joy is called joy associated with household life," thus joy is dependent on the enjoyment of the qualities of desire spoken of in the six sense-doors (Ma. Ni. 3.306).

"*Evarūpaṃ somanassaṃ sevitabba*," means such joy associated with renunciation is to be cultivated.

What is joy associated with renunciation? "What six kinds of joy are associated with renunciation? Having known the impermanence, change, fading away, and cessation of forms, one sees as it actually is with proper wisdom that forms both formerly and now are all impermanent, suffering, and subject to change, joy arises. Such joy is called joy associated with renunciation" (Ma. Ni. 3.308). Thus, for one who is able to arouse zeal after establishing insight in the six sense-doors with regards to a desired object that has come into range in terms of impermanence, etc., joy arises born of the joy, "My insight has been aroused."

"*Sevitabba*" means this joy that arises through renunciation, through insight, through recollection, and through the first jhāna, etc., is to be cultivated.

"*Evarūpaṃ somanassaṃ sevitabba sevitabba*."

There, "*yaṃ ce savitakkaṃ savicāra*" means in that joy associated with renunciation, one should know that whatever joy arises through renunciation, through insight, through recollection, and through the first jhāna is with initial application and sustained application.

"*Yaṃ ce avitakkaṃ avicāra*" means one should know that whatever joy arises through the second and third jhāna is without initial application and sustained application.

"*Ye avitakke avicāre, te paṇītatare*" means of these two, that which is without initial application and sustained application is more excellent.

"*Yaṃ ce savitakkaṃ savicāra yaṃ ce avitakkaṃ avicāra ye avitakke avicāre, te paṇītatare*."

What is spoken of by this? The arahantship of two is spoken of.

How? It is said that one bhikkhu establishes insight in joy that is with initial application and sustained application, and discerning, "What is this joy dependent on?", he understands, "Dependent on an object," and thus he is established in arahantship in due course according to the method spoken of in the Phassapañcama.

One bhikkhu establishes insight in joy that is without initial application and sustained application, and is established in arahantship in the manner spoken of.

There, even among those who have joy that they are attached to, that which is without initial application and sustained application is more excellent than that which is with initial application and sustained application.

Insight into joy that is without initial application and sustained application is more excellent than insight into joy that is with initial application and sustained application.

The attainment of fruition of joy that is without initial application and sustained application is more excellent than the attainment of fruition of joy that is with initial application and sustained application.

Therefore, the Blessed One said, "Those which are without initial application and sustained application are more excellent."
361
<p n="361" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">361</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> ‘‘Domanassaṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi sevitabbampi <pb ed="P" n="2.0279"/>, asevitabbampīti. Iti kho panetaṃ vuttaṃ, kiñcetaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ? Tattha yaṃ jaññā domanassaṃ ‘imaṃ kho me domanassaṃ sevato akusalā dhammā abhivaḍḍhanti, kusalā dhammā parihāyantī’ti, evarūpaṃ domanassaṃ na sevitabbaṃ. Tattha yaṃ jaññā domanassaṃ ‘imaṃ kho me domanassaṃ sevato akusalā dhammā parihāyanti, kusalā dhammā abhivaḍḍhantī’ti, evarūpaṃ domanassaṃ sevitabbaṃ. Tattha yaṃ ce savitakkaṃ savicāraṃ, yaṃ ce avitakkaṃ avicāraṃ, ye avitakke avicāre, te paṇītatare. Domanassaṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi sevitabbampi, asevitabbampī’ti iti yaṃ taṃ vuttaṃ, idametaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ.</p>
361.
“And I declare, O Indra, lord of gods, that sorrow is of two kinds: to be cultivated and not to be cultivated.
That is why this was said. What was said with reference to what? There, when one knows sorrow thus: ‘When I cultivate this sorrow, unwholesome *dharmas* increase and wholesome *dharmas* diminish,’ then such sorrow is not to be cultivated.
There, when one knows sorrow thus: ‘When I cultivate this sorrow, unwholesome *dharmas* diminish and wholesome *dharmas* increase,’ then such sorrow is to be cultivated.
Now, of such happiness as is accompanied by thinking (*savitakkaṃ*) and pondering (*savicāraṃ*), and of that which is not so accompanied, the latter is the more excellent.
And I declare, O Indra, lord of gods, that sorrow is of two kinds: to be cultivated and not to be cultivated." That is why this was said. What was said with reference to what?
<p n="361" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">361</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Evarūpaṃ</hi><pb ed="M" n="2.0318"/><hi rend="bold">domanassaṃ na sevitabba</hi>nti evarūpaṃ gehasitadomanassaṃ na sevitabbaṃ. Gehasitadomanassaṃ nāma – ‘‘tattha katamāni cha gehasitāni domanassāni? Cakkhuviññeyyānaṃ rūpānaṃ iṭṭhānaṃ kantānaṃ manāpānaṃ manoramānaṃ lokāmisapaṭisaṃyuttānaṃ appaṭilābhaṃ vā appaṭilābhato <pb ed="P" n="3.0726"/> samanupassato pubbe vā apaṭiladdhapubbaṃ atītaṃ niruddhaṃ vipariṇataṃ samanussarato uppajjati domanassaṃ, yaṃ evarūpaṃ domanassaṃ, idaṃ vuccati gehasitadomanassa’’nti (ma. ni. 3.307). Evaṃ chasu dvāresu iṭṭhārammaṇaṃ nānubhaviṃ, nānubhavissāmi, nānubhavāmīti vitakkayato uppannaṃ kāmaguṇanissitaṃ domanassaṃ.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Evarūpaṃ domanassaṃ sevitabba</hi>nti evarūpaṃ nekkhammasitadomanassaṃ sevitabbaṃ. Nekkhammasitadomanassaṃ nāma – ‘‘tattha katamāni cha nekkhammasitāni domanassāni? Rūpānaṃ tveva aniccataṃ viditvā vipariṇāmavirāganirodhaṃ pubbe ceva rūpā etarahi ca sabbe te rūpā aniccā, dukkhā, vipariṇāmadhammāti evametaṃ yathābhūtaṃ sampappaññāya disvā anuttaresu vimokkhesu pihaṃ upaṭṭhāpeti ‘kudāssu nāmāhaṃ tadāyatanaṃ, upasampajja viharissāmi, yadariyā etarahi āyatanaṃ upasampajja viharantī’ti. Iti anuttaresu vimokkhesu pihaṃ upaṭṭhāpayato uppajjati pihapaccayā domanassaṃ, yaṃ evarūpaṃ domanassaṃ, idaṃ vuccati nekkhammasitadomanassa’’nti (ma. ni. 3.307) evaṃ chasu dvāresu iṭṭhārammaṇe āpāthagate anuttaravimokkhasaṅkhātaariyaphaladhammesu pihaṃ upaṭṭhapetvā tadadhigamāya aniccādivasena vipassanaṃ paṭṭhapetvā ussukkāpetumasakkontassa imampi pakkhaṃ, imampi māsaṃ, imampi saṃvaccharaṃ vipassanaṃ ussukkāpetvā ariyabhūmiṃ pāpuṇituṃ nāsakkhinti anusocato uppannaṃ domanassaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Sevitabba</hi>nti idaṃ nekkhammavasena, vipassanāvasena, anussativasena, paṭhamajjhānādivasena ca uppajjanakadomanassaṃ sevitabbaṃ nāma.</p> <hi rend="bold">Evarūpaṃ domanassaṃ sevitabba</hi> <hi rend="bold">Sevitabba</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Tattha <pb ed="V" n="2.0286"/><hi rend="bold">yaṃ ce savitakkasavicāra</hi>nti tasmimpi duvidhe domanasse gehasitadomanassameva savitakkasavicāradomanassaṃ nāma. Nekkhammavasena, vipassanāvasena, anussativasena, paṭhamadutiyajjhānavasena ca uppannadomanassaṃ pana avitakkaavicāradomanassanti veditabbaṃ. Nippariyāyena pana avitakkaavicāradomanassaṃ nāma natthi. Domanassindriyañhi ekaṃsena akusalañceva savitakkasavicārañca, etassa pana bhikkhuno maññanavasena <pb ed="P" n="3.0727"/> savitakkasavicāranti ca avitakkaavicāranti ca vuttaṃ.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0286"/> <hi rend="bold">yaṃ ce savitakkasavicāra</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0727"/> <p rend="bodytext">Tatrāyaṃ <pb ed="M" n="2.0319"/> nayo – idha bhikkhu domanassapaccayabhūte savitakkasavicāradhamme avitakkaavicāradhamme ca domanassapaccayā eva uppanne maggaphaladhamme ca aññesaṃ paṭipattidassanavasena domanassanti gahetvā ‘‘kadā nu kho me savitakkasavicāradomanasse vipassanā paṭṭhapitā bhavissati, kadā avitakkaavicāradomanasse’’ti ca ‘‘kadā nu kho me savitakkasavicāradomanassaphalasamāpatti nibbattitā bhavissati, kadā avitakkaavicāradomanassaphalasamāpattī’’ti cintetvā temāsikaṃ, chamāsikaṃ, navamāsikaṃ vā paṭipadaṃ gaṇhāti. Temāsikaṃ gahetvā paṭhamamāse ekaṃ yāmaṃ jaggati, dve yāme niddāya okāsaṃ karoti, majjhime māse dve yāme jaggati, ekaṃ yāmaṃ niddāya okāsaṃ karoti, pacchimamāse caṅkamanisajjāyeva yāpeti. Evaṃ ce arahattaṃ pāpuṇāti, iccetaṃ kusalaṃ. No ce pāpuṇāti, visesetvā chamāsikaṃ gaṇhāti. Tatrāpi dve dve māse vuttanayena paṭipajjitvā arahattaṃ pāpuṇituṃ asakkonto visesetvā navamāsikaṃ gaṇhāti. Tatrāpi tayo tayo māse tatheva paṭipajjitvā arahattaṃ pāpuṇituṃ asakkontassa ‘‘na ladvaṃ vata me sabrahmacārīhi saddhiṃ visuddhipavāraṇaṃ pavāretu’’nti āvajjato domanassaṃ uppajjati, assudhārā pavattanti gāmantapabbhāravāsīmahāsīvattherassa viya.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0319"/> <p rend="subhead">Mahāsīvattheravatthu</p> <p rend="bodytext">Thero kira aṭṭhārasa mahāgaṇe vācesi. Tassovāde ṭhatvā tiṃsasahassā bhikkhū arahattaṃ pāpuṇiṃsu. Atheko bhikkhu ‘‘mayhaṃ tāva abbhantare guṇā appamāṇā, kīdisā nu kho me ācariyassa guṇā’’ti āvajjanto puthujjanabhāvaṃ passitvā ‘‘amhākaṃ ācariyo aññesaṃ avassayo hoti, attano bhavituṃ na sakkoti, ovādamassa dassāmī’’ti ākāsena gantvā vihārasamīpe otaritvā divāṭṭhāne nisinnaṃ ācariyaṃ upasaṅkamitvā vattaṃ dassetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Thero <pb ed="V" n="2.0287"/> – ‘‘kiṃ kāraṇā āgatosi piṇḍapātikā’’ti āha. Ekaṃ anumodanaṃ gaṇhissāmīti āgatosmi, bhanteti. Okāso na bhavissati, āvusoti? Vitakkamāḷake ṭhitakāle pucchissāmi, bhanteti. Tasmiṃ ṭhāne aññe <pb ed="P" n="3.0728"/> pucchantīti. Bhikkhācāramagge, bhanteti. Tatrāpi aññe pucchantīti. Dupaṭṭanivāsanaṭṭhāne, saṅghāṭipārupanaṭṭhāne, pattanīharaṇaṭṭhāne, gāme <pb ed="M" n="2.0320"/> caritvā āsanasālāyaṃ yāgupītakāle, bhanteti. Tattha aṭṭhakathātherā attano kaṅkhaṃ vinodenti, āvusoti. Antogāmato nikkhantakāle pucchissāmi, bhanteti. Tatrāpi aññe pucchanti, āvusoti. Antarāmagge, bhante, bhojanasālāyaṃ bhattakiccapariyosāne, bhante, divāṭṭhāne, pādadhovanakāle, mukhadhovanakāle, bhanteti? Tadā aññe pucchantīti. Tato paṭṭhāya yāva aruṇā apare pucchanti, āvusoti. Dantakaṭṭhaṃ gahetvā mukhadhovanatthaṃ gamanakāle, bhanteti? Tadā aññe pucchantīti. Mukhaṃ dhovitvā āgamanakāle, bhanteti? Tatrāpi aññe pucchantīti. Senāsanaṃ pavisitvā nisinnakāle, bhanteti? Tatrāpi aññe pucchantīti. Bhante, nanu mukhaṃ dhovitvā senāsanaṃ pavisitvā tayo cattāro pallaṅke usumaṃ gāhāpetvā yonisomanasikāre kammaṃ karontānaṃ okāsakālena bhavitabbaṃ siyā, maraṇakhaṇampi na labhissatha, bhante, phalakasadisattha bhante parassa avassayo hotha, attano bhavituṃ na sakkotha, na me tumhākaṃ anumodanāya atthoti ākāse uppatitvā agamāsi.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0287"/> <pb ed="P" n="3.0728"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0320"/> <p rend="bodytext">Thero – ‘‘imassa bhikkhuno pariyattiyā kammaṃ natthi, mayhaṃ pana aṅkusako bhavissāmīti āgato’’ti ñatvā ‘‘idāni okāso na bhavissati, paccūsakāle gamissāmī’’ti pattacīvaraṃ samīpe katvā sabbaṃ divasabhāgaṃ paṭhamayāmamajjhimayāmañca dhammaṃ vācetvā pacchimayāme ekasmiṃ there uddesaṃ gahetvā nikkhante pattacīvaraṃ gahetvā teneva saddhiṃ nikkhanto. Nisinnaantevāsikā ācariyo kenaci papañcena nikkhantoti maññiṃsu. Nikkhanto thero koci deva samānācariyabhikkhūti saññaṃ akāsi.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Thero kira ‘‘mādisassa arahattaṃ nāma kiṃ, dvīhatīheneva pāpuṇitvā paccāgamissāmī’’ti antevāsikānaṃ anārocetvāva āsāḷhīmāsassa juṇhapakkhaterasiyā nikkhanto gāmantapabbhāraṃ gantvā caṅkamaṃ āruyha kammaṭṭhānaṃ manasikaronto taṃ divasaṃ arahattaṃ gahetuṃ nāsakkhi. Uposathadivase sampatte ‘‘dvīhatīhena arahattaṃ gaṇhissāmīti āgato <pb ed="P" n="3.0729"/>, gahetuṃ pana nāsakkhiṃ. Tayo māse pana tīṇi divasāni viya yāva mahāpavāraṇā tāva jānissāmī’’ti vassaṃ upagantvāpi gahetuṃ nāsakkhi. Pavāraṇādivase cintesi – ‘‘ahaṃ dvīhatīhena <pb ed="V" n="2.0288"/> arahattaṃ gaṇhissāmīti āgato <pb ed="M" n="2.0321"/>, temāsenāpi nāsakkhiṃ, sabrahmacārino pana visuddhipavāraṇaṃ pavārentī’’ti. Tassevaṃ cintayato assudhārā pavattanti. Tato ‘‘na mañce mayhaṃ catūhi iriyāpathehi maggaphalaṃ uppajjissati, arahattaṃ appatvā neva mañce piṭṭhiṃ pasāressāmi, na pāde dhovissāmī’’ti mañcaṃ ussāpetvā ṭhapesi. Puna antovassaṃ pattaṃ, arahattaṃ gahetuṃ nāsakkhiyeva. Ekūnatiṃsapavāraṇāsu assudhārā pavattanti. Gāmadārakā therassa pādesu phālitaṭṭhānāni kaṇṭakehi sibbanti, davaṃ karontāpi ‘‘ayyassa mahāsīvattherassa viya pādā hontū’’ti davaṃ karonti.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0729"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0288"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0321"/> <p rend="bodytext">Thero tiṃsasaṃvacchare mahāpavāraṇādivase ālambaṇaphalakaṃ nissāya ṭhito ‘‘idāni me tiṃsa vassāni samaṇadhammaṃ karontassa, nāsakkhiṃ arahattaṃ pāpuṇituṃ, addhā me imasmiṃ attabhāve maggo vā phalaṃ vā natthi, na me laddhaṃ sabrahmacārīhi saddhiṃ visuddhipavāraṇaṃ pavāretu’’nti cintesi. Tassevaṃ cintayatova domanassaṃ uppajji, assudhārā pavattanti. Atha avidūraṭṭhāne ekā devadhītā rodamānā aṭṭhāsi. ‘‘Ko ettha rodasī’’ti? ‘‘Ahaṃ, bhante, devadhītā’’ti. ‘‘Kasmā rodasī’’ti? ‘‘Rodamānena maggaphalaṃ nibbattitaṃ, tena ahampi ekaṃ dve maggaphalāni nibbattessāmīti rodāmi, bhante’’ti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Tato thero – ‘‘bho mahāsīvatthera, devatāpi tayā saddhiṃ keḷiṃ karonti, anucchavikaṃ nu kho te eta’’nti vipassanaṃ vaḍḍhetvā saha paṭisambhidāhi arahattaṃ aggahesi. So ‘‘idāni nipajjissāmī’’ti senāsanaṃ paṭijaggitvā mañcakaṃ paññapetvā udakaṭṭhāne udakaṃ paccupaṭṭhapetvā ‘‘pāde dhovissāmī’’ti sopānaphalake nisīdi.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Antevāsikāpissa ‘‘amhākaṃ ācariyassa samaṇadhammaṃ kātuṃ gacchantassa tiṃsa vassāni, sakkhi nu kho visesaṃ nibbattetuṃ, nāsakkhī’’ti āvajjayamānā ‘‘arahattaṃ patvā pādadhovanatthaṃ nisinno’’ti disvā ‘‘amhākaṃ ācariyo amhādisesu antevāsikesu tiṭṭhantesu ‘attanāva pāde dhovissatī’ti aṭṭhānametaṃ, ahaṃ dhovissāmi ahaṃ dhovissāmī’’ti tiṃsasahassānipi ākāsena gantvā vanditvā ‘‘pāde <pb ed="P" n="3.0730"/> dhovissāma, bhante’’ti <pb ed="M" n="2.0322"/> āhaṃsu. Āvuso, idāni tiṃsa vassāni honti mama pādānaṃ adhotānaṃ, tiṭṭhatha, tumhe, ahameva dhovissāmīti.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0730"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0322"/> <p rend="bodytext">Sakkopi āvajjanto – ‘‘mayhaṃ ayyo mahāsīvatthero arahattaṃ patto tiṃsasahassānaṃ antevāsikānaṃ ‘pāde dhovissāmā’ti āgatānaṃ pāde dhovituṃ na deti. Mādise pana upaṭṭhāke tiṭṭhante <pb ed="V" n="2.0289"/> ‘mayhaṃ ayyo sayaṃ pāde dhovissatī’ti aṭṭhānametaṃ, ahaṃ dhovissāmī’’ti sanniṭṭhānaṃ katvā sujātāya deviyā saddhiṃ bhikkhusaṅghassa santike pāturahosi. So sujaṃ asurakaññaṃ purato katvā ‘‘apetha, bhante, mātugāmo’’ti okāsaṃ kāretvā theraṃ upasaṅkamitvā vanditvā purato ukkuṭiko nisīditvā ‘‘pāde dhovissāmi, bhante’’ti āha. Kosiya, idāni me tiṃsa vassāni pādānaṃ adhotānaṃ, devatānañca pakatiyāpi manussasarīragandho nāma jeguccho, yojanasate ṭhitānampi kaṇṭhe āsattakuṇapaṃ viya hoti, ahameva dhovissāmīti. Bhante, ayaṃ gandho nāma na paññāyati, tumhākaṃ pana sīlagandho cha devaloke atikkamitvā upari bhavaggaṃ patvā ṭhito. Sīlagandhato añño uttaritaro gandho nāma natthi, bhante, tumhākaṃ sīlagandhenamhi āgatoti vāmahatthena gopphakasandhiyaṃ gahetvā dakkhiṇahatthena pādatalaṃ parimajji. Daharakumārasseva pādā ahesuṃ. Sakko pāde dhovitvā vanditvā devalokameva gato.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0289"/> <p rend="bodytext">Evaṃ ‘‘na labhāmi sabrahmacārīhi saddhiṃ visuddhipavāraṇaṃ pavāretu’’nti āvajjantassa uppannaṃ domanassaṃ nissāya bhikkhuno maññanavasena vipassanāya ārammaṇampi vipassanāpi maggopi phalampi savitakkasavicāradomanassanti ca avitakkāvicāradomanassanti ca vuttanti veditabbaṃ.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Tattha eko bhikkhu savitakkasavicāradomanasse vipassanaṃ paṭṭhapetvā idaṃ domanassaṃ kiṃ nissitanti upadhārento vatthunissitanti pajānātīti phassapañcamake vuttanayeneva anukkamena arahatte patiṭṭhāti. Eko avitakkāvicāre domanasse vipassanaṃ paṭṭhapetvā vuttanayeneva arahatte patiṭṭhāti. Tattha abhiniviṭṭhadomanassesupi savitakkasavicārato avitakkaavicāraṃ paṇītataraṃ. Savitakkasavicāradomanassavipassanātopi avitakkāvicāradomanassavipassanā <pb ed="P" n="3.0731"/> paṇītatarā. Savitakkasavicāradomanassaphalasamāpattitopi avitakkāvicāradomanassaphalasamāpattiyeva paṇītatarā <pb ed="M" n="2.0323"/>. Tenāha bhagavā – ‘‘ye avitakkaavicāre te paṇītatare’’ti.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0731"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0323"/>
"This kind of grief should not be cultivated" means this kind of grief based on the home life should not be cultivated.

What is grief based on the home life? "What are the six kinds of grief based on the home life? Grief arises when one either regards as a privation failure to obtain visible objects cognizable by the eye that are sought after, desired, agreeable, gratifying and associated with worldliness, or when one recalls those formerly obtained that are past, ceased and changed. Such grief as this is called grief based on the home life” (Majjhima Nikāya 3.307). Thus, grief arises in the six sense doors when reflecting, "I have not experienced, will not experience, and do not experience a desirable object," grief that relies on the qualities of sensuality.

"This kind of grief should be cultivated" means this kind of grief based on renunciation should be cultivated.

What is grief based on renunciation? "What are the six kinds of grief based on renunciation? Knowing the impermanence, change, cessation, and ending of visible objects, and seeing with proper wisdom that all those visible objects, both past and present, are impermanent, suffering, and subject to change, one establishes longing for the unsurpassed liberations, thinking, 'When will I dwell having attained that state, which the noble ones now dwell having attained?'" Thus, grief arises due to longing when establishing longing for the unsurpassed liberations. Such grief as this is called grief based on renunciation (Majjhima Nikāya 3.307). Thus, having established longing for the noble fruits known as the unsurpassed liberations when a desirable object has entered the range of the six sense doors, and having initiated insight based on impermanence etc. for the attainment of that [fruit], grief arises when one laments, being unable to generate effort, "I was not able to attain the noble ground even after generating effort for insight in this fortnight, in this month, in this year." This grief that arises should be cultivated. This means grief that arises through renunciation, through insight, through mindfulness, and through the first *jhāna* and so on should be cultivated.

"This kind of grief should be cultivated."

There, "that which is with applied and sustained thought" means that in those two kinds of grief, only grief based on the home life is grief with applied and sustained thought. But grief that has arisen through renunciation, through insight, through mindfulness, and through the first and second *jhānas* should be understood as grief without applied and sustained thought. However, there is no grief without applied and sustained thought in an ultimate sense. For the faculty of grief is entirely unwholesome and with applied and sustained thought. But for this *bhikkhu*, it is said to be with applied and sustained thought and without applied and sustained thought based on his perception.

Here is the method: Here, a *bhikkhu*, having taken grief as grief, in dependence on both phenomena with applied and sustained thought and phenomena without applied and sustained thought which are the causes of grief, and also in dependence on the path and fruit phenomena that arise from grief, due to showing the practice to others, thinks, "When will insight be established in me in grief with applied and sustained thought? When [will it be established] in grief without applied and sustained thought?" and "When will the attainment of the fruit of grief with applied and sustained thought be produced in me? When [will] the attainment of the fruit of grief without applied and sustained thought?" and undertakes a three-month, six-month, or nine-month practice.

Having undertaken [the practice] for three months, in the first month he remains awake for one watch, makes room for sleep for two watches; in the middle month he remains awake for two watches, makes room for sleep for one watch; in the last month he spends [the time] only walking back and forth and sitting. If in this way he attains *arahatship*, that is skillful. If he does not attain it, he undertakes a six-month [practice] more intensely. There also, having practiced in the manner stated for two months at a time, if he is unable to attain *arahatship*, he undertakes a nine-month [practice] more intensely. There also, having practiced in the same way for three months at a time, if he is unable to attain *arahatship*, grief arises for him when he reflects, "I have not obtained the opportunity to invite the purification ceremony together with my fellow practitioners," and tears flow, like in the story of the Elder Mahāsīva who lived in a village bordering a mountain.

The Story of the Elder Mahāsīva

It is said that the Elder would recite the eighteen great sections [of scripture]. Thirty thousand *bhikkhus* attained *arahatship* standing in his instruction. Then one *bhikkhu*, reflecting, "My qualities are immeasurable internally, what are the qualities of my teacher?" saw his state as an ordinary person and [thought], "Our teacher is a refuge for others, but is not able to be [a refuge] for himself; I will give him instruction." Going through the air, he descended near the monastery, approached the teacher sitting in his daytime dwelling, showed respect, and sat down to one side.

The Elder said, "Why have you come, alms mendicant?"

"I have come to receive a blessing, Venerable Sir."

"There will be no opportunity, friend."

"I will ask when you are dwelling in the realm of thought, Venerable Sir."

At that place, other *bhikkhus*.
They ask questions."
"On the alms-round path, venerable sir."
"There too, others ask questions, reverend sir."
"At the place for putting on the lower robe, at the place for putting on the outer robe, at the place for taking out the bowl, in the village..."
"...having wandered, in the alms hall at the time for drinking rice gruel, venerable sir."
"There, the elders who are commentators dispel their doubt, friend."
"At the time of going out from the inner village, I will ask, venerable sir."
"There too, others ask questions, friend."
"In between the roads, venerable sir, in the dining hall, at the end of the meal duties, venerable sir, in the day dwelling, at the time of washing the feet, at the time of washing the face, venerable sir? Then others ask questions."
"From then on, until dawn, others ask questions, friend."
"Having taken a toothpick, at the time of going for washing the face, venerable sir? Then others ask questions."
"Having washed the face, at the time of returning, venerable sir? There too, others ask questions."
"Having entered the dwelling, at the time of sitting down, venerable sir? There too, others ask questions."
"Venerable sir, surely, having washed the face, having entered the dwelling, having warmed up three or four couches, for those who are doing deeds with wise attention, there should be a time for seclusion, you will not obtain even a moment to die; venerable sir, like a fruit, venerable sir, you are dependent on others, you are not able to be your own, I have no use for your approval," rising up into the sky, he left.

The elder – knowing that "This monk has no task of learning, but has come thinking, 'I will be an attendant for him'," – thinking, "Now there will be no opportunity, I will go at dawn," having placed the bowl and robe nearby, having had the Dhamma recited for the entire day, the first watch, and the middle watch, when one elder had taken the topic in the last watch and departed, having taken the bowl and robe, he departed together with him.
The resident pupils thought, "The teacher has left due to some hindrance."
The elder who had left made a sign that some deva was a co-teacher monk.

The elder, it seems, without informing the resident pupils, thinking, "What is it to me, this attainment of arahantship? I will attain it in two or three days and return," having departed on the thirteenth day of the bright fortnight of Āsāḷhī month, having gone to the edge of the village mountain, having ascended the walking path, applying his mind to the meditation subject, he was not able to attain arahantship that day.
