When the uposatha day arrived, he thought, "I have come thinking, 'I will attain arahantship in two or three days'," but he was not able to attain it.
For three months, thinking, "I will know in three days," having undertaken the rains retreat, he was not able to attain it.
On the Pavāraṇā day, he thought – "I have come thinking, 'I will attain arahantship in two or three days', but I was not able to in three months; but the fellow ব্রহ্মচারies are carrying out the purification ceremony of Pavāraṇā."
While he was thinking thus, tears flowed.
Then, "Not on a couch, with my four postures, will the fruit of the path arise; not having attained arahantship, I will never stretch out my back on a couch, I will not wash my feet," having had the couch removed, he put it away.
Again, the inner rains retreat arrived, he was not at all able to attain arahantship.
On twenty-nine Pavāraṇās, tears flowed.
The village children sew the split places on the elder's feet with thorns, even while playing, they play, "May the feet be like those of venerable Mahāsīvatthera."

The elder, in his thirtieth year, on the Mahāpavāraṇā day, standing relying on a support plank, "Now, for me, thirty years of doing the ascetic life, I was not able to attain arahantship, truly, in this existence, there is no path or fruit for me, I have not obtained to carry out the purification Pavāraṇā together with the fellow ব্রহ্মচারies," he thought.
While he was thinking thus, sadness arose, tears flowed.
Then, not far away, a certain deva daughter stood weeping.
"Who is weeping here?" "I, venerable sir, am a deva daughter."
"Why are you weeping?" "By weeping, the fruit of the path was produced, therefore, I too will produce one or two fruits of the path, I am weeping, venerable sir."
Then the elder said, "Reverend Mahāsīva, even the deities are sporting with you; is this unbecoming to you?" Having developed insight, he attained *arhatship* along with the discriminations.

He thought, "Now I shall lie down," and having prepared his lodging, arranged a couch, prepared water in the water receptacle, and sat down on the step-stone, thinking, "I will wash my feet."

His pupils, considering, "For thirty years our teacher has been going to perform the duties of a *śramaṇa*; is he able to produce a distinction, or is he not able?" and seeing him seated to wash his feet after attaining *arhatship*, [thought], "It is improper that our teacher should wash his own feet while such pupils as us are standing by; I will wash [them], I will wash [them]!" Even thirty thousand of them, going through the sky, venerated him and said, "We will wash your feet, *bhante*."

"Friends, for thirty years my feet have not been washed; stay here, you, I myself will wash them."

Sakka, considering, "My teacher, the revered Mahāsīva, having attained *arhatship*, does not allow the thirty thousand pupils who have come saying, 'We will wash your feet,' to wash his feet.

While such attendants as me are standing by, it is improper that my teacher should wash his own feet," having made a resolution, appeared with Sujātā, the goddess, near the community of monks.

He, having placed Sujā, the *asura* maiden, in front, having made an opportunity saying, "Step aside, *bhante*, this is a woman," approached the elder, venerated him, sat down squatting in front, and said, "I will wash your feet, *bhante*."

"Kosiya, for thirty years my feet have not been washed, and for the deities, even by nature, the smell of a human body is disgusting; even those standing a hundred *yojanas* away feel as if a rotting corpse is stuck in their throats, I myself will wash them."

"*Bhante*, this smell is not apparent; but your virtue-fragrance, having surpassed the six heavens, stands above the peak of existence.

There is no other fragrance more excellent than the fragrance of virtue; *bhante*, I have come because of your virtue-fragrance," with his left hand he grasped the ankle joint, and with his right hand he massaged the sole of the foot.

His feet were like those of a young boy.

Sakka, having washed his feet, venerated him and went to the world of the gods.

Thus, it should be understood that due to the sadness that arose in him thinking, "I am not able to observe the *pravāraṇa* ceremony of purification together with my fellow *brahmacārins*," depending on the monk's consideration, the object of insight, the insight, the path, and the fruit are said to be with initial application and sustained application-sadness, and without initial application and sustained application-sadness.

There, a certain monk, having established insight in sadness with initial application and sustained application, realizing, "This sadness depends on what?" understands that it depends on a thing; thus, according to the method stated in the *phassapañcamaka*, he gradually attains *arhatship*.

Another, having established insight in sadness without initial application and sustained application, attains *arhatship* according to the stated method.

There, even among the sadnesses that are clung to, that without initial application and sustained application is more excellent than that with initial application and sustained application.

Even from insight into sadness with initial application and sustained application, insight into sadness without initial application and sustained application is more excellent.

Even from the attainment of the fruit of sadness with initial application and sustained application, the attainment of the fruit of sadness without initial application and sustained application is more excellent.

Therefore, the Blessed One said, "Those without initial application and sustained application are more excellent."
362
<p n="362" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">362</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> ‘‘Upekkhaṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi sevitabbampi, asevitabbampīti iti kho panetaṃ vuttaṃ, kiñcetaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ? Tattha yaṃ jaññā upekkhaṃ ‘imaṃ kho me upekkhaṃ sevato <pb ed="T" n="2.0314"/> akusalā dhammā abhivaḍḍhanti, kusalā dhammā parihāyantī’ti, evarūpā upekkhā <pb ed="V" n="2.0206"/> na sevitabbā. Tattha yaṃ jaññā upekkhaṃ ‘imaṃ kho me upekkhaṃ sevato akusalā dhammā parihāyanti, kusalā dhammā abhivaḍḍhantī’ti, evarūpā upekkhā sevitabbā. Tattha yaṃ ce savitakkaṃ savicāraṃ, yaṃ ce avitakkaṃ avicāraṃ, ye avitakke avicāre, te paṇītatare. Upekkhaṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi sevitabbampi, asevitabbampīti iti yaṃ taṃ vuttaṃ, idametaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ.</p>
362.
"I, Devānaminda, declare equanimity to be of two kinds: to be cultivated and not to be cultivated." So it was said. With reference to what was this said? Therein, whatever equanimity one recognizes, saying, 'When I cultivate this equanimity, unwholesome *dharmas* increase and wholesome *dharmas* decline,' such equanimity is not to be cultivated.

Therein, whatever equanimity one recognizes, saying, 'When I cultivate this equanimity, unwholesome *dharmas* decline and wholesome *dharmas* increase,' such equanimity is to be cultivated.

Now, of such happiness as is accompanied by thinking (*savitakka*) and pondering (*savicāra*), and of that which is not so accompanied (*avitakka* *avicāra*), the latter is the more excellent (*praṇītatare*).

"I, Devānaminda, declare equanimity to be of two kinds: to be cultivated and not to be cultivated." So it was said. It was with reference to this that it was said.
<p n="362" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">362</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Evarūpā upekkhā na sevitabbā</hi>ti evarūpā gehasitaupekkhā na sevitabbā. Gehasitaupekkhā nāma ‘‘tattha katamā cha gehasitaupekkhā. Cakkhunā rūpaṃ disvā uppajjati upekkhā bālassa mūḷhassa puthujjanassa anodhijinassa avipākajinassa anādīnavadassāvino assutavato puthujjanassa, yā evarūpā upekkhā, rūpaṃ sā nātivattati, tasmā sā upekkhā gehasitāti vuccatī’’ti evaṃ chasu dvāresu iṭṭhārammaṇe āpāthagate guḷapiṇḍike nilīnamakkhikā <pb ed="V" n="2.0290"/> viya rūpādīni anativattamānā tattheva laggā laggitā hutvā uppannā kāmaguṇanissitā upekkhā na sevitabbā.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Evarūpā upekkhā sevitabbā</hi>ti evarūpā nekkhammasitā upekkhā sevitabbā. Nekkhammasitā upekkhā nāma – ‘‘tattha katamā cha nekkhammasitā upekkhā? Rūpānaṃ tveva aniccataṃ viditvā vipariṇāmavirāganirodhaṃ ‘pubbe ceva rūpā etarahi ca, sabbe te rūpā aniccā, dukkhā, vipariṇāmadhammā’ti evametaṃ yathābhūtaṃ sammappaññāya passato uppajjati upekkhā, yā evarūpā upekkhā, rūpaṃ sā ativattati, tasmā sā upekkhā nekkhammasitāti vuccatī’’ti (ma. ni. 3.308). Evaṃ chasu dvāresu iṭṭhāniṭṭhaārammaṇe āpāthagate iṭṭhe arajjantassa, aniṭṭhe adussantassa, asamapekkhanena asammuyhantassa uppannā vipassanā ñāṇasampayuttā upekkhā. Apica vedanāsabhāgā tatra majjhattupekkhāpi ettha upekkhāva. Tasmā <hi rend="bold">sevitabbā</hi>ti ayaṃ nekkhammavasena vipassanāvasena anussatiṭṭhānavasena paṭhamadutiyatatiyacatutthajjhānavasena ca uppajjanakaupekkhā sevitabbā nāma.</p> <hi rend="bold">Evarūpā upekkhā sevitabbā</hi> <hi rend="bold">sevitabbā</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Ettha <hi rend="bold">yaṃ ce savitakkaṃ savicāra</hi>nti tāyapi nekkhammasitaupekkhāya yaṃ nekkhammavasena vipassanāvasena anussatiṭṭhānavasena paṭhamajjhānavasena ca uppannaṃ savitakkasavicāraṃ upekkhanti jāneyya. <hi rend="bold">Yaṃ ce avitakkaṃ avicāra</hi>nti yaṃ pana dutiyajjhānādivasena uppannaṃ avitakkāvicāraṃ upekkhanti jāneyya. <hi rend="bold">Ye</hi><pb ed="P" n="3.0732"/><hi rend="bold">avitakke avicāre te paṇītatare</hi>ti etāsu dvīsu yā avitakkaavicārā, sā paṇītatarāti attho. Iminā kiṃ kathitaṃ hoti <pb ed="M" n="2.0324"/>? Dvinnaṃ arahattaṃ kathitaṃ. Eko hi bhikkhu savitakkasavicāraupekkhāya vipassanaṃ paṭṭhapetvā ayaṃ upekkhā kiṃ nissitāti upadhārento vatthunissitāti pajānātīti phassapañcamake vuttanayeneva anukkamena arahatte patiṭṭhāti. Eko avitakkāvicārāya upekkhāya vipassanaṃ paṭṭhapetvā vuttanayeneva arahatte patiṭṭhāti. Tattha abhiniviṭṭhaupekkhāsupi savitakkasavicārato avitakkāvicārā paṇītatarā. Savitakkasavicāraupekkhāvipassanātopi avitakkāvicāraupekkhāvipassanāpaṇītatarā. Savitakkasavicāraupekkhāphalasamāpattitopi avitakkāvicārupekkhāphalasamāpattiyeva paṇītatarā. Tenāha bhagavā ‘‘ye avitakke avicāre te paṇītatare’’ti.</p> <hi rend="bold">yaṃ ce savitakkaṃ savicāra</hi> <hi rend="bold">Yaṃ ce avitakkaṃ avicāra</hi> <hi rend="bold">Ye</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0732"/> <hi rend="bold">avitakke avicāre te paṇītatare</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0324"/>
362. "Such equanimity should not be cultivated" means such equanimity associated with household life should not be cultivated.

What is equanimity associated with household life?

"Here, what are the six kinds of equanimity associated with household life?

When a foolish, infatuated ordinary person, an untaught ordinary person who has not conquered his limitations, who has not conquered future *kamma* result, who is unperceiving of danger, sees a visible object with the eye, equanimity arises. Such equanimity as this does not surmount the visible object; therefore, that equanimity is called associated with household life." Thus, in the six sense-doors, when an agreeable object comes into the range [of perception], like a fly stuck on a lump of jaggery, not surmounting forms and so on, but being attached and clinging to them, equanimity dependent on the qualities of sensual pleasure that has arisen should not be cultivated.

"Such equanimity should be cultivated" means such equanimity associated with renunciation should be cultivated.

What is equanimity associated with renunciation?

"Here, what are the six kinds of equanimity associated with renunciation? Knowing the impermanence of forms, their change, detachment, and cessation, 'Both formerly and now, all those forms are impermanent, suffering, and subject to change,' thus, when equanimity arises in one who sees this as it really is with right wisdom, such equanimity as this surmounts form; therefore, that equanimity is called associated with renunciation" (M. III. 308).

Thus, in the six sense-doors, when an agreeable or disagreeable object comes into the range [of perception], equanimity conjoined with insight knowledge that arises in one who is not attached to the agreeable, who is not hostile to the disagreeable, and who is not confused by improper reflection. Moreover, the equanimity that is a part of feeling is also equanimity here.

Therefore, "should be cultivated" means that equanimity associated with renunciation, associated with insight, associated with the places of mindfulness, and associated with the first, second, third, and fourth *jhānas* should be cultivated.

"Such equanimity should be cultivated, should be cultivated."

Here, "whatever is with applied and sustained thought" should be known as the equanimity with applied and sustained thought that has arisen through that equanimity associated with renunciation, associated with insight, associated with the places of mindfulness, and associated with the first *jhāna*.

"Whatever is without applied and sustained thought" should be known as the equanimity without applied and sustained thought that has arisen through the second *jhāna* and so on.

"Those that are without applied thought and without sustained thought are more sublime" means that of these two, that which is without applied thought and without sustained thought is more sublime.

What is said by this? The *arahatship* of two [types of individuals] is described.

For one *bhikkhu*, having established insight with equanimity with applied and sustained thought, contemplating, "On what is this equanimity dependent?" knows "It is dependent on a basis," and in the same way as stated in the *Phassapañcamaka*, he is established in *arahatship* in due course.

Another, having established insight with equanimity without applied and sustained thought, is established in *arahatship* in the way stated.

There, even among equanimities which are clung to, that without applied and sustained thought is more sublime than that with applied and sustained thought.

Insight [meditation] with equanimity without applied and sustained thought is more sublime than insight [meditation] with equanimity with applied and sustained thought.

The attainment of fruition of equanimity without applied and sustained thought is more sublime than the attainment of fruition of equanimity with applied and sustained thought.

Therefore, the Blessed One said, "Those that are without applied thought and without sustained thought are more sublime."

"Whatever is with applied and sustained thought, whatever is without applied and sustained thought, those that are without applied thought and without sustained thought are more sublime."
363
<p n="363" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">363</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> ‘‘Evaṃ paṭipanno kho, devānaminda, bhikkhu papañcasaññāsaṅkhānirodhasāruppagāminiṃ paṭipadaṃ paṭipanno hotī’’ti. Itthaṃ bhagavā sakkassa devānamindassa pañhaṃ puṭṭho byākāsi. Attamano sakko devānamindo bhagavato bhāsitaṃ <pb ed="M" n="2.0223"/> abhinandi anumodi – ‘‘evametaṃ, bhagavā, evametaṃ, sugata, tiṇṇā mettha kaṅkhā vigatā kathaṃkathā bhagavato pañhaveyyākaraṇaṃ sutvā’’ti.</p> <p rend="subhead">Pātimokkhasaṃvaro</p>
"And this, Lord of the Gods, is the practice that a *bhikṣu* has undertaken, a practice that leads to conformity with the cessation of proliferation (*prapañcasaññāsaṅkhānirodhasāruppagāminiṃ paṭipadaṃ paṭipanno hoti*)."

Thus, the Blessed One answered Śakra, the Lord of the Gods, when asked this question.

Delighted, Śakra, the Lord of the Gods, rejoiced at and approved of the Blessed One's words, saying, "So it is, Blessed One, so it is, O Sugata! My doubts are dispelled, my uncertainty is gone, having heard the Blessed One's explanation of the question."

The restraint of the *prātimokṣa*.
<p n="363" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">363</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Evaṃ paṭipanno kho, devānaminda, bhikkhu papañcasaññāsaṅkhānirodhasāruppagāminiṃ paṭipadaṃ paṭipanno hotī</hi>ti bhagavā arahattanikūṭena desanaṃ niṭṭhapesi. Sakko pana sotāpattiphalaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0291"/> patto. Buddhānañhi ajjhāsayo hīno na hoti, ukkaṭṭhova hoti. Ekassapi bahūnampi dhammaṃ desentā arahatteneva kūṭaṃ gaṇhanti. Sattā pana attano anurūpe upanissaye ṭhitā keci sotāpannā honti, keci sakadāgāmī, keci anāgāmī, keci arahanto. Rājā viya hi bhagavā, rājakumārā viya veneyyā. Yathā hi rājā bhojanakāle attano pamāṇena piṇḍaṃ uddharitvā rājakumārānaṃ upaneti, te tato attano mukhappamāṇeneva kabaḷaṃ karonti, evaṃ bhagavā attajjhāsayānurūpāya desanāya arahatteneva kūṭaṃ gaṇhāti. Veneyyā attano upanissayappamāṇena tato sotāpattiphalamattaṃ vā sakadāgāmianāgāmiarahattaphalameva vā gaṇhanti. Sakko pana sotāpanno jāto. Sotāpanno ca hutvā bhagavato puratoyeva cavitvā taruṇasakko hutvā nibbatti, devatānañhi cavamānānaṃ attabhāvassa gatāgataṭṭhānaṃ nāma na paññāyati, dīpasikhāgamanaṃ viya hoti. Tasmā sesadevatā na jāniṃsu. Sakko pana sayaṃ cutattā bhagavā ca appaṭihatañāṇattā dveva janā jāniṃsu. Atha sakko cintesi <pb ed="P" n="3.0733"/> ‘‘mayhañhi bhagavatā tīsu ṭhānesu nibbattitaphalameva kathitaṃ, ayañca pana maggo vā phalaṃ vā sakuṇikāya viya uppatitvā gahetuṃ na sakkā, āgamanīyapubbabhāgapaṭipadāya assa bhavitabbaṃ. Handāhaṃ upari khīṇāsavassa pubbabhāgapaṭipadaṃ pucchāmī’’ti.</p> <p rend="subhead">Pātimokkhasaṃvaravaṇṇanā</p>
Thus behaving, Indra of the Gods, a monk is behaving in a way that leads to the cessation of perceptions and notions, and is in accordance with the state of an Arhat." Thus, the Blessed One concluded the teaching with the highest point of Arahantship.

Sakka, however, attained the fruit of stream-entry (srotaāpattiphala). For the Buddhas' intention is never inferior, it is always supreme. While teaching the Dharma to one or many, they take their stand on Arahantship itself. But beings, abiding in a suitable condition according to their own potential, some become stream-enterers (srotaāpanna), some once-returners (sakadāgāmī), some non-returners (anāgāmī), some Arhats. For the Blessed One is like a king, and those to be trained are like princes. Just as a king, at mealtime, takes a portion according to his own measure and offers it to the princes, and they then make a mouthful according to the measure of their own mouth, so too, the Blessed One, with a teaching in accordance with his own intention, takes his stand on Arahantship itself. Those to be trained, according to the measure of their own potential, take from it only the fruit of stream-entry (srotaāpattiphala), or only the fruit of once-returner (sakṛdāgāmiphala), non-returner (anāgāmiphala), or Arhatship.

Sakka, however, was born a stream-enterer (srotaāpanna). And having become a stream-enterer, he passed away right in front of the Blessed One and was reborn as a young Sakka. For the destination and place of arrival of beings who are passing away are not evident, it is like the path of a flame. Therefore, the remaining deities did not know. Sakka, however, because he himself had passed away, and the Blessed One, because of his unobstructed knowledge, these two people knew.

Then Sakka thought: "Indeed, the Blessed One has spoken to me only of the fruit that arises in the three places. But this path or fruit cannot be seized by flying up like a bird; it must be through a practice that is a preliminary part of the path that is to be arrived at. Come, let me ask about the preliminary practice of an Arhat who has destroyed the āsavas."

Explanation of the Pātimokkha restraint
364
<p n="364" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">364</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Itiha sakko devānamindo bhagavato bhāsitaṃ abhinanditvā anumoditvā bhagavantaṃ uttariṃ pañhaṃ apucchi –</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Kathaṃ paṭipanno pana, mārisa, bhikkhu pātimokkhasaṃvarāya paṭipanno hotī’’ti? ‘‘Kāyasamācāraṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi – sevitabbampi, asevitabbampi. Vacīsamācāraṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi – sevitabbampi, asevitabbampi. Pariyesanaṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi – sevitabbampi, asevitabba’’mpi.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Kāyasamācāraṃpāhaṃ <pb ed="P" n="2.0280"/>, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi sevitabbampi asevitabbampīti iti kho panetaṃ vuttaṃ, kiñcetaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ? Tattha yaṃ jaññā kāyasamācāraṃ ‘imaṃ kho me kāyasamācāraṃ sevato akusalā dhammā abhivaḍḍhanti, kusalā dhammā parihāyantī’ti, evarūpo kāyasamācāro na sevitabbo. Tattha yaṃ jaññā kāyasamācāraṃ ‘imaṃ kho me kāyasamācāraṃ <pb ed="T" n="2.0315"/> sevato akusalā dhammā parihāyanti, kusalā dhammā abhivaḍḍhantī’ti, evarūpo kāyasamācāro sevitabbo. Kāyasamācāraṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi – sevitabbampi, asevitabbampīti iti yaṃ taṃ vuttaṃ, idametaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ.</p> <pb ed="P" n="2.0280"/> <pb ed="T" n="2.0315"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Vacīsamācāraṃpāhaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0207"/>, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi – sevitabbampi, asevitabbampī’ti. Iti kho panetaṃ vuttaṃ, kiñcetaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ? Tattha yaṃ jaññā vacīsamācāraṃ ‘imaṃ kho me vacīsamācāraṃ sevato akusalā dhammā abhivaḍḍhanti, kusalā dhammā parihāyantī’ti, evarūpo vacīsamācāro na sevitabbo. Tattha yaṃ jaññā vacīsamācāraṃ ‘imaṃ kho me vacīsamācāraṃ sevato akusalā dhammā parihāyanti, kusalā dhammā abhivaḍḍhantī’ti, evarūpo vacīsamācāro sevitabbo. Vacīsamācāraṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi – sevitabbampi, asevitabbampīti iti yaṃ taṃ vuttaṃ, idametaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0207"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Pariyesanaṃpāhaṃ <pb ed="M" n="2.0224"/>, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi – sevitabbampi, asevitabbampīti iti kho panetaṃ vuttaṃ, kiñcetaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ? Tattha yaṃ jaññā pariyesanaṃ ‘imaṃ kho me pariyesanaṃ sevato akusalā dhammā abhivaḍḍhanti, kusalā dhammā parihāyantī’ti, evarūpā pariyesanā na sevitabbā. Tattha yaṃ jaññā pariyesanaṃ ‘imaṃ <pb ed="T" n="2.0316"/> kho me pariyesanaṃ sevato akusalā dhammā parihāyanti, kusalā dhammā abhivaḍḍhantī’ti, evarūpā pariyesanā sevitabbā. Pariyesanaṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi – sevitabbampi, asevitabbampīti iti yaṃ taṃ vuttaṃ, idametaṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0224"/> <pb ed="T" n="2.0316"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Evaṃ <pb ed="P" n="2.0281"/> paṭipanno kho, devānaminda, bhikkhu pātimokkhasaṃvarāya paṭipanno hotī’’ti. Itthaṃ bhagavā sakkassa devānamindassa pañhaṃ puṭṭho byākāsi. Attamano sakko devānamindo bhagavato bhāsitaṃ abhinandi anumodi – ‘‘evametaṃ, bhagavā, evametaṃ, sugata. Tiṇṇā mettha kaṅkhā vigatā kathaṃkathā bhagavato pañhaveyyākaraṇaṃ sutvā’’ti.</p> <pb ed="P" n="2.0281"/> <p rend="subhead">Indriyasaṃvaro</p>
364. Then Śakra, the ruler of the gods, having rejoiced and approved of the Blessed One's words, asked the Blessed One a further question:

"How, sir, does a *bhikṣu* practice so as to be practicing the restraint of the *prātimokṣa*?"

"Ruler of the Gods, I declare that there are two kinds of bodily conduct (*kāyasamācāra*): the kind to be pursued (*sevitabba*), and the kind to be avoided (*asevitabba*).

Ruler of the Gods, I declare that there are two kinds of verbal conduct (*vacīsamācāra*): the kind to be pursued, and the kind to be avoided.

Ruler of the Gods, I declare that there are two kinds of seeking (*pariyesana*): the kind to be pursued, and the kind to be avoided."

"Ruler of the Gods, I declare that there are two kinds of bodily conduct: the kind to be pursued and the kind to be avoided.' Thus it was said, but with reference to what was this said? When one knows of bodily conduct that 'by cultivating this bodily conduct, unwholesome (*akuśala*) *dharmas* increase and wholesome (*kuśala*) *dharmas* diminish,' that kind of bodily conduct should not be cultivated.

When one knows of bodily conduct that 'by cultivating this bodily conduct, unwholesome *dharmas* diminish and wholesome *dharmas* increase,' that kind of bodily conduct should be cultivated.

Ruler of the Gods, I declare that there are two kinds of bodily conduct: the kind to be pursued and the kind to be avoided.' Thus it was said, and it was with reference to this that it was said.

"Ruler of the Gods, I declare that there are two kinds of verbal conduct: the kind to be pursued and the kind to be avoided." Thus it was said, but with reference to what was this said? When one knows of verbal conduct that 'by cultivating this verbal conduct, unwholesome *dharmas* increase and wholesome *dharmas* diminish,' that kind of verbal conduct should not be cultivated.

When one knows of verbal conduct that 'by cultivating this verbal conduct, unwholesome *dharmas* diminish and wholesome *dharmas* increase,' that kind of verbal conduct should be cultivated.

Ruler of the Gods, I declare that there are two kinds of verbal conduct: the kind to be pursued and the kind to be avoided.' Thus it was said, and it was with reference to this that it was said.

"Ruler of the Gods, I declare that there are two kinds of seeking: the kind to be pursued and the kind to be avoided." Thus it was said, but with reference to what was this said? When one knows of seeking that 'by cultivating this seeking, unwholesome *dharmas* increase and wholesome *dharmas* diminish,' that kind of seeking should not be cultivated.

When one knows of seeking that 'by cultivating this seeking, unwholesome *dharmas* diminish and wholesome *dharmas* increase,' that kind of seeking should be cultivated.

Ruler of the Gods, I declare that there are two kinds of seeking: the kind to be pursued and the kind to be avoided.' Thus it was said, and it was with reference to this that it was said.

"Thus, Ruler of the Gods, a *bhikṣu* is practicing who is practicing the restraint of the *prātimokṣa*."

In this way, the Blessed One answered Śakra, the ruler of the gods, when asked a question.

Delighted, Śakra, the ruler of the gods, rejoiced at and approved of the Blessed One's words: "So it is, Blessed One, so it is, Well-Gone! My doubt is dispelled, my uncertainty is removed, by hearing the Blessed One's answer to the question!"

Control of the Senses (*indriyasaṃvaro*)
<p n="364" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">364</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Tato <pb ed="M" n="2.0325"/> taṃ pucchanto <hi rend="bold">kathaṃ paṭipanno pana, mārisā</hi>tiādimāha. Tattha <hi rend="bold">pātimokkhasaṃvarāyā</hi>ti uttamajeṭṭhakasīlasaṃvarāya. <hi rend="bold">Kāyasamācārampī</hi>tiādi sevitabbakāyasamācārādivasena pātimokkhasaṃvaradassanatthaṃ vuttaṃ. Sīlakathā ca nāmesā kammapathavasena vā paṇṇattivasena vā kathetabbā hoti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Tattha kammapathavasena kathentena asevitabbakāyasamācāro tāva pāṇātipātaadinnādānamicchācārehi kathetabbo. Paṇṇattivasena kathentena kāyadvāre paññattasikkhāpadavītikkamavasena kathetabbo. Sevitabbakāyasamācāro pāṇātipātādiveramaṇīhi ceva kāyadvāre paññattasikkhāpadaavītikkamena ca kathetabbo. Asevitabbavacīsamācāro musāvādādivacīduccaritena ceva vacīdvāre paññattasikkhāpadavītikkamena ca kathetabbo. Sevitabbavacīsamācāro musāvādādiveramaṇīhi ceva vacīdvāre paññattasikkhāpadaavītikkamena ca kathetabbo.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Pariyesanā <pb ed="V" n="2.0292"/> pana kāyavācāhi pariyesanāyeva. Sā kāyavacīsamācāragahaṇena gahitāpi samānā yasmā ājīvaṭṭhamakasīlaṃ nāma etasmiññeva dvāradvaye uppajjati, na ākāse, tasmā ājīvaṭṭhamakasīladassanatthaṃ visuṃ vuttā. Tattha nasevitabbapariyesanā anariyapariyesanāya kathetabbā. Sevitabbapariyesanā ariyapariyesanāya. Vuttañhetaṃ –</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0292"/> <p rend="indent">‘‘Dvemā, bhikkhave, pariyesanā anariyā ca pariyesanā, ariyā ca pariyesanā. Katamā ca, bhikkhave, anariyā pariyesanā? Idha, bhikkhave, ekacco attanā jātidhammo samāno jātidhammaṃyeva pariyesati, attanā jarādhammo, byādhidhammo, maraṇadhammo, sokadhammo, saṃkilesadhammo samāno saṃkilesadhammaṃyeva pariyesati.</p> <p rend="indent">Kiñca, bhikkhave, jātidhammaṃ vadetha? Puttabhariyaṃ, bhikkhave <pb ed="P" n="3.0734"/>, jātidhammaṃ, dāsidāsaṃ jātidhammaṃ ajeḷakaṃ jātidhammaṃ, kukkuṭasūkaraṃ jātidhammaṃ, hatthigavāssavaḷavaṃ jātidhammaṃ, jātarūparajataṃ jātidhammaṃ. Jātidhammā <pb ed="M" n="2.0326"/> hete, bhikkhave, upadhayo, etthāyaṃ gathito mucchito ajjhāpanno attanā jātidhammo samāno jātidhammaṃyeva pariyesati.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0734"/> <pb ed="M" n="2.0326"/> <p rend="indent">Kiñca, bhikkhave, jarādhammaṃ vadetha? Puttabhariyaṃ, bhikkhave, jarādhammaṃ…pe… jarādhammaṃyeva pariyesati.</p> <p rend="indent">Kiñca, bhikkhave, byādhidhammaṃ vadetha? Puttabhariyaṃ, bhikkhave, byādhidhammaṃ, dāsidāsaṃ byādhidhammaṃ, ajeḷakaṃ, kukkuṭasūkaraṃ, hatthigavāssavaḷavaṃ byādhidhammaṃ. Byādhidhammā hete, bhikkhave, upadhayo, etthāyaṃ gathito mucchito ajjhāpanno attanā byādhidhammo samāno byādhidhammaṃyeva pariyesati.</p> <p rend="indent">Kiñca, bhikkhave, maraṇadhammaṃ vadetha? Puttabhariyaṃ, bhikkhave, maraṇadhammaṃ…pe… maraṇadhammaṃyeva pariyesati.</p> <p rend="indent">Kiñca, bhikkhave, sokadhammaṃ vadetha? Puttabhariyaṃ…pe… sokadhammaṃyeva pariyesati.</p> <p rend="indent">Kiñca, bhikkhave, saṃkilesadhammaṃ vadetha…pe… jātarūparajataṃ saṃkilesadhammaṃ. Saṃkilesadhammā <pb ed="V" n="2.0293"/>, hete, bhikkhave, upadhayo, etthāyaṃ gathito mucchito ajjhāpanno attanā saṃkilesadhammo samāno saṃkilesadhammaṃyeva pariyesati. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, anariyā pariyesanāti (ma. ni. 1.274).</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0293"/> <p rend="bodytext">Apica kuhanādivasena pañcavidhā, agocaravasena chabbidhā vejjakammādivasena ekavīsatividhā, evaṃ pavattā sabbāpi anesanā anariyapariyesanāyevāti veditabbā.</p> <p rend="indent">‘‘Katamā ca, bhikkhave, ariyā pariyesanā? Idha, bhikkhave, ekacco attanā jātidhammo samāno jātidhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā ajātaṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ pariyesati, attanā jarādhammo, byādhi, maraṇa, soka, saṃkilesadhammo samāno saṃkilesadhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā asaṃkiliṭṭhaṃ anuttaraṃ <pb ed="M" n="2.0327"/> yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ pariyesati. Ayaṃ ariyā pariyesanāti (ma. ni. 1.275).</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0327"/> <p rend="bodytext">Apica pañca kuhanādīni cha agocare ekavīsatividhañca anesanaṃ vajjetvā bhikkhācariyāya dhammena samena pariyesanāpi ariyapariyesanāyevāti veditabbā.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Ettha ca yo yo <pb ed="P" n="3.0735"/> ‘‘na sevitabbo’’ti vutto, so so pubbabhāge pāṇātipātādīnaṃ sambhārapariyesanāpayogakaraṇagamanakālato paṭṭhāya na sevitabbova. Itaro ādito paṭṭhāya sevitabbo, asakkontena cittampi uppādetabbaṃ. Apica saṅghabhedādīnaṃ atthāya parakkamantānaṃ devadattādīnaṃ viya kāyasamācāro na sevitabbo, divasassa dvattikkhattuṃ tiṇṇaṃ ratanānaṃ upaṭṭhānagamanādivasena pavatto dhammasenāpatimahāmoggallānattherādīnaṃ viya kāyasamācāro sevitabbo. Dhanuggahapesanādivasena vācaṃ bhindantānaṃ devadattādīnaṃ viya vacīsamācāro na sevitabbo, tiṇṇaṃ ratanānaṃ guṇakittanādivasena pavatto dhammasenāpatimahāmoggallānattherādīnaṃ viya vacīsamācāro sevitabbo. Anariyapariyesanaṃ pariyesantānaṃ devadattādīnaṃ viya pariyesanā na sevitabbā, ariyapariyesanameva pariyesantānaṃ dhammasenāpatimahāmoggallānattherādīnaṃ viya pariyesanā sevitabbā.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0735"/> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Evaṃ paṭipanno kho</hi>ti evaṃ asevitabbaṃ kāyavacīsamācāraṃ pariyesanañca pahāya sevitabbānaṃ pāripūriyā paṭipanno, devānaminda, bhikkhu pātimokkhasaṃvarāya uttamajeṭṭhakasīlasaṃvaratthāya <pb ed="V" n="2.0294"/> paṭipanno nāma hotīti bhagavā khīṇāsavassa āgamanīyapubbabhāgapaṭipadaṃ kathesi.</p> <hi rend="bold">Evaṃ paṭipanno kho</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0294"/> <p rend="subhead">Indriyasaṃvaravaṇṇanā</p>
364. Then, asking him, he said, "How have you undertaken [the training], friend?"

There, "for the sake of the *pātimokkhasaṃvara*" means for the sake of the supreme, best restraint in morality.

"Also, physical conduct" etc. is stated to show the *pātimokkhasaṃvara* in terms of physical conduct to be cultivated, etc.

And this, namely, the discourse on morality, should be given in terms of the path of action or in terms of the precepts.

There, when speaking in terms of the path of action, uncultivated physical conduct should be discussed first, with regard to killing, stealing, and sexual misconduct.

When speaking in terms of the precepts, it should be discussed in terms of transgression of the precepts laid down at the door of the body.

Cultivated physical conduct should be discussed in terms of abstinence from killing, etc., and in terms of non-transgression of the precepts laid down at the door of the body.

Uncultivated verbal conduct should be discussed in terms of verbal misconduct, such as lying, etc., and in terms of transgression of the precepts laid down at the door of speech.

Cultivated verbal conduct should be discussed in terms of abstinence from lying, etc., and in terms of non-transgression of the precepts laid down at the door of speech.

Search, however, is only a search by means of body and speech.

Although that is included by the taking up of physical and verbal conduct, since the morality of right livelihood arises only in these two doors, not in the sky, therefore it is stated separately to show the morality of right livelihood.

There, uncultivated search should be discussed with respect to the ignoble search.

Cultivated search [should be discussed] with respect to the noble search.

For it was said:

“Monks, there are these two kinds of search: the ignoble search and the noble search.

And what, monks, is the ignoble search? Here, monks, someone being himself subject to birth seeks what is also subject to birth; being himself subject to ageing, sickness, death, sorrow, defilement, he seeks what is also subject to defilement.

“And what, monks, do you call subject to birth? Wife and children, monks, are subject to birth, men and women slaves are subject to birth, goats and sheep are subject to birth, fowl and pigs are subject to birth, elephants, cattle, horses, and mares are subject to birth, gold and silver are subject to birth.

These, monks, are possessions subject to birth, and one who is tied to these things, infatuated with them, and utterly committed to them, being himself subject to birth, seeks what it also subject to birth.

“And what, monks, do you call subject to ageing? Wife and children, monks, are subject to ageing…pe… he seeks what is also subject to ageing.

“And what, monks, do you call subject to sickness? Wife and children, monks, are subject to sickness, men and women slaves are subject to sickness, goats and sheep, fowl and pigs, elephants, cattle, horses, and mares are subject to sickness.

These, monks, are possessions subject to sickness, and one who is tied to these things, infatuated with them, and utterly committed to them, being himself subject to sickness, seeks what is also subject to sickness.

“And what, monks, do you call subject to death? Wife and children, monks, are subject to death…pe… he seeks what is also subject to death.

“And what, monks, do you call subject to sorrow? Wife and children…pe… he seeks what is also subject to sorrow.

“And what, monks, do you call subject to defilement…pe… gold and silver are subject to defilement.

These, monks, are possessions subject to defilement, and one who is tied to these things, infatuated with them, and utterly committed to them, being himself subject to defilement, seeks what is also subject to defilement.

This, monks, is the ignoble search” (M.
Ni.
1.
274).

Moreover, all search that occurs in this way – fivefold in terms of deceit, sixfold in terms of improper scope, twenty-onefold in terms of medical practice, etc. – should be understood as the ignoble search.

“And what, monks, is the noble search? Here, monks, someone being himself subject to birth, having understood the danger in what is subject to birth, seeks the unborn, unsurpassed security from bondage, *nirvāṇa*; being himself subject to ageing, sickness, death, sorrow, defilement, having understood the danger in what is subject to defilement, he seeks the undefiled, unsurpassed.
seeks out the secure *yogakkhema*, the *Nibbāna* free from disease.

This is called the Noble Search (*ariyā pariyesanā*).

Furthermore, it should be understood that avoiding the five hypocritical means, the six improper pastures, and the twenty-one kinds of wrong livelihood, even searching for alms according to the Dharma in an even manner, is indeed a Noble Search (*ariyapariyesanā*).

Here, whatever conduct is said to be "not to be cultivated," from the initial stage of accumulating materials, searching, employing, acting, going, and the time related to killing living beings, etc., that conduct should not be cultivated.

The other conduct, from the beginning, should be cultivated, and one should generate a mind even when unable to do so.

Furthermore, bodily conduct for the sake of causing schism in the *saṅgha*, etc., like that of Devadatta, etc., should not be cultivated. Bodily conduct that occurs two or three times a day in the manner of attending to and visiting the three jewels (*ratana*), etc., like that of the Dharma Commander, the great *thera* Mahā-Moggallāna, etc., should be cultivated.

Verbal conduct that breaks speech through archery practice, sending messages, etc., like that of Devadatta, etc., should not be cultivated. Verbal conduct that occurs through praising the qualities of the three jewels (*ratana*), etc., like that of the Dharma Commander, the great *thera* Mahā-Moggallāna, etc., should be cultivated.

The un-Noble Search (*anariyapariyesana*) of those searching, like Devadatta, etc., that search should not be cultivated. The search of those searching for the Noble Search (*ariyapariyesana*) itself, like that of the Dharma Commander, the great *thera* Mahā-Moggallāna, etc., that search should be cultivated.

Thus, "one who is practicing in this way," meaning having abandoned bodily conduct, verbal conduct, and searching that should not be cultivated, and practicing the completeness of what should be cultivated, that *bhikkhu*, Devānaminda, is said to be "one who is practicing" for the sake of restraint according to the *prātimokṣa* and for the sake of the highest, most excellent restraint of morality (*śīla*). The Blessed One (*bhagavā*) spoke of the path of preliminary actions leading to one who is without outflows (*khīṇāsava*).

Description of the Restraint of the Senses (*indriyasaṃvaravaṇṇanā*).
365
<p n="365" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">365</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Itiha sakko devānamindo bhagavato bhāsitaṃ abhinanditvā anumoditvā bhagavantaṃ uttariṃ pañhaṃ apucchi –</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Kathaṃ paṭipanno pana, mārisa, bhikkhu indriyasaṃvarāya paṭipanno hotī’’ti? ‘‘Cakkhuviññeyyaṃ rūpaṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi – sevitabbampi, asevitabbampi. Sotaviññeyyaṃ saddaṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi – sevitabbampi, asevitabbampi. Ghānaviññeyyaṃ gandhaṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi – sevitabbampi, asevitabbampi. Jivhāviññeyyaṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0208"/> rasaṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi – sevitabbampi, asevitabbampi. Kāyaviññeyyaṃ phoṭṭhabbaṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi – sevitabbampi, asevitabbampi. Manoviññeyyaṃ dhammaṃpāhaṃ, devānaminda, duvidhena vadāmi – sevitabbampi, asevitabbampī’’ti.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0208"/> <p rend="bodytext">Evaṃ vutte, sakko devānamindo bhagavantaṃ etadavoca –</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Imassa kho ahaṃ, bhante, bhagavatā saṅkhittena bhāsitassa evaṃ vitthārena atthaṃ ājānāmi. Yathārūpaṃ, bhante, cakkhuviññeyyaṃ rūpaṃ sevato akusalā dhammā abhivaḍḍhanti, kusalā dhammā parihāyanti, evarūpaṃ cakkhuviññeyyaṃ rūpaṃ <pb ed="M" n="2.0225"/> na sevitabbaṃ <pb ed="P" n="2.0282"/>. Yathārūpañca kho, bhante, cakkhuviññeyyaṃ rūpaṃ sevato akusalā dhammā parihāyanti, kusalā dhammā abhivaḍḍhanti, evarūpaṃ cakkhuviññeyyaṃ rūpaṃ sevitabbaṃ. Yathārūpañca kho, bhante, sotaviññeyyaṃ <pb ed="T" n="2.0317"/> saddaṃ sevato…pe… ghānaviññeyyaṃ gandhaṃ sevato… jivhāviññeyyaṃ rasaṃ sevato… kāyaviññeyyaṃ phoṭṭhabbaṃ sevato… manoviññeyyaṃ dhammaṃ sevato akusalā dhammā abhivaḍḍhanti, kusalā dhammā parihāyanti, evarūpo manoviññeyyo dhammo na sevitabbo. Yathārūpañca kho, bhante, manoviññeyyaṃ dhammaṃ sevato akusalā dhammā parihāyanti, kusalā dhammā abhivaḍḍhanti, evarūpo manoviññeyyo dhammo sevitabbo.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0225"/> <pb ed="P" n="2.0282"/> <pb ed="T" n="2.0317"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Imassa kho me, bhante, bhagavatā saṅkhittena bhāsitassa evaṃ vitthārena atthaṃ ājānato tiṇṇā mettha kaṅkhā vigatā kathaṃkathā bhagavato pañhaveyyākaraṇaṃ sutvā’’ti.</p>
365. Then, Śakra, the ruler of the gods, having rejoiced at and approved of the Blessed One's words, asked the Blessed One a further question:

"But how, sir, is a *bhikṣu* practicing restraint of the senses a practitioner?"

"Ruler of the Gods, I declare that forms cognizable by the eye (*cakṣurvijñeya rūpa*) are of two kinds: the kind to be pursued (*sevitabba*), and the kind to be avoided (*asevitabba*).

Ruler of the Gods, I declare that sounds cognizable by the ear (*śrotravijñeya śabda*) are of two kinds: the kind to be pursued, and the kind to be avoided.

Ruler of the Gods, I declare that odours cognizable by the nose (*ghānavijñeya gandha*) are of two kinds: the kind to be pursued, and the kind to be avoided.

Ruler of the Gods, I declare that tastes cognizable by the tongue (*jihvāvijñeya rasa*) are of two kinds: the kind to be pursued, and the kind to be avoided.

Ruler of the Gods, I declare that tangibles cognizable by the body (*kāyavijñeya sparśa*) are of two kinds: the kind to be pursued, and the kind to be avoided.

Ruler of the Gods, I declare that mind-objects cognizable by the mind (*manovijñeya dharma*) are of two kinds: the kind to be pursued, and the kind to be avoided."

When this was said, Śakra, the ruler of the gods, said this to the Blessed One:

"Venerable sir, I understand the meaning of what was spoken by the Blessed One in brief in this way, in detail:

Venerable sir, such forms cognizable by the eye, when cultivated, cause unwholesome states (*akuśalā dharma*) to increase and wholesome states (*kuśalā dharma*) to diminish, should not be cultivated. But such forms cognizable by the eye, when cultivated, cause unwholesome states to diminish and wholesome states to increase, should be cultivated.

And the same applies to sounds cognizable by the ear…odours cognizable by the nose…tastes cognizable by the tongue…tangibles cognizable by the body…mind-objects cognizable by the mind: such mind-objects, when cultivated, cause unwholesome states to increase and wholesome states to diminish, should not be cultivated.

Venerable sir, such mind-objects cognizable by the mind, when cultivated, cause unwholesome states to diminish and wholesome states to increase, should be cultivated.

"Venerable sir, as I understand the meaning of what was spoken by the Blessed One in brief in this way, in detail, these three kinds of doubt have been dispelled, uncertainty is gone, having heard the Blessed One's answer to the question."
<p n="365" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">365</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Dutiyapucchāyaṃ <hi rend="bold">indriyasaṃvarāyā</hi>ti indriyānaṃ pidhānāya, guttadvāratāya saṃvutadvāratāyāti attho. Vissajjane panassa <hi rend="bold">cakkhuviññeyyaṃ rūpampī</hi>tiādi sevitabbarūpādivasena indriyasaṃvaradassanatthaṃ vuttaṃ. Tattha <hi rend="bold">evaṃ vutte</hi>ti heṭṭhā somanassādipañhāvissajjanānaṃ sutattā imināpi evarūpena bhavitabbanti sañjātapaṭibhāno bhagavatā evaṃ vutte sakko devānamindo <pb ed="M" n="2.0328"/> bhagavantaṃ etadavoca, etaṃ <hi rend="bold">imassa kho ahaṃ, bhante</hi>ti ādikaṃ vacanaṃ avoca. Bhagavāpissa okāsaṃ datvā tuṇhī ahosi. Kathetukāmopi hi yo atthaṃ sampādetuṃ na sakkoti, atthaṃ sampādetuṃ sakkonto vā na kathetukāmo hoti, na tassa bhagavā okāsaṃ karoti. Ayaṃ pana yasmā kathetukāmo ceva, sakkoti ca atthaṃ sampādetuṃ tenassa bhagavā okāsamakāsi.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Tattha <hi rend="bold">evarūpaṃ na sevitabba</hi>nti ādīsu ayaṃ saṅkhepo – yaṃ <pb ed="P" n="3.0736"/> rūpaṃ passato rāgādayo uppajjanti, taṃ na sevitabbaṃ na daṭṭhabbaṃ na oloketabbanti attho. Yaṃ pana passato asubhasaññā vā saṇṭhāti, pasādo vā uppajjati, aniccasaññāpaṭilābho vā hoti, taṃ sevitabbaṃ.</p> <hi rend="bold">evarūpaṃ na sevitabba</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0736"/> <p rend="bodytext">Yaṃ cittakkharaṃ cittabyañjanampi saddaṃ suṇato rāgādayo uppajjanti, evarūpo saddo na sevitabbo. Yaṃ pana atthanissitaṃ dhammanissitaṃ kumbhadāsigītampi suṇantassa pasādo vā uppajjati, nibbidā vā saṇṭhāti, evarūpo saddo sevitabbo.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Yaṃ gandhaṃ ghāyato rāgādayo uppajjanti, evarūpo gandho na sevitabbo. Yaṃ pana gandhaṃ ghāyato asubhasaññādipaṭilābho hoti, evarūpo gandho sevitabbo.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Yaṃ rasaṃ sāyato rāgādayo uppajjanti, evarūpo raso na sevitabbo. Yaṃ pana rasaṃ sāyato āhāre paṭikūlasaññā ceva uppajjati, sāyitapaccayā ca kāyabalaṃ nissāya ariyabhūmiṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0295"/> okkamituṃ sakkoti, mahāsīvattherabhāgineyyasīvasāmaṇerassa viya paribhuñjantasseva kilesakkhayo vā hoti, evarūpo raso sevitabbo.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0295"/> <p rend="bodytext">Yaṃ phoṭṭhabbaṃ phusato rāgādayo uppajjanti, evarūpaṃ phoṭṭhabbaṃ na sevitabbaṃ. Yaṃ pana phusato sāriputtattherādīnaṃ viya āsavakkhayo ceva, vīriyañca supaggahitaṃ, pacchimā ca janatā diṭṭhānugatiṃ āpādanena anuggahitā hoti, evarūpaṃ phoṭṭhabbaṃ sevitabbaṃ. Sāriputtatthero kira tiṃsa vassāni mañce piṭṭhiṃ na pasāresi. Tathā mahāmoggallānatthero. Mahākassapatthero vīsavassasataṃ mañce piṭṭhiṃ na pasāresi. Anuruddhatthero paññāsa vassāni. Bhaddiyatthero tiṃsa vassāni. Soṇatthero aṭṭhārasa <pb ed="M" n="2.0329"/> vassāni. Raṭṭhapālatthero dvādasa. Ānandatthero pannarasa. Rāhulatthero dvādasa. Bākulatthero asīti vassāni. Nāḷakatthero yāvaparinibbānā mañce piṭṭhiṃ na pasāresīti.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0329"/> <p rend="bodytext">Ye manoviññeyye dhamme samannāharantassa rāgādayo uppajjanti, ‘‘aho, vata yaṃ paresaṃ paravittūpakaraṇaṃ taṃ mamassā’’tiādinā nayena vā abhijjhādīni āpāthamāgacchanti evarūpā dhammā na sevitabbā. ‘‘Sabbe sattā averā hontū’’ti evaṃ mettādivasena, ye vā pana tiṇṇaṃ therānaṃ dhammā, evarūpā sevitabbā. Tayo kira therā vassūpanāyikadivase kāmavitakkādayo <pb ed="P" n="3.0737"/> akusalavitakkā na vitakketabbāti katikaṃ akaṃsu. Atha pavāraṇadivase saṅghatthero saṅghanavakaṃ pucchi – ‘‘āvuso, imasmiṃ temāse kittake ṭhāne cittassa dhāvituṃ dinna’’nti? Na, bhante, pariveṇaparicchedato bahi dhāvituṃ adāsinti. Dutiyaṃ pucchi – ‘‘tava āvuso’’ti? Nivāsagehato, bhante, bahi dhāvituṃ na adāsinti. Atha dvepi theraṃ pucchiṃsu ‘‘tumhākaṃ pana, bhante’’ti? Niyakajjhattakhandhapañcakato, āvuso, bahi dhāvituṃ na adāsinti. Tumhehi, bhante, dukkaraṃ katanti. Evarūpo manoviññeyyo dhammo sevitabbo.</p> <pb ed="P" n="3.0737"/>
365. In the second question, *indriyasaṃvarāya* means the restraint of the senses, that is, the guarding of the doors [of perception], the closing of the doors, this is the meaning.

In the explanation, *cakkhuviññeyyaṃ rūpampī* etc., is said in order to show the restraint of the senses by way of forms and so forth that should be cultivated.

There, *evaṃ vutte* means that, because it has been heard that the answers to the questions about pleasant feelings and so forth are such, Sakra, the lord of the gods, having an understanding that it will be of this form, when the Blessed One had spoken thus (p. 2.0328) said this to the Blessed One: “This is for me, venerable sir,” he spoke these words and so forth.

The Blessed One gave him an opportunity and remained silent.

For the Blessed One does not give an opportunity to someone who desires to speak but is not able to accomplish the meaning, or who is able to accomplish the meaning but does not desire to speak.

But because this one both desires to speak and is able to accomplish the meaning, therefore the Blessed One gave him an opportunity.

There, *evarūpaṃ na sevitabba* etc., this is the summary: (p. 3.0736) when a form is seen, lust and so forth arise, that should not be cultivated, should not be seen, should not be looked at, this is the meaning.

But when, by seeing something, foul perception arises, or joy arises, or the attainment of the perception of impermanence [arises], that should be cultivated.

*evarūpaṃ na sevitabba* (p. 3.0736)

When, by hearing a sound that is characterized by thought and is expressive of thought, lust and so forth arise, that kind of sound should not be cultivated.

But when, by hearing a sound that is connected with meaning and connected with the Dharma, even the song of a servant girl carrying a water pot, joy arises, or disgust arises, that kind of sound should be cultivated.

When, by smelling an odor, lust and so forth arise, that kind of odor should not be cultivated.

But when, by smelling an odor, the attainment of foul perception and so forth arises, that kind of odor should be cultivated.

When, by tasting a flavor, lust and so forth arise, that kind of flavor should not be cultivated.

But when, by tasting a flavor, foul perception of food arises, and also, relying on the strength of the body due to the condition of having tasted, one is able to step onto the noble plane (p. 2.0295), or, just as for Sīva, the nephew of the elder Mahāsīva, even while enjoying [food], the destruction of the afflictions occurs, that kind of flavor should be cultivated.

(p. 2.0295) When, by touching a tangible object, lust and so forth arise, that kind of tangible object should not be cultivated.

But when, by touching something, just as for the elders Śāriputra and so forth, the destruction of the influxes occurs, and also energy is well established, and also the later generation is assisted by bringing about conformity to their view, that kind of tangible object should be cultivated.

It is said that the elder Śāriputra did not stretch out his back on a couch for thirty years.

Likewise, the elder Mahāmoggallāna.

The elder Mahākassapa did not stretch out his back on a couch for one hundred and twenty years.

The elder Anuruddha for fifty years.

The elder Bhaddiya for thirty years.

The elder Soṇa for eighteen (p. 2.0329) years.

The elder Raṭṭhapāla for twelve.

The elder Ānanda for fifteen.

The elder Rāhula for twelve.

The elder Bākula for eighty years.

It is said that the elder Nāḷaka did not stretch out his back on a couch until his parinirvāṇa.

(p. 2.0329) When, for someone who reflects on mental objects, lust and so forth arise, [thinking,] "Oh, that which is the property and equipment of others might be mine!" and so forth, in this way or in that way, covetousness and so forth come into the path, those kinds of dharmas should not be cultivated.

"May all beings be without enmity!" in this way, by way of loving-kindness and so forth, or those dharmas of the three elders, those kinds of things should be cultivated.

It is said that those three elders, on the rain-retreat entry day, made an agreement (p. 3.0737) that unskillful thoughts, such as thoughts of sensual pleasure, should not be thought.

Then, on the day of the pavāraṇa, the elder of the Saṃgha asked the Saṃgha-novice: “Friend, in this three-month period, how much space was given for the mind to wander?” “No, venerable sir, [space] was not given to wander outside of the boundary of the monastery.”
He asked a second time, "Friend, what about you?" "Venerable sir, they did not allow me to run outside the dwelling."

Then they both asked the elder, "And what about you, venerable sir?" "Friend, they did not allow me to run outside from the group of five aggregates that are my own."

Venerable Sir, you have done what is difficult.

A *dhamma* that is perceived by the mind in this way should be cultivated.
366
<p n="366" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">366</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Itiha sakko devānamindo bhagavato bhāsitaṃ abhinanditvā anumoditvā bhagavantaṃ uttariṃ pañhaṃ apucchi –</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Sabbeva nu kho, mārisa, samaṇabrāhmaṇā ekantavādā ekantasīlā ekantachandā ekantaajjhosānā’’ti? ‘‘Na kho, devānaminda, sabbe samaṇabrāhmaṇā ekantavādā ekantasīlā ekantachandā ekantaajjhosānā’’ti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Kasmā pana, mārisa, na sabbe samaṇabrāhmaṇā ekantavādā ekantasīlā ekantachandā ekantaajjhosānā’’ti? ‘‘Anekadhātu nānādhātu kho, devānaminda, loko. Tasmiṃ anekadhātunānādhātusmiṃ loke yaṃ yadeva sattā dhātuṃ abhinivisanti, taṃ tadeva thāmasā parāmāsā <pb ed="V" n="2.0209"/> abhinivissa voharanti – ‘idameva saccaṃ moghamañña’nti. Tasmā <pb ed="T" n="2.0318"/> na sabbe samaṇabrāhmaṇā ekantavādā ekantasīlā ekantachandā ekantaajjhosānā’’ti.</p> <pb ed="V" n="2.0209"/> <pb ed="T" n="2.0318"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Sabbeva nu kho, mārisa, samaṇabrāhmaṇā accantaniṭṭhā <pb ed="P" n="2.0283"/> accantayogakkhemī accantabrahmacārī accantapariyosānā’’ti? ‘‘Na kho, devānaminda, sabbe samaṇabrāhmaṇā accantaniṭṭhā accantayogakkhemī accantabrahmacārī accantapariyosānā’’ti.</p> <pb ed="P" n="2.0283"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Kasmā <pb ed="M" n="2.0226"/> pana, mārisa, na sabbe samaṇabrāhmaṇā accantaniṭṭhā accantayogakkhemī accantabrahmacārī accantapariyosānā’’ti? ‘‘Ye kho, devānaminda, bhikkhū taṇhāsaṅkhayavimuttā te accantaniṭṭhā accantayogakkhemī accantabrahmacārī accantapariyosānā. Tasmā na sabbe samaṇabrāhmaṇā accantaniṭṭhā accantayogakkhemī accantabrahmacārī accantapariyosānā’’ti.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0226"/> <p rend="bodytext">Itthaṃ bhagavā sakkassa devānamindassa pañhaṃ puṭṭho byākāsi. Attamano sakko devānamindo bhagavato bhāsitaṃ abhinandi anumodi – ‘‘evametaṃ, bhagavā, evametaṃ, sugata. Tiṇṇā mettha kaṅkhā vigatā kathaṃkathā bhagavato pañhaveyyākaraṇaṃ sutvā’’ti.</p>
366. Then Śakra, the head of the gods, having rejoiced at and approved of the Blessed One's words, asked the Blessed One a further question:

‘‘Sir, are all ascetics and *brāhmaṇas* adherents of a single view, a single discipline, a single desire, a single devotion?’’ ‘‘No, head of the gods, not all ascetics and *brāhmaṇas* are adherents of a single view, a single discipline, a single desire, a single devotion.’’

‘‘But why, sir, are not all ascetics and *brāhmaṇas* adherents of a single view, a single discipline, a single desire, a single devotion?’’ ‘‘The world, head of the gods, is of diverse elements and various elements.
In this world of diverse elements and various elements, beings become strongly attached to one or another element, and adhering to that with tenacity and prejudice, they assert: ‘This alone is the truth, everything else is false!’
Therefore not all ascetics and *brāhmaṇas* are adherents of a single view, a single discipline, a single desire, a single devotion.’’

‘‘Sir, are all ascetics and *brāhmaṇas* ultimately accomplished, ultimately secure from the yoke, ultimately practicing the holy life, ultimately at the final goal?’’ ‘‘No, head of the gods, not all ascetics and *brāhmaṇas* are ultimately accomplished, ultimately secure from the yoke, ultimately practicing the holy life, ultimately at the final goal.’’

‘‘But why, sir, are not all ascetics and *brāhmaṇas* ultimately accomplished, ultimately secure from the yoke, ultimately practicing the holy life, ultimately at the final goal?’’ ‘‘Those monks, head of the gods, who are liberated through the destruction of craving are ultimately accomplished, ultimately secure from the yoke, ultimately practicing the holy life, ultimately at the final goal.
Therefore not all ascetics and *brāhmaṇas* are ultimately accomplished, ultimately secure from the yoke, ultimately practicing the holy life, ultimately at the final goal.’’

Thus the Blessed One answered Śakra, the head of the gods, when questioned.
And Śakra, the head of the gods, delighted and approved of the Blessed One’s words, exclaiming: ‘‘So it is, Blessed One, so it is, Well-Farer!
I have overcome my doubt and got rid of uncertainty through hearing the Lord’s answer to the question!’’
<p n="366" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">366</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Ekantavādā</hi>ti ekoyeva anto vādassa etesaṃ, na dvedhā gatavādāti <hi rend="bold">ekantavādā,</hi> ekaññeva vadantīti pucchati. <hi rend="bold">Ekantasīlā</hi>ti ekācārā. <hi rend="bold">Ekantachandā</hi>ti ekaladdhikā. <hi rend="bold">Ekantaajjhosānā</hi>ti ekantapariyosānā.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Anekadhātu nānādhātu kho, devānaminda, loko</hi>ti devānaminda, ayaṃ loko anekajjhāsayo nānajjhāsayo. Ekasmiṃ gantukāme eko ṭhātukāmo hoti. Ekasmiṃ ṭhātukāme eko sayitukāmo hoti. Dve sattā ekajjhāsayā nāma dullabhā. Tasmiṃ anekadhātunānādhātusmiṃ <pb ed="V" n="2.0296"/> loke yaṃ yadeva dhātuṃ yaṃ yadeva ajjhāsayaṃ sattā abhinivisanti gaṇhanti, taṃ tadeva. <hi rend="bold">Thāmasā parāmāsā</hi>ti thāmena ca parāmāsena ca. <hi rend="bold">Abhinivissa voharantī</hi>ti suṭṭhu gaṇhitvā voharanti, kathenti dīpenti kittenti. <hi rend="bold">Idameva saccaṃ moghamañña</hi>nti idaṃ amhākameva vacanaṃ saccaṃ, aññesaṃ vacanaṃ moghaṃ tucchaṃ niratthakanti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Anekadhātu nānādhātu kho, devānaminda, loko</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0296"/> <hi rend="bold">Thāmasā parāmāsā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Abhinivissa voharantī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Idameva saccaṃ moghamañña</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Accantaniṭṭhā</hi>ti <pb ed="M" n="2.0330"/> anto vuccati vināso, antaṃ atītā niṭṭhā etesanti accantaniṭṭhā. Yā etesaṃ niṭṭhā, yo paramassāso nibbānaṃ, taṃ sabbesaṃ vināsātikkantaṃ niccanti vuccati. <hi rend="bold">Yogakkhemo</hi>ti nibbānasseva nāmaṃ, accanto yogakkhemo etesanti <hi rend="bold">accantayogakkhemī.</hi> Seṭṭhaṭṭhena brahmaṃ ariyamaggaṃ carantīti <hi rend="bold">brahmacārī.</hi> Accantatthāya brahmacārī <hi rend="bold">accantabrahmacārī. Pariyosāna</hi>ntipi nibbānassa nāmaṃ. Accantaṃ pariyosānaṃ etesanti <hi rend="bold">accantapariyosānā.</hi></p> <hi rend="bold">Accantaniṭṭhā</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0330"/> <hi rend="bold">Yogakkhemo</hi> <hi rend="bold">accantayogakkhemī.</hi> <hi rend="bold">brahmacārī.</hi> <hi rend="bold">accantabrahmacārī. Pariyosāna</hi> <hi rend="bold">accantapariyosānā.</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Taṇhāsaṅkhayavimuttā</hi>ti <pb ed="P" n="3.0738"/> taṇhāsaṅkhayoti maggopi nibbānampi. Maggo taṇhaṃ saṅkhiṇāti vināsetīti taṇhāsaṅkhayo. Nibbānaṃ yasmā taṃ āgamma taṇhā saṅkhiyati vinassati, tasmā taṇhāsaṅkhayo. Taṇhāsaṅkhayena maggena vimuttā, taṇhāsaṅkhaye nibbāne vimuttā adhimuttāti <hi rend="bold">taṇhāsaṅkhayavimuttā.</hi></p> <hi rend="bold">Taṇhāsaṅkhayavimuttā</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0738"/> <hi rend="bold">taṇhāsaṅkhayavimuttā.</hi> <p rend="bodytext">Ettāvatā ca bhagavatā cuddasapi mahāpañhā byākatā honti. Cuddasa mahāpañhā nāma issāmacchariyaṃ eko pañho, piyāppiyaṃ eko, chando eko, vitakko eko, papañco eko, somanassaṃ eko, domanassaṃ eko, upekkhā eko, kāyasamācāro eko, vacīsamācāro eko, pariyesanā eko, indriyasaṃvaro eko, anekadhātu eko, accantaniṭṭhā ekoti.</p>
366. *Ekantavādā*: because theirs is a one-sided doctrine (anta vādassa), not a doctrine that goes in two ways (dvedhā gatavādā); therefore, *ekantavādā*, he asks, "do they speak only one thing (ekaññeva vadantīti)?"
*Ekantasīlā*: single conduct (ekācārā).
*Ekantachandā*: single desire (ekaladdhikā).
*Ekantaajjhosānā*: single end (ekantapariyosānā).

The Blessed One said, "This world (loko), O Indra of the gods (devānaminda), is of diverse elements (anekadhātu), of various elements (nānādhātu)"; O Indra of the gods, this world is of diverse inclinations (anekajjhāsayo), of various inclinations (nānajjhāsayo).
When one person wants to go (ekasmiṃ gantukāme), the other wants to stay (eko ṭhātukāmo hoti).
When one person wants to stay (ekasmiṃ ṭhātukāme), the other wants to lie down (eko sayitukāmo hoti).
It is rare to find two beings of identical inclinations (dve sattā ekajjhāsayā nāma dullabhā).
In this world of diverse elements and various elements (tasmiṃ anekadhātunānādhātusmiṃ loke), whatever element (dhātuṃ) or whatever inclination (ajjhāsayaṃ) beings adhere to (abhinivisanti gaṇhanti), that is what they become (taṃ tadeva).
*Thāmasā parāmāsā*: by force (thāmena) and by adherence (parāmāsena).
*Abhinivissa voharantī*: having grasped well (suṭṭhu gaṇhitvā), they declare (voharanti), they narrate (kathenti), they illuminate (dīpenti), they praise (kittenti).
*Idameva saccaṃ moghamañña*: "this alone is true (saccaṃ), other things are false (moghaṃ)," that is, "our word alone is true, the words of others are vain (moghaṃ), empty (tucchaṃ), and meaningless (niratthakanti)."

Diverse elements, various elements, O Indra of the gods, [is] the world!
By force, by adherence!
Having grasped, they declare!
This alone is true, other things are false!
*Accantaniṭṭhā*: *anta* means destruction (vināso), *niṭṭhā* is the end (antaṃ atītā) that has passed beyond these; therefore, *accantaniṭṭhā*.
That which is their end (niṭṭhā), which is the highest breath (paramassāso), nirvāṇa (nibbānaṃ), that is said to be the eternal (niccanti) that has transcended all destruction (sabbesaṃ vināsātikkantaṃ).
*Yogakkhemo*: is a name for nirvāṇa itself; because theirs is the absence (accanto) of *yogakkhemo*, [they are called] *accantayogakkhemī*.
He who lives the noble path (ariyamaggaṃ carantīti) in the highest sense (seṭṭhaṭṭhena), O Brahman (brahmā), is called a *brahmacārī* (celibate, religious student).
The *brahmacārī* for the sake of the highest goal is the *accantabrahmacārī*.
*Pariyosāna* is also a name for nirvāṇa.
Because theirs is the end that has transcended (accantaṃ pariyosānaṃ), [they are called] *accantapariyosānā*.

*Accantaniṭṭhā*.
*Yogakkhemo*.
*Accantayogakkhemī*.
*Brahmacārī*.
*Accantabrahmacārī*.
*Pariyosāna*.
*Accantapariyosānā*.

*Taṇhāsaṅkhayavimuttā*: *taṇhāsaṅkhayo* means both the path (maggo) and nirvāṇa (nibbānaṃ).
*Maggo taṇhaṃ saṅkhiṇāti vināsetīti taṇhāsaṅkhayo*: because the path destroys (saṅkhiṇāti vināsetīti) craving (*taṇhā*), it is *taṇhāsaṅkhayo*.
*Nibbānaṃ yasmā taṃ āgamma taṇhā saṅkhiyati vinassati, tasmā taṇhāsaṅkhayo*: because when nirvāṇa is reached (*taṃ āgamma*), craving is destroyed (*taṇhā saṅkhiyati vinassati*), therefore it is *taṇhāsaṅkhayo*.
*Taṇhāsaṅkhayena maggena vimuttā, taṇhāsaṅkhaye nibbāne vimuttā adhimuttāti taṇhāsaṅkhayavimuttā*: liberated by the path that destroys craving, liberated in nirvāṇa where craving is destroyed, because they are intent [on that liberation], [they are called] *taṇhāsaṅkhayavimuttā*.

*Taṇhāsaṅkhayavimuttā*.
*Taṇhāsaṅkhayavimuttā*.

Thus, the Blessed One has explained these fourteen great questions (cuddasapi mahāpañhā).
The fourteen great questions are: envy and stinginess, one question; the beloved and the unloved, one; desire, one; thought, one; proliferation, one; joy, one; sorrow, one; equanimity, one; bodily conduct, one; verbal conduct, one; seeking, one; sense restraint, one; diverse elements, one; the highest end, one.
367
<p n="367" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">367</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Itiha sakko devānamindo bhagavato bhāsitaṃ abhinanditvā anumoditvā bhagavantaṃ etadavoca –</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Ejā, bhante, rogo, ejā gaṇḍo, ejā sallaṃ, ejā imaṃ purisaṃ parikaḍḍhati tassa tasseva bhavassa abhinibbattiyā. Tasmā ayaṃ puriso uccāvacamāpajjati <pb ed="T" n="2.0319"/>. Yesāhaṃ, bhante, pañhānaṃ ito bahiddhā aññesu samaṇabrāhmaṇesu okāsakammampi nālatthaṃ, te me bhagavatā byākatā. Dīgharattānusayitañca pana <note>dīgharattānupassatā, yañca pana (syā.), dīgharattānusayino, yañca pana (sī. pī.)</note> me vicikicchākathaṃkathāsallaṃ, tañca bhagavatā abbuḷha’’nti.</p> <pb ed="T" n="2.0319"/> <note>dīgharattānupassatā, yañca pana (syā.), dīgharattānusayino, yañca pana (sī. pī.)</note> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Abhijānāsi <pb ed="P" n="2.0284"/> no tvaṃ, devānaminda, ime pañhe aññe samaṇabrāhmaṇe pucchitā’’ti? ‘‘Abhijānāmahaṃ, bhante, ime pañhe aññe samaṇabrāhmaṇe pucchitā’’ti. ‘‘Yathā kathaṃ pana te, devānaminda, byākaṃsu? Sace te agaru bhāsassū’’ti. ‘‘Na kho me, bhante, garu yatthassa bhagavā nisinno bhagavantarūpo vā’’ti. ‘‘Tena hi, devānaminda, bhāsassū’’ti. ‘‘Yesvāhaṃ <note>yesāhaṃ (sī. syā. pī.)</note>, bhante <pb ed="V" n="2.0210"/>, maññāmi samaṇabrāhmaṇā āraññikā pantasenāsanāti, tyāhaṃ upasaṅkamitvā ime pañhe pucchāmi, te mayā puṭṭhā na sampāyanti, asampāyantā mamaṃyeva paṭipucchanti – ‘ko nāmo āyasmā’ti? Tesāhaṃ puṭṭho byākaromi – ‘ahaṃ kho, mārisa, sakko devānamindo’ti. Te mamaṃyeva uttari paṭipucchanti – ‘kiṃ panāyasmā, devānaminda <note>devānamindo (sī. pī.)</note>, kammaṃ katvā imaṃ ṭhānaṃ patto’ti? Tesāhaṃ yathāsutaṃ yathāpariyattaṃ dhammaṃ desemi. Te tāvatakeneva attamanā <pb ed="M" n="2.0227"/> honti – ‘sakko ca no devānamindo diṭṭho, yañca no apucchimhā, tañca no byākāsī’ti. Te aññadatthu mamaṃyeva sāvakā sampajjanti, na cāhaṃ tesaṃ. Ahaṃ kho pana, bhante, bhagavato sāvako sotāpanno avinipātadhammo niyato sambodhiparāyaṇo’’ti <pb ed="T" n="2.0320"/>.</p> <pb ed="P" n="2.0284"/> <note>yesāhaṃ (sī. syā. pī.)</note> <pb ed="V" n="2.0210"/> <note>devānamindo (sī. pī.)</note> <pb ed="M" n="2.0227"/> <pb ed="T" n="2.0320"/> <p rend="subhead">Somanassapaṭilābhakathā</p>
367. Thus, Śakra, the Lord of the Gods, having rejoiced and approved of the Blessed One's speech, said this to the Blessed One:

“Craving (*ejā*), venerable sir (*bhante*), is a disease (*roga*), craving is a sore (*gaṇḍa*), craving is a dart (*sallaṃ*), craving drags this person (*purisaṃ*) around for the sake of the arising of that very existence (*bhavassa abhinibbattiyā*). Therefore, this person undergoes various states (*uccāvacamāpajjati*).

“Whereas other ascetics and *brāhmaṇas* gave me no chance to ask these questions (*pañhānaṃ*), the Blessed One (*bhagavatā*) has explained them to me. The dart (*sallaṃ*) of doubt (*vicikicchā*) and uncertainty (*kathaṃkathā*) that had long lain dormant in me, has been removed by the Blessed One (*bhagavatā abbuḷha’’nti*).”

“Do you remember, Lord of the Gods (*devānaminda*), that you asked these questions of other ascetics and *brāhmaṇas*?”

“I remember, venerable sir (*bhante*), that I asked these questions of other ascetics and *brāhmaṇas*.”

“Then, if you don’t mind, Lord of the Gods (*devānaminda*), please tell me what they said.”

“It is not troublesome for me, venerable sir (*bhante*), when the Blessed One (*bhagavā*) or anyone like him is present.”

“Then tell me, Lord of the Gods (*devānaminda*).”

“Venerable sir (*bhante*), I approach those ascetics and *brāhmaṇas* who, I think, are forest-dwellers (*āraññikā*) and live in remote lodgings (*pantasenāsanāti*) and ask them these questions (*pañhe pucchāmi*). But when asked by me, they are not able to explain (*na sampāyanti*). Being unable to explain, they instead ask me, ‘What is your name, venerable sir (*āyasmā*)?’ When asked by them, I reply, ‘I am Śakra (*sakko*), Lord of the Gods (*devānamindo*)’.

They then ask me further, ‘What good deed (*kammaṃ*) have you done, venerable sir (*āyasmā*), to attain this state (*ṭhānaṃ patto*)?’ Then I teach them the *dharma* as I have heard it and as it is known to me (*yathāsutaṃ yathāpariyattaṃ dhammaṃ desemi*).

They are delighted by just that much, thinking, ‘We have seen Śakra (*sakko*), Lord of the Gods (*devānamindo diṭṭho*), and he has explained (*byākāsī*) what we asked him (*apucchimhā*)’. Instead of me becoming their disciple, they become my disciples (*sāvakā sampajjanti, na cāhaṃ tesaṃ*).

However, venerable sir (*bhante*), I am a disciple (*sāvako*) of the Blessed One (*bhagavato*), one who has entered the stream (*sotāpanno*), is not subject to downfall (*avinipātadhammo*), is assured (*niyato*), and is headed for enlightenment (*sambodhiparāyaṇo*).”
0320 Somanassapaṭilābhakathā
<p n="367" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">367</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Ejā</hi>ti calanaṭṭhena taṇhā vuccati. Sā pīḷanaṭṭhena <hi rend="bold">rogo,</hi> anto padussanaṭṭhena <hi rend="bold">gaṇḍo,</hi> anuppaviṭṭhaṭṭhena <hi rend="bold">sallaṃ. Tasmā ayaṃ puriso</hi>ti yasmā ejā attanā katakammānurūpena purisaṃ tattha tattha abhinibbattatthāya kaḍḍhati, tasmā ayaṃ puriso tesaṃ tesaṃ bhavānaṃ vasena uccāvacaṃ āpajjati. Brahmaloke ucco hoti, devaloke avaco. Devaloke ucco, manussaloke avaco. Manussaloke ucco, apāye avaco. <hi rend="bold">Yesāhaṃ, bhante</hi>ti yesaṃ ahaṃ bhante. Sandhivasena panettha ‘‘yesāha’’nti hoti. <hi rend="bold">Yathāsutaṃ yathāpariyatta</hi>nti yathā mayā suto ceva uggahito ca, evaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Dhammaṃ desemī</hi>ti sattavatapadaṃ dhammaṃ desemi. <hi rend="bold">Na cāhaṃ tesa</hi>nti ahaṃ pana tesaṃ sāvako na <pb ed="M" n="2.0331"/> sampajjāmi. <hi rend="bold">Ahaṃ kho pana, bhante</hi>tiādinā attano sotāpannabhāvaṃ jānāpeti.</p> <p rend="subhead">Somanassapaṭilābhakathāvaṇṇanā</p>
367. "Eja" means *taṇhā* (craving) because of its nature of moving (calanaṭṭhena).

That (*sā*), due to its nature of oppressing (pīḷanaṭṭhena), is *roga* (disease); due to its nature of internally corrupting (anto padussanaṭṭhena), is *gaṇḍa* (boil); due to its nature of deeply penetrating (anuppaviṭṭhaṭṭhena), is *sallaṃ* (dart).

"Therefore, this person (*tasmā ayaṃ puriso*)" means, because *ejā* impels a person (*purisaṃ*) according to deeds done by himself (*attanā katakammānurūpena*) to be born (*abhinibbattatthāya*) in various places (*tattha tattha*), therefore, this person (*tasmā ayaṃ puriso*) undergoes diverse (*uccāvacaṃ*) states (*āpajjati*) according to the influence (*vasena*) of those various existences (*tesaṃ tesaṃ bhavānaṃ*).

In the Brahma world (*brahmaloke*) he is high (*ucco hoti*), in the deva world (*devaloke*) he is low (*avaco*).

In the deva world (*devaloke*) he is high (*ucco*), in the human world (*manussaloke*) he is low (*avaco*).

In the human world (*manussaloke*) he is high (*ucco*), in the *apāya* (realm of suffering) he is low (*avaco*).

"Yesāhaṃ, bhante" means, "Of whom I am, O Bhante."

Here, through the way of combination (*sandhivasena*), it is "yesāhaṃ."

"Yathāsutaṃ yathāpariyatta" means, "Just as it was heard by me (*yathā mayā suto*), and also grasped (*uggahito ca*), thus it is (*evaṃ*)."

"Dhammaṃ desemī" means, "I teach the *dhamma*," the phrase being "sattavatapadaṃ dhammaṃ desemi."

"Na cāhaṃ tesa" means, "I, indeed, am not a disciple (*sāvako na sampajjāmi*) of those."

"Ahaṃ kho pana, bhante" etc., makes known (*jānāpeti*) his own state of being a *sotāpanna* (stream-enterer).

Narration of the Story of Gaining Joy (*somanassapaṭilābhakathāvaṇṇanā*).
368
<p n="368" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">368</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> ‘‘Abhijānāsi no tvaṃ, devānaminda, ito pubbe evarūpaṃ vedapaṭilābhaṃ somanassapaṭilābha’’nti? ‘‘Abhijānāmahaṃ <pb ed="P" n="2.0285"/>, bhante, ito pubbe evarūpaṃ vedapaṭilābhaṃ somanassapaṭilābha’’nti. ‘‘Yathā kathaṃ pana tvaṃ, devānaminda, abhijānāsi ito pubbe evarūpaṃ vedapaṭilābhaṃ somanassapaṭilābha’’nti?</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Bhūtapubbaṃ, bhante, devāsurasaṅgāmo samupabyūḷho <note>samūpabbuḷho (sī. pī.)</note> ahosi. Tasmiṃ kho pana, bhante, saṅgāme devā jiniṃsu, asurā parājayiṃsu <note>parājiṃsu (sī. pī.)</note>. Tassa mayhaṃ, bhante, taṃ saṅgāmaṃ abhivijinitvā vijitasaṅgāmassa etadahosi – ‘yā ceva dāni dibbā ojā yā ca asurā ojā, ubhayametaṃ <note>ubhayamettha (syā.)</note> devā paribhuñjissantī’ti. So kho pana me, bhante, vedapaṭilābho somanassapaṭilābho sadaṇḍāvacaro sasatthāvacaro na nibbidāya na virāgāya na nirodhāya na upasamāya na abhiññāya na sambodhāya na nibbānāya saṃvattati. Yo kho pana me ayaṃ, bhante, bhagavato dhammaṃ sutvā vedapaṭilābho somanassapaṭilābho, so adaṇḍāvacaro asatthāvacaro ekantanibbidāya virāgāya nirodhāya upasamāya abhiññāya sambodhāya nibbānāya saṃvattatī’’ti.</p> <note>samūpabbuḷho (sī. pī.)</note> <note>parājiṃsu (sī. pī.)</note> <note>ubhayamettha (syā.)</note>
368.
"Do you not recall, Indra, Lord of the Gods, having previously experienced such acquisition of feeling ( *vedapaṭilābha* ) and such acquisition of joy ( *somanassapaṭilābha* )?"

"I do recall, venerable sir ( *bhante* ), having previously experienced such acquisition of feeling and such acquisition of joy."

"How then, Indra, Lord of the Gods, do you recall previously experiencing such acquisition of feeling and such acquisition of joy?"

"Formerly, venerable sir, a battle ( *devāsurasaṅgāmo* ) between the gods and the asuras was joined.
In that battle, venerable sir, the gods were victorious ( *devā jiniṃsu* ), and the asuras were defeated ( *asurā parājayiṃsu* ).
Then, venerable sir, having conquered in that battle, the thought occurred to me, the victor in battle: 'Now the gods will enjoy both the divine nourishment ( *dibbā ojā* ) and the nourishment of the asuras ( *asurā ojā* ).'
But that acquisition of feeling and acquisition of joy, venerable sir, which was connected with blows and weapons, did not lead to disenchantment ( *nibbidā* ), dispassion ( *virāga* ), cessation ( *nirodha* ), peace ( *upasama* ), higher knowledge ( *abhiññā* ), enlightenment ( *sambodha* ), or nirvāṇa ( *nibbāna* ).
However, that acquisition of feeling and acquisition of joy that I obtained, venerable sir, by hearing the Dharma ( *dhammaṃ* ) from the Blessed One ( *bhagavato* ), that which is not connected with blows and weapons, does lead to disenchantment, dispassion, cessation, peace, higher knowledge, enlightenment, and nirvāṇa."
<p n="368" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">368</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Vedapaṭilābha</hi>nti <pb ed="V" n="2.0297"/> tuṭṭhipaṭilābhaṃ. <hi rend="bold">Devāsurasaṅgāmo</hi>ti devānañca asurānañca saṅgāmo. <hi rend="bold">Samupabyūḷho</hi>ti samāpanno nalāṭena nalāṭaṃ paharaṇākārappatto viya. Etesaṃ kira kadāci mahāsamuddapiṭṭhe saṅgāmo hoti tattha pana chedanavijjhanādīhi aññamaññaṃ ghāto nāma natthi, dārumeṇḍakayuddhaṃ viya jayaparājayamattameva hoti. Kadāci devā jinanti, kadāci asurā. Tattha yasmiṃ saṅgāme devā puna apaccāgamanāya asure <pb ed="P" n="3.0739"/> jiniṃsu, taṃ sandhāya <hi rend="bold">tasmiṃ kho pana bhante</hi>tiādimāha. <hi rend="bold">Ubhayameta</hi>nti ubhayaṃ etaṃ. Duvidhampi ojaṃ ettha devaloke devāyeva paribhuñjissantīti evamassa āvajjantassa balavapītisomanassaṃ uppajji. <hi rend="bold">Sadaṇḍāvacaro</hi>ti sadaṇḍāvacarako, daṇḍaggahaṇena satthaggahaṇena saddhiṃ ahosi, na nikkhittadaṇḍasatthoti dasseti. <hi rend="bold">Ekantanibbidāyā</hi>ti ekanteneva vaṭṭe nibbindanatthāyāti sabbaṃ mahāgovindasutte vuttameva.</p>
368. *Vedapaṭilābha* means *tuṭṭhipaṭilābhaṃ*.

*Devāsurasaṅgāmo* means *devānañca asurānañca saṅgāmo*.

*Samupabyūḷho* means *samāpanno nalāṭena nalāṭaṃ paharaṇākārappatto viya*.

It is said that sometimes there is a battle on the surface of the great ocean, but there is no mutual killing with cutting, piercing, etc.; there is only a matter of victory and defeat, like a stick-puppet fight.

Sometimes the gods conquer, sometimes the asuras.

Referring to that battle in which the gods conquered the asuras for not returning again, he said, "*tasmiṃ kho pana bhante*," etc.

*Ubhayame**ta*nti means *ubhayaṃ etaṃ*.

Even the two kinds of *ojaṃ* here in the world of gods, they will enjoy it, thus arose strong joy and gladness to him contemplating.

*Sadaṇḍāvacaro* means *sadaṇḍāvacarako, daṇḍaggahaṇena satthaggahaṇena saddhiṃ ahosi, na nikkhittadaṇḍasatthoti dasseti*.

*Ekantanibbidāyā* means *ekanteneva vaṭṭe nibbindanatthāyāti sabbaṃ mahāgovindasutte vuttameva*.
369
<p n="369" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">369</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> ‘‘Kiṃ pana tvaṃ, devānaminda, atthavasaṃ sampassamāno evarūpaṃ vedapaṭilābhaṃ somanassapaṭilābhaṃ pavedesī’’ti? ‘‘Cha kho ahaṃ, bhante, atthavase sampassamāno evarūpaṃ vedapaṭilābhaṃ somanassapaṭilābhaṃ pavedemi.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Idheva <pb ed="T" n="2.0321"/><pb ed="V" n="2.0211"/> tiṭṭhamānassa, devabhūtassa me sato;</p> <pb ed="T" n="2.0321"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0211"/> <p rend="gathalast">Punarāyu ca me laddho, evaṃ jānāhi mārisa.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Imaṃ <pb ed="M" n="2.0228"/> kho ahaṃ, bhante, paṭhamaṃ atthavasaṃ sampassamāno <pb ed="P" n="2.0286"/> evarūpaṃ vedapaṭilābhaṃ somanassapaṭilābhaṃ pavedemi.</p> <pb ed="M" n="2.0228"/> <pb ed="P" n="2.0286"/> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Cutāhaṃ diviyā kāyā, āyuṃ hitvā amānusaṃ;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Amūḷho gabbhamessāmi, yattha me ramatī mano.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Imaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante, dutiyaṃ atthavasaṃ sampassamāno evarūpaṃ vedapaṭilābhaṃ somanassapaṭilābhaṃ pavedemi.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Svāhaṃ amūḷhapaññassa <note>amūḷhapañhassa (?)</note>, viharaṃ sāsane rato;</p> <note>amūḷhapañhassa (?)</note> <p rend="gathalast">Ñāyena viharissāmi, sampajāno paṭissato.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Imaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante, tatiyaṃ atthavasaṃ sampassamāno evarūpaṃ vedapaṭilābhaṃ somanassapaṭilābhaṃ pavedemi.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Ñāyena me carato ca, sambodhi ce bhavissati;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Aññātā viharissāmi, sveva anto bhavissati.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Imaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante, catutthaṃ atthavasaṃ sampassamāno evarūpaṃ vedapaṭilābhaṃ somanassapaṭilābhaṃ pavedemi.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Cutāhaṃ mānusā kāyā, āyuṃ hitvāna mānusaṃ;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Puna devo bhavissāmi, devalokamhi uttamo.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Imaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante, pañcamaṃ atthavasaṃ sampassamāno evarūpaṃ vedapaṭilābhaṃ somanassapaṭilābhaṃ pavedemi.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Te <pb ed="T" n="2.0322"/><pb ed="V" n="2.0212"/><note>ye (?)</note> paṇītatarā devā, akaniṭṭhā yasassino;</p> <pb ed="T" n="2.0322"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0212"/> <note>ye (?)</note> <p rend="gathalast">Antime vattamānamhi, so nivāso bhavissati.</p> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Imaṃ <pb ed="P" n="2.0287"/> kho ahaṃ, bhante, chaṭṭhaṃ atthavasaṃ sampassamāno evarūpaṃ vedapaṭilābhaṃ somanassapaṭilābhaṃ pavedemi.</p> <pb ed="P" n="2.0287"/> <p rend="bodytext">‘‘Ime kho ahaṃ, bhante, cha atthavase sampassamāno evarūpaṃ vedapaṭilābhaṃ somanassapaṭilābhaṃ pavedemi.</p>
"But what is it, lord of gods, that seeing which advantage, you declare such attainment of feeling, such attainment of joy?"

"It is these six advantages, venerable sir, that seeing which, I declare such attainment of feeling, such attainment of joy."

"While staying right here, being a god, mindful,

and I have gained renewed life, know this, sir."

"This, venerable sir, is the first advantage that, seeing which, I declare such attainment of feeling, such attainment of joy."

"Having passed away from the divine body, giving up non-human life,

without delusion I will enter a womb, where my mind delights."

"This, venerable sir, is the second advantage that, seeing which, I declare such attainment of feeling, such attainment of joy."

"I, of undeluded wisdom, delighting in dwelling in the Dispensation,

I will live righteously, fully aware and mindful."

"This, venerable sir, is the third advantage that, seeing which, I declare such attainment of feeling, such attainment of joy."

"And as I live righteously, if enlightenment should come to pass,

I will live unknown, that very one will be the end."

"This, venerable sir, is the fourth advantage that, seeing which, I declare such attainment of feeling, such attainment of joy."

"Having passed away from the human body, giving up human life,

I will be a god again, supreme in the world of gods (devaloka)."

"This, venerable sir, is the fifth advantage that, seeing which, I declare such attainment of feeling, such attainment of joy."

"Those devas (devā), more excellent, Peerless, glorious,

in the final round, that will be the dwelling."

"This, venerable sir, is the sixth advantage that, seeing which, I declare such attainment of feeling, such attainment of joy."

"It is these six advantages, venerable sir, that seeing which, I declare such attainment of feeling, such attainment of joy."
<p n="369" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">369</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Pavedesī</hi>ti kathesi dīpesi. <hi rend="bold">Idhevā</hi>ti imasmiññeva okāse. <hi rend="bold">Devabhūtassa me sato</hi>ti devassa me sato. <hi rend="bold">Punarāyu ca me laddho</hi>ti puna aññena kammavipākena me jīvitaṃ laddhanti, iminā attano cutabhāvaṃ ceva upapannabhāvañca āvikaroti.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Diviyā kāyā</hi>ti dibbā attabhāvā. <hi rend="bold">Āyuṃ hitvā amānusa</hi>nti dibbaṃ āyuṃ jahitvā. <hi rend="bold">Amūḷho gabbhamessāmī</hi>ti niyatagatikattā amūḷho
