hutvā. <hi rend="bold">Yattha me ramatī mano</hi>ti yattha me mano ramissati, tattheva khattiyakulādīsu gabbhaṃ upagacchissāmīti sattakkhattuṃ deve ca mānuse cāti imamatthaṃ dīpeti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Diviyā kāyā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Āyuṃ hitvā amānusa</hi> <hi rend="bold">Amūḷho gabbhamessāmī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Yattha me ramatī mano</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Ñāyena viharissāmī</hi>ti manussesu upapannopi mātaraṃ jīvitā voropanādīnaṃ abhabbattā ñāyena kāraṇena samena viharissāmīti attho.</p> <hi rend="bold">Ñāyena viharissāmī</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Sambodhi</hi><pb ed="M" n="2.0332"/><hi rend="bold">ce bhavissatī</hi>ti idaṃ sakadāgāmimaggaṃ sandhāya vadati, sace sakadāgāmī bhavissāmīti dīpeti. <hi rend="bold">Aññātā viharissāmī</hi>ti aññātā ājānitukāmo hutvā viharissāmi. <hi rend="bold">Sveva anto bhavissatī</hi>ti so eva me manussaloke anto bhavissatīti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Sambodhi</hi> <pb ed="M" n="2.0332"/> <hi rend="bold">ce bhavissatī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Aññātā viharissāmī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Sveva anto bhavissatī</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Puna</hi><pb ed="V" n="2.0298"/><hi rend="bold">devo bhavissāmi, devalokasmiṃ uttamo</hi>ti puna devalokasmiṃ uttamo sakko devānamindo bhavissāmīti vadati.</p> <hi rend="bold">Puna</hi> <pb ed="V" n="2.0298"/> <hi rend="bold">devo bhavissāmi, devalokasmiṃ uttamo</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Antime vattamānamhī</hi>ti antime bhave vattamāne. <hi rend="bold">So nivāso bhavissatī</hi>ti ye te āyunā ca paññāya ca akaniṭṭhā jeṭṭhakā sabbadevehi paṇītatarā devā, avasāne me so nivāso bhavissati <pb ed="P" n="3.0740"/>. Ayaṃ kira tato sakkattabhāvato cuto tasmiṃ attabhāve anāgāmimaggassa paṭiladdhattā uddhaṃsoto akaniṭṭhagāmī hutvā avihādīsu nibbattanto avasāne akaniṭṭhe nibbattissati. Taṃ sandhāya evamāha. Esa kira avihesu kappasahassaṃ vasissati, atappesu dve kappasahassāni, sudassesu cattāri kappasahassāni, sudassīsu aṭṭha, akaniṭṭhesu soḷasāti ekatiṃsa kappasahassāni brahmaāyuṃ anubhavissati. Sakko devarājā anāthapiṇḍiko gahapati visākhā mahāupāsikāti tayopi hi ime ekappamāṇaāyukā eva, vaṭṭābhiratasattā nāma etehi sadisā sukhabhāgino nāma natthi.</p> <hi rend="bold">Antime vattamānamhī</hi> <hi rend="bold">So nivāso bhavissatī</hi> <pb ed="P" n="3.0740"/>
<p n="369" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">369</hi><hi rend="dot">.
</hi><hi rend="bold">"pavedesī"</hi> means he spoke, he indicated.
<hi rend="bold">"idhevā"</hi> means in this very place.
<hi rend="bold">"devabhūtassa me sato"</hi> means of the deity, of my being.
<hi rend="bold">"punarāyu ca me laddho"</hi> means also, renewed life was obtained by me; thereby, he reveals both the fact of his death and the fact of his rebirth.
</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">"diviyā kāyā"</hi> means divine existences.
<hi rend="bold">"āyuṃ hitvā amānusa"</hi> means having abandoned divine life, non-human.
<hi rend="bold">"amūḷho gabbhamessāmī"</hi> means because of determined destiny, having become unconfused.
<hi rend="bold">"yattha me ramatī mano"</hi> means wherever my mind delights, there in a kṣatriya family, etc., I will enter a womb; in seven instances, among gods and humans, he indicates this meaning.
</p> <hi rend="bold">diviyā kāyā</hi> <hi rend="bold">āyuṃ hitvā amānusa</hi> <hi rend="bold">amūḷho gabbhamessāmī</hi> <hi rend="bold">yattha me ramatī mano</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">"ñāyena viharissāmī"</hi> means even having arisen among humans, because of the unsuitability of depriving a mother of life, etc., I will live justly, with reason, equally; this is the meaning.
</p> <hi rend="bold">ñāyena viharissāmī</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">sambodhi</hi><pb ed="m" n="2.
0332"/><hi rend="bold">"ce bhavissatī"</hi> means this refers to the path of the once-returner; if I will be a once-returner, he indicates.
<hi rend="bold">"aññātā viharissāmī"</hi> means unknown, desiring to know, I will live.
<hi rend="bold">"sveva anto bhavissatī"</hi> means that very one will be the end for me in the human world.
</p> <hi rend="bold">sambodhi</hi> <pb ed="m" n="2.
0332"/> <hi rend="bold">ce bhavissatī</hi> <hi rend="bold">aññātā viharissāmī</hi> <hi rend="bold">sveva anto bhavissatī</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">puna</hi><pb ed="v" n="2.
0298"/><hi rend="bold">"devo bhavissāmi, devalokasmiṃ uttamo"</hi> means again, in the world of the gods, the highest, Śakra, the lord of the gods, I will become, he says.
</p> <hi rend="bold">puna</hi> <pb ed="v" n="2.
0298"/> <hi rend="bold">devo bhavissāmi, devalokasmiṃ uttamo</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">"antime vattamānamhī"</hi> means in the last becoming, while continuing.
<hi rend="bold">"so nivāso bhavissatī"</hi> means those who, by life and by wisdom, are the highest and most excellent, more exquisite than all the gods, at the end, that will be my dwelling <pb ed="p" n="3.
0740"/>.
This one, it seems, having died from that state of being Śakra, in that existence, because of having regained the path of non-returning, becoming one who goes upstream, one who goes to Akaniṣṭha, while being reborn in Aviha, etc., in the end, will be reborn in Akaniṣṭha.
Referring to that, he said thus.
This one, it seems, will dwell for a thousand aeons in Aviha, two thousand aeons in Atappa, four thousand aeons in Sudassa, eight in Sudassī, sixteen in Akaniṣṭha; thus, experiencing a Brahmā life of thirty-one thousand aeons.
Śakra, the divine king, Anāthapiṇḍika, the householder, Visākhā, the great female lay follower—these three, indeed, are of the same measure of life, called beings delighted in the round; there is no one similar to these who shares in happiness.
</p> <hi rend="bold">antime vattamānamhī</hi> <hi rend="bold">so nivāso bhavissatī</hi> <pb ed="p" n="3.
0740"/>
370
<p n="370" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">370</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi>‘‘Apariyositasaṅkappo <pb ed="M" n="2.0229"/>, vicikiccho kathaṃkathī.</p> <p rend="gathalast">Vicariṃ dīghamaddhānaṃ, anvesanto tathāgataṃ.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Yassu maññāmi samaṇe, pavivittavihārino;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Sambuddhā iti maññāno, gacchāmi te upāsituṃ.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘‘Kathaṃ ārādhanā hoti, kathaṃ hoti virādhanā’;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Iti puṭṭhā na sampāyanti <note>sambhonti (syā.)</note>, magge paṭipadāsu ca.</p> <note>sambhonti (syā.)</note> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Tyassu yadā maṃ jānanti, sakko devānamāgato;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Tyassu mameva pucchanti, ‘kiṃ katvā pāpuṇī idaṃ’.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Tesaṃ yathāsutaṃ dhammaṃ, desayāmi jane sutaṃ <note>janesuta (ka. sī.)</note>;</p> <note>janesuta (ka. sī.)</note> <p rend="gathalast">Tena attamanā honti, ‘diṭṭho no vāsavoti ca’.</p> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Yadā ca buddhamaddakkhiṃ, vicikicchāvitāraṇaṃ;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Somhi vītabhayo ajja, sambuddhaṃ payirupāsiya <note>payirupāsayiṃ (syā. ka.)</note>.</p> <note>payirupāsayiṃ (syā. ka.)</note> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Taṇhāsallassa hantāraṃ, buddhaṃ appaṭipuggalaṃ;</p> <p rend="gathalast">Ahaṃ <pb ed="T" n="2.0323"/> vande mahāvīraṃ, buddhamādiccabandhunaṃ.</p> <pb ed="T" n="2.0323"/> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Yaṃ <pb ed="P" n="2.0288"/><pb ed="V" n="2.0213"/> karomasi brahmuno, samaṃ devehi mārisa;</p> <pb ed="P" n="2.0288"/> <pb ed="V" n="2.0213"/> <p rend="gathalast">Tadajja tuyhaṃ kassāma <note>dassāma (syā. ka.)</note>, handa sāmaṃ karoma te.</p> <note>dassāma (syā. ka.)</note> <p rend="gatha1">‘‘Tvameva asi <note>tuvamevasi (pī.)</note> sambuddho, tuvaṃ satthā anuttaro;</p> <note>tuvamevasi (pī.)</note> <p rend="gathalast">Sadevakasmiṃ lokasmiṃ, natthi te paṭipuggalo’’ti.</p>
370. Unfulfilled in aims, doubtful, questioning.

Wandering a long road, seeking the *tathāgata*.

"Among those ascetics, I imagine, living in seclusion;

I go to serve those who seem to be fully awakened ones, thinking 'Surely they are enlightened.'"

"'How does reverence occur? How does irreverence occur?'"

When asked thus, they do not satisfy, regarding the path and its practices.

"But when they know me, that I, Śakra, have come among the gods,"

"They ask me, 'Having done what, did you attain this?'"

"To them, I teach the Dharma as I have heard it, comprehensible to people;"

"Thereby they become delighted, saying, 'We have seen Vāsava!'"

"But now I’ve seen the Buddha, the dispeller of doubt;

Today, I am without fear, having attended upon the Fully Awakened One."

"The Buddha, the slayer of the dart of craving, the incomparable one;"

I pay homage to the great hero, the Buddha, kinsman of the sun.

Just as we worship Brahmā, along with the gods, O venerable one;

So likewise today we worship you, come, let us do reverence to you.

"You are the Fully Awakened One, you are the unsurpassed Teacher;"

"In the world with its gods, there is no equal to you."
<p n="370" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">370</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Apariyositasaṅkappo</hi>ti aniṭṭhitamanoratho. <hi rend="bold">Yassu maññāmi samaṇe</hi>ti ye ca samaṇe pavivittavihārinoti maññāmi.</p> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Ārādhanā</hi>ti sampādanā. <hi rend="bold">Virādhanā</hi>ti asampādanā. <hi rend="bold">Na sampāyantī</hi>ti sampādetvā kathetuṃ na sakkonti.</p> <hi rend="bold">Ārādhanā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Virādhanā</hi> <hi rend="bold">Na sampāyantī</hi> <p rend="bodytext"><hi rend="bold">Ādiccabandhuna</hi>nti ādiccopi gotamagotto, bhagavāpi gotamagotto, tasmā evamāha. <hi rend="bold">Yaṃ karomasī</hi>ti yaṃ pubbe brahmuno namakkāraṃ karoma. <hi rend="bold">Samaṃ devehī</hi>ti devehi saddhiṃ, ito paṭṭhāya idāni amhākaṃ brahmuno namakkārakaraṇaṃ natthīti dasseti. <hi rend="bold">Sāmaṃ karomā</hi>ti namakkāraṃ karoma.</p> <hi rend="bold">Ādiccabandhuna</hi> <hi rend="bold">Yaṃ karomasī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Samaṃ devehī</hi> <hi rend="bold">Sāmaṃ karomā</hi>
370. *apariyositasaṅkappo* means unfulfilled wish.
*yassu maññāmi samaṇe* means those recluses who live in solitude, I think.

*ārādhanā* means accomplishment.
*virādhanā* means non-accomplishment.
*na sampāyantī* means they are not able to accomplish and speak.

*ādiccabandhuna* means Ādicca is also of the Gotama clan, the Blessed One is also of the Gotama clan, therefore he said thus.
*yaṃ karomasī* means that which we formerly do as homage to Brahmā, we do.
*samaṃ devehī* means together with the gods, from here on, now there is no making of homage to Brahmā for us, he shows.
*sāmaṃ karomā* means we do homage.

*ādiccabandhuna*, *yaṃ karomasī*, *samaṃ devehī*, *sāmaṃ karomā*
371
<p n="371" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">371</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi> Atha kho sakko devānamindo pañcasikhaṃ gandhabbaputtaṃ āmantesi – ‘‘bahūpakāro kho mesi tvaṃ, tāta pañcasikha, yaṃ tvaṃ bhagavantaṃ paṭhamaṃ pasādesi. Tayā, tāta, paṭhamaṃ pasāditaṃ pacchā mayaṃ taṃ bhagavantaṃ dassanāya upasaṅkamimhā arahantaṃ sammāsambuddhaṃ. Pettike vā ṭhāne ṭhapayissāmi <pb ed="M" n="2.0230"/>, gandhabbarājā bhavissasi, bhaddañca te sūriyavacchasaṃ dammi, sā hi te abhipatthitā’’ti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Atha kho sakko devānamindo pāṇinā pathaviṃ parāmasitvā tikkhattuṃ udānaṃ udānesi – ‘‘namo tassa bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassā’’ti.</p> <p rend="bodytext">Imasmiñca pana veyyākaraṇasmiṃ bhaññamāne sakkassa devānamindassa virajaṃ vītamalaṃ dhammacakkhuṃ udapādi – ‘‘yaṃ kiñci samudayadhammaṃ, sabbaṃ taṃ nirodhadhamma’’nti. Aññesañca asītiyā devatāsahassānaṃ <pb ed="P" n="2.0289"/>, iti ye sakkena devānamindena ajjhiṭṭhapañhā puṭṭhā <pb ed="T" n="2.0324"/>, te bhagavatā byākatā. Tasmā imassa veyyākaraṇassa sakkapañhātveva adhivacananti.</p> <pb ed="P" n="2.0289"/> <pb ed="T" n="2.0324"/> <p rend="centre">Sakkapañhasuttaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ aṭṭhamaṃ.</p>
Then Sakka, ruler of the gods, said to Pañcasikha, son of a *gandharva*: “You have been of great help to me, dear Pañcasikha, in that you were the first to please the Blessed One. Because you were the first to please him, we were later able to approach and see the Blessed One, the *arhat*, the fully enlightened Buddha (*sammāsambuddha*). I will establish you in a fatherly position, you shall be king of the *gandharvas*, and I will give you Bhaddā Suriyavacchasā, whom you desired.”

Then Sakka, ruler of the gods, touched the earth with his hand and exclaimed three times: “Homage to that Blessed One, the *arhat*, the fully enlightened Buddha (*sammāsambuddha*)!”

And while this explanation was being spoken, the dust-free, stainless *dharma*-eye arose in Sakka, ruler of the gods: “Whatever has the nature of arising, all that has the nature of ceasing.” And also in eighty thousand other deities. Thus, the questions that Sakka, ruler of the gods, had asked, were explained by the Blessed One. Therefore, this exposition is called "Sakka's Questions" (*sakkapañha*).

The Sakka's Questions *sūtra*, the eighth, is finished.
<p n="371" rend="bodytext"><hi rend="paranum">371</hi><hi rend="dot">.</hi><hi rend="bold">Parāmasitvā</hi>ti <pb ed="M" n="2.0333"/> tuṭṭhacitto sahāyaṃ hatthena hatthamhi paharanto viya pathaviṃ paharitvā, sakkhibhāvatthāya vā paharitvā ‘‘yathā tvaṃ niccalo, evamahaṃ bhagavatī’’ti. <hi rend="bold">Ajjhiṭṭhapañhā</hi>ti ajjhesitapañhā patthitapañhā. Sesaṃ sabbattha uttānamevāti.</p> <p rend="centre">Iti sumaṅgalavilāsiniyā dīghanikāyaṭṭhakathāyaṃ</p> <p rend="centre">Sakkapañhasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.</p>
"Having touched" (parāmasitvā): with a delighted mind, as if striking a friend's hand with his own hand, or striking the earth to bear witness, [saying,] "Just as you are immovable, so am I devoted."

"Asked question" (ajjhiṭṭhapañhā): a requested question, a desired question.

The rest is clear throughout (sesaṃ sabbattha uttānamevāti).

Thus ends the commentary on the Sakkapañha Sutta in the Sumaṅgalavilāsinī, commentary on the Dīgha Nikāya.
